**Compiled from eleven research runs (V.00 protocol), 21–22 September 2026, and extended through the deep-dive passes of 28–30 September 2026 — the Hawkes–Willis cluster, the Cushman–Delano confirmations, the Washbourne, Townshend, Hannay, DeArmond, Kilmer, Parke, and Atkinson lines (§13A). ## 0. How to Read This Document

**Evidence grades** used throughout:

- **[S]** — Primary or official source: court records, county histories published at the time, newspapers of record, Mayflower Society publications.
- **[PR]** — Peer-reviewed genealogy: *New England Historical and Genealogical Register* (NEHGR), *The American Genealogist* (TAG).
- **[A]** — Published genealogical book or archive-grade transcription (1901 Carpenter genealogy, 1915 Webster genealogy, Hough/Landmarks county histories, published vital records).
- **[B]** — Well-sourced user trees (WikiTree profiles with citations, curated family sites).
- **[FS]** — FamilySearch/MyHeritage user-contributed profiles. Useful as *leads*; every FS fact needs a primary record behind it before it counts as proof.
- **[H]** — Working hypothesis (clearly labeled).

**Three golden rules used in compiling this history:**
1. *Collect, don't adopt.* Family traditions are evidence of what the family believed, not proof of what happened. Each tradition is logged in Section 12 with its verification status.
2. *Names are fossils.* Middle names in America usually preserve a mother's maiden name or a revered ancestor: **Edmund, Moses, Dunton, Atkinson, Webster** in this family each mark a real person located in the record.
3. *One record can close a generation.* The open items in Section 11 are each one document away from resolution.

**One note on voice:** the technical sections of this document speak of "the user" in the third person — the reader whose family this is. *The Story in Plain English* and the short summaries speak to you directly. Both are the same person; the register simply keeps its researcher's distance.


---

## A Short Glossary (for readers new to genealogy)

- **Vital records** — government- or church-kept registers of births, marriages, and deaths. Before the 1850s in America, these were kept by towns and churches, not states — which is why "the Petersham vital records" means one specific published book.
- **Silver Book** — one of the Mayflower Society's compiled volumes, giving every documented descendant of a given Mayflower passenger for the first five generations. If you can document yourself into any name in a Silver Book, the Society will help you complete the proof.
- **Mayflower Compact** — the self-government agreement signed aboard the *Mayflower* in November 1620 by the adult men of the party.
- **Yeoman / husbandman** — a working farmer who owned or rented his land; below a gentleman, above a laborer. Most of your ancestors were yeomen.
- **Proprietor of a newspaper** — the owner and effective editor of a paper. In 1830s America this made John Carpenter a public figure of his county.
- **Associate judge** — a county trial judge; Oswego County's first bench in 1816 had several, Edmund Hawks among them.
- **Monthly meeting (MM)** — the local unit of Quaker church government; its records are among the best colonial sources because Quakers recorded everything.
- **Indenture / apprenticeship record** — a bound labor contract; guild apprenticeship registers (like the Blacksmiths' Company record naming Isaac Allerton) often preserve parentage.
- **Collateral kin / affinal** — relatives by blood but not direct descent ("collateral"), or by marriage ("affinal," sometimes called "kin by marriage"). Several famous connections here are of these kinds, and the document says so every time.
- **Half-cousin** — cousins sharing only one ancestor in the relevant couple (here: two lines of Isaac Allerton's children by different wives).
- **æ** (on old gravestones) — Latin *aetatis*, "in the year of age"; "æ 58" means the person was 58 years old.
- **"Last survivor of the Mayflower"** — Mary Allerton Cushman, Isaac's daughter, outlived every other passenger, dying in 1699.
- **GEDCOM / tree platforms** — FamilySearch and MyHeritage are shared family trees built by thousands of users; treat any single fact there as a lead until a primary record confirms it.
- **"Collect, don't adopt"** — this document's rule for family tradition: write it down faithfully, tag it, and only *believe* it when a record independently confirms it.

**New terms from the deep-dive passes (28–30 September 2026):**

- **Palatine** — a German refugee of the early 1700s, fleeing war and famine in the Rhineland (the Palatinate and neighboring Hesse) for America; thousands came in 1710 alone. Most of this family's German lines — the Kilmers among them — were Palatines.
- **Valiant Sixty** — the first fifty or so Quaker missionaries, sent across England (and beyond) in the 1650s; the founding generation of the Society of Friends. One of them was an Atkinson.
- **Freigericht** — a 'Free Court': a district on the Main–Kinzig border in Germany whose farmers answered only to the Emperor, by a legend going back to Barbarossa. The Kilmer homeland sat inside one.
- **Ragman Roll** — the 1296 parchment on which Scottish landowners swore loyalty to Edward I of England; its names are the first documents of many Scottish families — the Hannays among them.
- **Magna Carta Surety** — one of the twenty-five barons who forced King John to accept Magna Carta at Runnymede in June 1215. Three stand in this family's documented lines.
- **IPM (inquisition post mortem)** — the medieval Crown's estate inquiry after a landholder's death; the documents that preserve who inherited what, and the family's best medieval paper.
- **Bishops' Transcripts** — annual copies of parish registers sent to the bishop; they survive when the register itself has a gap — the route around missing pages.
- **Soundex** — the census index's phonetic filing code (Atkinson, Adkinson, and Atkison file together); the trick for finding a family that spelled its name six ways.
- **Stadtholder** — the chief magistrate of the Dutch provinces, held in the 1400s–1700s by the house of Orange; the office a Lannoy ancestor held.
- **Golden Fleece** — the Burgundian order of knighthood, the most exclusive in medieval Europe; fifteen Lannoys received it.
- **Haplogroup / Y-DNA** — the genetic signature passed father-to-son; when two men's Y-DNA matches, their male lines share a common ancestor even where paper fails.
- **Croix de Guerre** — France's war cross, awarded to allied soldiers for valor; the poet Joyce Kilmer's posthumous decoration.
- **Nova Scotia baronet** — a hereditary title sold by King James I/VI to fund the settling of Nova Scotia; a 17th-century fundraising honor, not a seat in the British peerage.

## 1. Executive Summary

**James Edmund Carpenter II** (b. 1980, New Jersey; raised in Connecticut) descends from two firmly documented colonial families and carries the strong, still-open tradition of a third Mayflower line.

**What is documented or nearly so:**
- **Paternal Carpenter line:** five living-memory generations traced — the user's father (b. 1956), grandfather **William Atkinson Carpenter** (b. ~1927), great-grandfather **James Dunton Carpenter Jr.** (1885–1972) and his wife **Emily Atkinson**, great-great-grandfather **James Dunton Carpenter Sr.** (1854–1945), and **John Carpenter** — proprietor of the *Oswego Palladium* newspaper, 1832–1845 — who married **Mary Hawkes** in Oswego, New York, on 3 January 1848.
- **Hawkes family:** Mary's father, **Judge Edmund Hawks** (b. ~9 Oct 1780, Petersham, Massachusetts — d. 21 Mar 1850, Oswego), first associate judge of Oswego County (1816–1818), and his wife **Polly Eaton** (b. ~1783), are documented; their seven children are named. The user's middle name, **Edmund**, is Judge Edmund's namesake, passed down five generations.
- **Maternal Abbott–Webster line:** grandfather **Richard Webster Abbott** (b. ~1931, living, of Gloversville, Fulton County, NY) descends from **Sylvester Abbott** and **Louise Webster** (m. 1831, Hanover, Oneida County, NY); Louise's line runs **Guy Webster → Elijah Webster & Lois Coe → Deacon Benjamin Webster of Litchfield (1698–1755) → Deacon Jonathan Webster (1657–1735) → Lt. Robert Webster (1619–1676) → Governor John Webster of Connecticut (bapt. 1590, Cossington, England — d. 1661)**. This is the line of the family tradition "direct to Noah Webster" — corrected by the evidence: the family descends from Governor John Webster via his son **Robert's** branch; **Noah Webster descends from the same Robert through a different son. The user and Noah Webster Jr. are distant cousins, not ancestor and descendant.**
- **The Mayflower tradition:** three independent family traditions — Allerton via the Carpenter/Hawkes line, kinship with the Lee family of Virginia, and the specific names **Fear Brewster and Sarah** — all converge on **Isaac Allerton, Mayflower passenger (1620)**. The likely documented route is through the Hawkes family of Lynn, Massachusetts (Mayflower Society line MF17:60), pending one vital record (the Petersham birth entry of Judge Edmund Hawks).

**Famous kin, stated precisely:** President **Zachary Taylor** (blood descendant of the Lee–Allerton marriage and of the Maverick line), President **Franklin D. Roosevelt** (Cushman–Delano line of the same Allerton family), President **John Adams** (Hawkes line), signers **Richard Henry and Francis Lightfoot Lee**, and **General Robert E. Lee** — the whole Stratford Hall dynasty (kin by the marriage of Hancock Lee to Sarah Allerton; §6.2), and Governor **John Webster** (direct ancestor). The **Hannay clan of Sorbie Tower, Scotland**, is a live family tradition with a plausible American bridge (Andrew Hannay, b. Galloway 1733, to Albany County NY 1774) and two candidate entry points in the tree (the Parke and Hawkes lines).

## The Story in Plain English

*(If the sections below ever feel technical, live here. This is the whole document without the citations.)*

Somewhere around 1620, a well-off young Londoner named **Isaac Allerton** — a tailor's son from the Suffolk village of East Bergholt — broke his apprenticeship, fled to Holland, married, and sailed on the *Mayflower*. He outlived the first winter that killed half the company, remarried the daughter of the colony's religious leader, got rich, quarreled with the Puritans, and died in Connecticut in 1659. Two of his daughters married two brothers of a Marblehead shipwright's family, and their children married two children of a Lynn farmer named **John Hawkes** — which is how, if one 1780 birth entry reads the way every clue says it should, **Isaac Allerton became your eighth-great-grandfather.**

His grandchildren's grandchildren drifted west with the country — from Massachusetts farm towns to a new county on Lake Ontario, where in 1816 a young judge named **Edmund Hawks** helped organize Oswego County, New York. His daughter **Mary** married **John Carpenter**, who had come up from New Jersey and owned the local newspaper for fifteen years. Their son was named **James Dunton Carpenter** — Dunton being the family next door that had intermarried the Hawkses — and the names keep walking down the generations like stepping stones: James **Dunton** Carpenter Jr. married **Emily Atkinson**; their son **William Atkinson Carpenter** married a **Parke**; their son was **James Edmund Carpenter** — *Edmund* for the old judge — and his son, born in 1980, is you.

On your mother's side, the stepping stones are different but the pattern is the same. A girl named **Louise Webster** married a New York farmer named Sylvester Abbott in 1831, and because of her, four generations of Abbott men have carried **Webster** as a middle name — your grandfather **Richard Webster Abbott** is the living proof of it. Louise's family walks back through Litchfield, Connecticut, to **Governor John Webster**, who helped found Hartford in 1636 and governed Connecticut in 1656. His family can be traced in English parish registers for five generations before he was born, to about the year 1485, in one Leicestershire village. **Noah Webster** — the dictionary writer — belongs to the *same* governor's family, through a parallel branch: the family's old claim of being "direct to Noah" is really a claim of being direct to the *same ancestor Noah had*. You and the dictionary are cousins.

The family also remembers three ships besides the *Mayflower*: the ***Bevis*** (1638), which carried the first Carpenters; the ***Diligent*** (1638), which carried the Pecks and possibly a Hawke brother; and the ***Gale of Whitehaven*** (1774), which carried **Andrew Hannay** from Galloway, Scotland, to Albany County, New York — a man whose clan, the **Hannays of Sorbie Tower**, your family still names as its own. The place where that Scottish thread might enter the American family is marked too: a woman born on the English–Scottish border in 1668 whose middle name was **Hannah**.

None of this is fantasy, and none of it is finished. Step back and the wider family comes into view. Through Adam Hawkes alone — one Norfolk farmer who followed his neighbors to Massachusetts — the family counts two presidents (Adams father and son) and, astonishingly, **Diana, Princess of Wales**, among its cousins, all through Adam's daughter Susanna and the Cogswells of Ipswich. Through the Cushman and Delano marriages come Franklin Roosevelt and a signer of the Declaration. Through the carpenter's trade and the *Bevis* comes an astronaut. One of the New England Websters became the dictionary; his family's published lists of descendants include Emily Dickinson, Johnny Carson, and a president named Hayes. None of these are borrowed celebrities — each is a cousin of a cousin, reached by marriage and descent through the same few families who kept marrying each other on the same coast of New England for two hundred years.

Every claim above sits in one of three boxes: **proven** (the newspaper, the judge, the governor, the cousin-branch to Noah, the Lee marriage, the Adamses, the princess), **one record away** (Allerton, the *Bevis* link, the Sorbie link), or **tradition, faithfully recorded** (the martyr legends, the oldest English pedigrees). Section 12 lists every claim in its box. Section 11 lists the five specific pages that would move the remaining names into the proven box — one of which, the family Bible, is sitting in your parents' house.

## 4. The Carpenter Immigrant Question — *Bevis* (1638) vs. Providence (1637)

There are **two famous Carpenter immigrant lines** in colonial New England, and the user's family tradition — *"a relative on a boat that came after the Mayflower called the Bevis"* — points to the first:

### 4.1 The Rehoboth line — the *Bevis*, 1638 [S/PR]
The ***Bevis* of Hampton** (150 tons, master Robert Batten) sailed from Southampton, May 1638, with 61 passengers. On the manifest [S — Hotten's Original Lists]:
- **William Carpenter, age 62, carpenter, "of Horwell"** (Wherwell, Hampshire) — b. ~1575/76
- **William Carpenter Jr., age 33, carpenter, of Wherwell** — b. ~1605
- his wife **Abigail (Briant), 32**, and four children under 10 (John, Abigail, William, **Joseph**), plus a 14-year-old apprentice

The younger William is **William Carpenter of Rehoboth (1605–1658)**, progenitor of the Rehoboth line. Research by Eugene Zubrinski (TAG) places the family at **Shalbourne, Wiltshire** — where William Jr. married Abigail Briant on **28 April 1625** — with a documented stop at Wherwell, a village with a strong dissenting tradition (two nonconformist vicars served there), long enough to obtain the Crown-required "certificates of conformity." Fellow passengers included Richard Dummer, Richard Austin (ancestor of Texas founders Moses and Stephen F. Austin), and the Littlefields. [PR/NA]
**⚠ The "1303 ancestor"**: the old *Carpenter Memorial* pedigree tracing both Carpenter lines to "John Carpenter, 1303, of Dilwyn, Hereford" — town clerk of London, MP — is admitted by its own descendants to be **"a fabrication, as the proofs are not forthcoming."** Do not build on it. [NEHGR-based analysis]

**The user's tree above the immigrant (screenshot 4, verdict):** the tree extends six generations above the *Bevis* William: **William 1576–1638** (father) — *manifest-consistent* (aged 62 in 1638 → b. 1575/6), the one solid generation; then **William 1505–1580, John 1485–1540, James 1460–, "Esq." William 1440–1520** — these are the **fabricated Dilwyn pedigree**, imported from unsourced compilations: **rejected**. The documented Carpenter wall stands at William b. ~1575/6 (Wherwell/Shalbourne, Hants–Wilts border; the family at Westcourt Manor, Shalbourne, from 1608; son's marriage to **Abigail Briant** at Shalbourne, 28 Apr 1625). **Flag:** the tree names the elder William's wife "Abigail or Mary (Batt)" — the primary record says **Briant**; "Mary Batt" is an unsourced alternate.

**The full fabricated chain, as screenshotted from the user's tree (logged for the record, REJECTED as lineage):** "John Carpenter" b. 1299 (the legendary "John of Dilwyn, 1303, town clerk of London" — the apex of the *Carpenter Memorial* fabrication) → Richard 1335 m. "Christina" 1331–1366 → John 1356–1442 (an 86-year lifespan — a hallmark of invented lines) → John 1410 → "Esq." William 1440–1520 → William 1505–1580 → [documented wall: William 1576–1638, manifest-consistent]. Tells visible in the tree itself: round pattern dates (1299/1335/1356/1410/1440), an implausible longevity, and MyHeritage *match badges* on the cards — the mechanism by which the fabrication self-replicates (tree copies tree; the icon resembles verification). Nothing above the 1576 William may be cited as ancestry.

### 4.2 The Providence line — 1637 [PR/A]
**William Carpenter of Providence, RI (d. 1681)** — of the 1901 *History and Genealogy of the Carpenter Family… from the Settlement at Providence, R.I., 1637–1901* (full text online). Current best evidence (NEHGR 159, 2005) suggests he was likely of **Amesbury, Wiltshire** — son of Richard Carpenter (m. Alice Knight there, 1603) — and *not* of Wrington, Somerset. He is **not** proven kin to Alexander Carpenter of Wrington (father of Alice Carpenter, wife of Governor William Bradford), whatever the old books claimed.

### 4.3 Which line is yours?
The user's direct Carpenter line runs through **John Carpenter of Woodbury, Gloucester County, NJ**. Rehoboth-line Carpenters are documented across New Jersey — e.g., a SAR biography describes **Henry Carpenter of Ewing, NJ as "believed to be a descendant of William Carpenter Jr. of Rehoboth"** [B] — and the southwest NJ/Philadelphia orbit fits a Woodbury family. **The deciding document:** the full Carpenter entry in *Genealogical and Memorial History of the State of New Jersey* (1910, vol. 3), which names John Carpenter's father. Until that sentence is read, both candidates stand — but the family tradition says *Bevis*, and the *Bevis* tradition matches the Rehoboth line.


### 4.4 The Amawalk Carpenters (Providence-line cluster) and the "Edmund" question [A/FS]
The Providence genealogy's own index places a whole Carpenter family at the user's doorstep: *"**William Carpenter of Amawalk, Westchester County, N.Y., has made a family tree of John(45) and his numerous descendants"* — i.e., the Providence-line Carpenters were the Amawalk/Yorktown Quaker family. The documented sub-lines include **Jacob Carpenter (No. 128, b. 1745, m. Rachel Davenport) and Gilbert Carpenter (No. 132, b. 1754, m. Sarah Davenport), farmers at Yorktown**; **John (No. 370, b. 1771)**; and **William Carpenter (No. 823, b. ~1798 Yorktown, farmer at Cortlandt, m. Winifred S. Carpenter — whose 1884 obituary noted "Funeral at Amawalk")**. The Amawalk Friends (Quaker) records, 1724–1908, are fully transcribed online — dense with Carpenter marriages and certificates of removal.

The collateral **James Edmund Carpenter (1842–1916)** of Amawalk (§3.6) presumably descends from this cluster; if **John Carpenter** (the Oswego newspaperman) was born into or married into the Amawalk family before his Woodbury/Oswego years, the two Carpenter branches join. One intriguing alternative namesake for the modern "Edmund": **Edmond T. Carpenter — Mayor of Jersey City, NJ** (m. Sarah Jane Riker, 1853; d. Jersey City 28 Nov 1861; sons Edmond T. Jr., Daniel H., Henry; daughter Irene) — a New Jersey Carpenter whose given name matches the user's exactly and whose dates fit the naming window. [A]

## 5. The Hawkes Family — and the Allerton Question

### 5.1 Judge Edmund Hawks and Polly Eaton [B/FS — profile; A-grade family mapping]
- **Edmund Hawks** — **BORN OCTOBER 9, 1780 — DIED MARCH 21, 1850** [Bible-primary: the family's own record leaf, photographed September 2026]; the birthplace Petersham (WikiTree, Stu Wilson) attaches to the same date. **The Bible and the Petersham candidate's date are an exact match.**
- **Associate Judge, Oswego County, 1816–1818** — among the county's first appointed officers: "David Easton and **Edmund Hawks, judges**; Daniel Hawks, Jr., assistant justice" [S — county record, 1816].
- Married **POLLY HAWKS — BORN DECEMBER 29, 1782 — DIED AUGUST 18, 1849** [Bible-primary; the FamilySearch "Polly Eaton, b. ~1783" record matches — she died seven months before Edmund, so the 1850-census widow theory is moot; he died two months before the census].
- **Children [Bible-primary — the Births leaf, in the family's hand]:** Olive (b. Oct 24, 1804), James (b. June 26, 1806), **MOSES (b. Feb 10, 1808 — the Rathbone husband; the four-generation Moses name, Bible-confirmed)**, Lydia (b. Oct 11, 1809, d. Sept 5, 1828), John (b. May 24, 1811, d. April 1872), Laura (b. April 30, 1813), Nathaniel (b. March 20, 1815, d. Oct 13, 1830), Edmund Jr. (b. Dec 29, 1818, d. Jan 24, 1912), and **MARY (b. December 1821 — the user's direct ancestress; the tree reads Dec 16, the Bible reads Dec 6 — one digit's difference, flagged).** *Note: the Bible lists nine children — Giles of Volney and Horace (1824) are NOT among them; those hypotheses are collateral families, retired.*
- **Earlier assembled children** [FS/B/A, superseded by the Bible where they differ]:
  - **Moses Hawks** — m. [a Rathbone; the Rathbone genealogy records "married Moses Hawks, son of Judge Edmund Hawks, one of the earliest settlers in the old village of Oswego"]. The name **Moses** is the family's repeating marker (see 5.2).
  - **Giles Hawks** — m. Harriet A. Hollister; settled North Volney as a child (d. 10 May 1883) [A — Volney history; DAR lineage record].
  - **Mary Hawkes** — b. **16 December 1821**; m. **John Carpenter**, 3 Jan 1848 — the user's line.
  - **Allen A. Hawks** — b. ~1822 (Massachusetts per one record; in California by the 1860s).
  - **Horace Hawks** — b. 1824, Oswego; m. **Rebecca** (~1845); their son **William Eugene Hawks b. 10 Jan 1847** (Independence, Allegany Co., NY per WikiTree); the family went west — Horace d. **1886, Los Angeles**; descendants in Arizona.
  - **Laura Hawkes** — b. 30 Apr 1813, Oswego [FS].
  - **Edmund Hawks Jr.** — b. ~24 Dec 1818, Oswego [B — WikiTree index].
- Also in the 1855 household: **Hannah (Griggs) Wilber (1779–1868)** — b. Union, Tolland Co., CT; widow of Benjamin H. Wilber; buried Riverside Cemetery, Oswego. Her exact relationship (wife of a relative; possible late wife of Edmund after Polly) is unproven — logged as a lead.


**The Hannibal Hawks cluster — the Dunton connection, documented.** The 1895 *Landmarks of Oswego County* records of the neighboring town of Hannibal: *"a family named Hawks settled in the town. In 1815 three of their number were married, namely, **Daniel Hawks and Emily Field, William Hawks and Eliza Dunton, and Asa Dunton and Lois Hawks**"* — a double Hawks–Dunton wedding — *"Cyrus Hawks was born here in 1819 and Hiram F. in 1822"*; Daniel Hawks Jr. was town supervisor in 1822. [A] This is the documented web into which the name **Dunton** — carried by three generations of the user's Carpenters — almost certainly reaches back. Whether the Hannibal family and Judge Edmund's family are the same Hawks family (brothers settling adjacent towns) or two related ones is **open**; the Petersham birth entry and the 1850 census answer it.

### 5.2 The decisive hypothesis — Edmund's parentage [H]
The only Hawkes family proven to carry **Isaac Allerton's blood** is that of **John Hawkes of Lynn, Massachusetts** — proven because John married **two Allerton granddaughters**: *"John Hawkes married, first, Rebecca Maverick — daughter of Moses and Remember (Allerton) Maverick — and second, Sarah Cushman — daughter of Elder Thomas and Mary (Allerton) Cushman. The two wives were own cousins, and granddaughters of Isaac Allerton of The Mayflower."* [S — *The Mayflower Descendant*, vol. 17, 1915, "Hawkes Notes," Massachusetts Society of Mayflower Descendants]

The chain from that marriage:
```
Isaac ALLERTON (Mayflower, 1620)
└─ Remember Allerton (1615–1656) m. Moses MAVERICK of Marblehead
   └─ Rebecca Maverick m. (1658) John HAWKES of Lynn (son of Adam Hawkes)
      └─ Moses Hawke (1659–1741), m. Margaret COGSWELL
         └─ Moses Hawkes (1699–1760), m. Susannah HICHINS
            └─ DANIEL HAWKES (b. Lynn 20 Oct 1749 – d. Richfield, Otsego Co., NY, 1830/31),
               m. Rhoda PERHAM (Pepperell, MA, 1771)
               ├─ Moses Hawks — b. 1779, PETERSHAM, Worcester Co., MA
               └─ Daniel Hawks — b. 15 Mar 1780, PETERSHAM (d. 1843, Clay, Onondaga Co., NY;
                  the Hannibal/Clay justice who m. Emily Field, 1815)
```
[PR — Mayflower Society Patriots Project, MF17:60, documenting Daniel Hawkes of Lynn; B — WikiTree Hawkes-762; FS — KZMX-LKJ]

**Judge Edmund Hawks — born Petersham, about 9 October 1780 — is the same age, in the same town, as Daniel's documented sons.** The Petersham town history shows "Daniel Hawks" as a landowner of that era; its heads-of-families list also shows a "Simeon Hawks," so a second Petersham Hawks family remains possible. **If the Petersham vital record reads "Edmund, son of Daniel & Rhoda (Perham) Hawks, b. 9 Oct 1780," the Allerton descent is proven**: the user would be a descendant of Isaac Allerton through the **Maverick** branch, Mayflower Society line **MF17:60** — and, through the same Daniel Hawkes, the Society's other documented Hawkes patriots belong to the extended family: **Adam Hawkes** of Lynn (d. Boston 1778, MF17:61), **Nathan Hawkes** of Lynn (MF17:60), and **Thomas Hawkes** of Lynn (d. 1808, MF17:104 — through the *Cushman* branch). [S — GSMD Patriots Project]

**The Moses name, four generations deep** — Moses Hawke (1659) → Moses Hawkes (1699) → [Daniel (1749)] → Moses (1779, Petersham) → and Judge Edmund naming his son **Moses** — is the naming-pattern evidence; it is not proof, but it is exactly the kind of evidence that survives when paper thins.

**The rival branch (logged, not adopted):** a **Deerfield/Charlemont, MA** Hawks family — descendants of **John Hawks of Windsor/Hadley (b. ~1613, d. 1662)**, the *other* Hawkes immigrant, whose name appears in Windsor records as **H-a-k-e-s** — spread through Deerfield, Hadley, Charlemont, Gill, and Petersham's neighbors. WikiTree carries an *Edmund Hawks (1787–1832)* of Savoy, MA (son of Ichabod Hawks) who is **a different man** (the judge died 1850). If Edmund's father were of this Windsor/Hadley family, there is **no** Allerton descent. The Windsor immigrant's origins are officially unknown; some researchers (Lane; family compilations) make him a brother of Adam Hawkes of Lynn — both allegedly sons of John Hawke of Hingham, Norfolk (where sons Stephen, bap. 1602, and Adam, bap. 1605, were baptized) — but **the Adam Hawkes Family Association itself states: "No proof has been discovered to date to support that claim."** [B/A — AHFA statement]

### 5.3 Isaac Allerton — the immigrant [PR/S]
- Born about **1586–87, probably East Bergholt, Suffolk** — the breakthrough identification (Caleb Johnson, Sue Allan & Simon Neal, *"The English Origin and Parentage of Mayflower Passenger Isaac Allerton,"* **NEHGR 173 (2019): 197–205**): son of **Bartholomew Allerton, tailor** (of Ipswich/East Bergholt) **and Mary**. The parish registers of East Bergholt do not survive before 1653, and the family were religious dissentents — which is why the trail was cold for four centuries.
- A 1609 Blacksmiths' Company apprenticeship record ("Isack Allerton son of Bartholomew Allerton of Ipswich in County Suffolk, tailor," bound to James Glyn) shows him breaking his indenture and surfacing in **Leiden**, where he married **Mary Norris, 4 November 1611**.
- **Mayflower, 1620**; signer of the Compact; assistant governor; the wealthiest Pilgrim; seven Atlantic crossings. First wife Mary died February 1620/21; married **Fear Brewster** (daughter of Elder William Brewster) c. 1626 — she died by December 1634; third wife Joanna Swinnerton.
- Children: **Bartholomew** (returned to England; d. Bramfield, Suffolk, 1658/59 — his will survives, transcribed by Leslie Mahler); **Remember** (m. **Moses Maverick**); **Mary** (m. **Thomas Cushman** — d. 1699, the last surviving Mayflower passenger); **Sarah**; and by Fear, **Isaac Allerton Jr.**
- Business quarrels drove him from Plymouth; he built a trading empire from New Amsterdam (house on Broadway, wharf, warehouse) and **died at New Haven, Connecticut, February 1658/59 — the only member of the Mayflower company buried in Connecticut** (marker on the New Haven Green, 1941). [S — Mayflower Society profile; Winthrop journal episodes]
- Sister: **Sarah Allerton**, who married successively John Vincent, **Degory Priest** (Mayflower passenger), and Godbert Godbertson, and came on the *Anne* (1623) bringing Isaac's daughter Sarah. A probable brother: **John Allerton**, Mayflower passenger (d. 1621). A possible kinsman (unproven): **Ralph Allerton of Great Bentley, Essex, burned at the stake 1555** — a Marian martyr; the family tradition of a Reformation martyr attaches to the name, on both sides of the Atlantic, in *both* the Allerton and Hawke families. [PR/B; lore flagged]

### 5.4 The Mavericks [PR — NEHGR 69 (1915): 146–159 (Elizabeth French); NEHGR 115 (1961): 248–253 (Hunt); NEHGR 122 (1968): 282–283]
Three generations of Devon clergy, documented in the parish and diocesan records:
- **Robert Maverick, clerk, of Awliscombe** (d. 1573; his predecessor at the Awliscombe church was installed 1554).
- His son **Rev. Peter Maverick, *alias Bull***, ordained 1573/4, vicar of Awliscombe from 1580, m. 1577 **Dorothy Tucke** (daughter of tenants of the Mayor of Exeter); died a violent death c. 1616. The alias has never been explained — the best surmise is that Peter was a step-son who took his step-father's name.
- His son **Rev. John Maverick** (bapt. Awliscombe 28 Dec 1578 – d. Dorchester 3 Feb 1635/6): Exeter College, Oxford (B.A. 1599, M.A. 1603); ordained 1597; rector of Beaworthy 1615–1629; **sailed on the *Mary & John*, March 1630**, and co-founded the church at Dorchester with John Warham. Winthrop's eulogy: *"a man of very humble spirit, and faithful in furthering the work of the Lord, both in church and civil state."*
- **Moses Maverick** (bapt. **South Huish, 3 Nov 1611** – d. 1686), founder of Marblehead: m. (1) by 6 May 1635 **Remember Allerton** (daughter of Isaac Allerton); m. (2) 1656 Eunice (___) Roberts. Their daughter **Rebecca** married **John Hawkes of Lynn** (1658); after her early death John married her cousin **Sarah Cushman**; Moses's 1686 will names **"Moses Hawks, the only surviving child of my daughter Rebecca."** Moses's siblings: Samuel (the famous colonist, emigrated first), Elias, Aaron, Mary (m. Rev. James Parker), Abigail (m. John Manning). The Cushmans are themselves Mayflower gentry: Sarah's father-in-law **Robert Cushman** was the Mayflower party's business agent in London and crossed on the *Fortune* (1621); her husband Thomas had arrived in 1623 and lived to bury the last of the passengers.
- **The guardian-uncle:** John's uncle **Rev. Radford Maverick** (b. 1560; vicar of Ilsington) **raised the orphaned Mary Gye as his ward** — her grandfather Robert Gye paid him "great sums" for her upbringing — and performed her marriage to his own nephew John in 1600. Hunt's surmise (unproven, flagged): the elder Robert Maverick's unnamed wife may have been a **Prouse**, sister of Mary Prowse — which would make the Mavericks and Gyes cousins and explain the wardship, the name "Radford" (after Mary's great-uncle, the murdered judge), and the phrase "cousin-german" in the records.

### 5.5 Adam Hawkes of Lynn — the English head of the line [PR — Great Migration 3:253–257; NEHGR 178 (2024)]
- Baptized **26 January 1604/5 at Hingham, Norfolk**; first documented in New England at Charlestown, 1634/5 (the Winthrop Fleet 1630 tradition is **not supported by evidence** per the Great Migration project); settled Lynn (Saugus); d. 13 March 1671/2. His first wife was **Anne, widow of the unidentified Mr. Hutchinson** (the first husband's identity established by Robert Battle, *NEHGR* 178 (Fall 2024): 307–318); by her: son **John Hawkes** (1633–1694, the Allerton son-in-law) and daughter **Susanna Hawkes** (b. ~1633). Estate: £817 11s (£672 real estate) — a prosperous yeoman. He spent decades in litigation with the Saugus Iron Works over flooding of his lands (his son John finally arranged the dam's destruction in May 1682).
- His probate distributes the family farm with explicit reference to **"Moses Hauks, son of John Hauks by Rebeckah Hauks, daughter of Mr. Moses Mavericke"** — the Allerton connection in the founding generation's own hand.
- **⚠ Parentage corrections (PGM):** Adam was **not** the son of Mary Cowper (her Hingham marriage to John Hawkes occurred in 1612/3, years after Adam's birth), and there is **no known association** between Adam and John Hawks of Hadley or Matthew Hawkes of the *Diligent* — the "brothers" tradition is disowned by the Great Migration project.
- **Notable descendants of Adam & Anne through their daughter Susanna Hawkes & William Cogswell of Ipswich:** **President John Adams** and **President John Quincy Adams** (per Gary Boyd Roberts, *Ancestors of American Presidents*, 2009), and — through the same Cogswell line — **Diana, Princess of Wales** (per Richard K. Evans, *The Ancestry of Diana, Princess of Wales*, NEHGS 2007, p. 330). The user descends through Adam's *son* John — so the presidents and the princess are the user's **cousins through Adam Hawkes** (they via Susanna, the user via John). *Lead flagged:* Moses Hawke (the user's line) married **Margaret Cogswell** — possibly of the same Ipswich Cogswell family; unproven.

## 6. The Lee Connection — "We are related to Francis Lightfoot Lee"

**The documented marriage:** **Hancock Lee (1653–1709)**, fifth son of **Richard Lee I "the Emigrant"** of Virginia, married as his second wife **Sarah Allerton (c. 1670–1731)** — daughter of **Isaac Allerton Jr.** of Westmoreland County and **granddaughter of Mayflower passenger Isaac Allerton** (and, through Isaac Jr.'s mother Fear Brewster, of Elder William Brewster). Isaac Jr.'s **own will (1702) names "daughter Sarah Lee."** [S — will; Lee Family Archive: "The fifth son of the family founder, Hancock, married (the second time) Sarah Allerton, a granddaughter of Isaac Allerton, who was a passenger on the Mayflower"]

**The relationships, precisely:**
- **Sarah Allerton Lee** descends from Isaac Allerton via his **second** wife Fear Brewster. The user's hypothesized line descends via his **first** wife Mary Norris (through Remember → Maverick → Hawkes). Shared ancestor: **Isaac Allerton only** — half-cousins, several generations removed.
- **Francis Lightfoot Lee (1734–1797)**, signer of the Declaration, was the **great-nephew by marriage** of Sarah: his line runs Richard Lee I → **Richard Lee II** (the scholar, m. Laetitia Corbin) → **Thomas Lee** (president of the King's Council, m. **Hannah Harrison Ludwell**) → Francis Lightfoot. He carried no Allerton blood, and **he died childless** — so "related to Francis Lightfoot Lee" can only ever mean collateral kin, exactly as the tradition states it. [S/B]
- **The blood descendant of the Lee–Allerton marriage is President Zachary Taylor** — great-grandson of Hancock and Sarah — who thereby descends from Isaac Allerton through this Lee line *and* (per the Mayflower Society) through the Maverick line as well. The same marriage later connects to Jefferson Davis through Taylor's daughter. [B — Lee Family FAQ; S — GSMD]
- **The Lee pedigree in England** is among the deepest of any American family: from **Reyner de Lega, c. 1200**, through the Lees of **Coton / Nordley Regis, Shropshire** (Richard Lee, b. 1563, father of the emigrant), per the surviving Cobbs Hall family Bible and the College of Arms, as analyzed by W. L. Montague (*Virginia Magazine of History and Biography*, 1954). If the user's Allerton line proves, this 800-year pedigree enters the family's *collateral* heritage chart.

### 6.1 The children of Hancock Lee and Sarah Allerton [S — James Reid Hancock, "Ann (Lee) Eustace, Daughter of Hancock Lee… and His Second Wife Sarah Allerton," *The Mayflower Descendant* 59 (2010): 13; Merrick, *MD* 42 (1992)]
The modern standard account, which carefully separates Sarah's children from those of Hancock's first wife Mary Kendall:
1. **Anne Lee** (b. ~1700–1702) — m. **William Eustace**. *Not* to be confused with her half-sister **Anna** Lee (daughter of the first marriage), who m. William Armistead — two different daughters, untangled by the Society's researchers.
2. **Isaac Lee** (b. by Jan 1706/7 – d. by Nov 1727) — died young, unmarried ("of Stepney, Middlesex" in one record).
3. **John Lee** (b. by 18 May 1709 – d. 11 Aug 1789) — lived to old age, probably never married.
4. **Elizabeth Lee** (b. 1709 — the **twin sister of Hancock II**) — m. (1) **Swan Jones** (one son, John Jones, b. ~1733); (2), in 1737, **Zachary Taylor** of Orange County, VA. Four Taylor children: Zachary Jr. (b. 1735); Elizabeth (m. Thomas Bell); Hancock Taylor (~1742); and **Richard Taylor (1744–1829)** — Revolutionary colonel, who carried the family to Kentucky and was the **father of President Zachary Taylor**. Elizabeth Lee is thus the president's grandmother, and Sarah Allerton Lee his great-grandmother.
5. **Hancock Lee II** (b. 1709 – d. 1762 per the Society research; some compilations give 1789) — m. **Mary Willis**; of Ditchley and Warrenton; justice of King George County; the son who carried this Lee branch forward.

The couple's tombstones survive at **Ditchley, Northumberland County, VA**, and Sarah's stone states the Mayflower connection outright: *"Sarah, his last Wife, Daughter of Isaac Allerton, Esq., Who departed this Life 17th May, Anno Domo, 1731."* (Hancock's stone styles him the seventh son of Richard Lee the Emigrant.)

*Research footnote — the records stay live:* a 2024 *Mayflower Quarterly* article (Hahn) disproved the old assumption that Sarah had also married a man named Newton; the "Newton" confusion attached to her niece **Mary Allerton Newton** (daughter of Isaac Jr.'s daughter Mary). Sarah's only husband was Hancock Lee.

### 6.2 Robert E. Lee and the Allerton kinship [S/B]
**Robert Edward Lee (1807–1870)** descends: Henry Lee III "Light-Horse Harry" (1756–1818) → Henry Lee II of Leesylvania (1730–1787) → Henry Lee I of Lee Hall (1691–1747) → **Richard Lee II "the Scholar" (1647–1715)** → Richard Lee I the Emigrant. Richard Lee II was the **brother of Hancock Lee** — Sarah Allerton's husband — making Sarah Robert E. Lee's great-great-great-aunt **by marriage**. Like Francis Lightfoot Lee, Robert E. Lee carried **no Allerton blood**; the kinship runs through the marriage, not the bloodstream. The blood relationship inside that family belonged to the user's cousin: **Zachary Taylor was Robert E. Lee's third cousin once removed** (through Richard Lee I's line), and the two served together in the Mexican–American War — Lee on Taylor's staff in northern Mexico. *(Flagged, unproven, logged: Robert E. Lee's wife, Mary Anna Randolph Custis, descends from the Virginia Parke–Custis line — Frances Parke, daughter of Governor Daniel Parke, married John Custis IV of Williamsburg; that Parke family is a different immigrant line from the user's Hopewell Parkes, §3.2.)*

**Why the tradition is trustworthy:** the family's three Allerton traditions (via Carpenter/Hawkes; kin to the Lees; the names Fear Brewster and Sarah) are independent memories that all converge on the same ancestor — the signature of genuine transmission rather than borrowed glamour.

## 7. The Maternal Line — Abbott to Governor John Webster

### 7.1 Richard Webster Abbott (b. ~1931, living) [FS/B]
Age 94 as of 2026. MyHeritage-indexed trees place the family at **Gloversville, Fulton County, NY**, with wife **Barbara** (the user's Kilmer grandmother) and a son Richard Webster Abbott Jr. (b. ~1960). The four-generation middle-name pattern (**Webster** as a middle name) is the family's own signature of the Webster descent.

### 7.2 William Webster Abbott (27 Apr 1844 – 31 Aug 1924) [FS/B]
Born **Hanover, Oneida County, NY** (FamilySearch LHZX-H94), son of **Sylvester Abbot and C. Louisa Webster** [A-FS — the mother's name now carries an initial the file's research did not have: see the flag below]; in the 1850 census (age 6) at **Concord, Erie County, NY**; died 31 August 1924. **NEW (29 September dive) [A-FS]: he married ELLA V. BAKER on 1 January 1880, at Hanover — the village of Hanover in the town of Marshall, Oneida County** (the 'Hanover, Marshall, Oneida' of the FS record resolving to the same place). The documented daughter **Ora Ruby (Abbott) Smith (1883–1952)** is presumably Ella Baker's; the **son** — the missing link between William and the user's grandfather — would be a child of William & Ella, born in the 1880s–1900s. **Flag:** FS names William's mother *'C. Louisa Webster'*; §7.3's research gives *Louise Webster (1813–1905), daughter of Guy Webster & Lucretia Mason* — presumed the same woman (the C initial unexplained; William's death certificate or the FS profile full read will settle it).

### 7.3 Sylvester Abbott and Louise Webster (m. 1 Oct 1831) [B]
**Louise Webster** — b. 1813, Hanover, Oneida Co., NY; d. 1905; "her father settled first in Hanover, N.Y." (WikiTree). Daughter of **Guy Webster (1787–1853)** and **Lucretia Mason (1790–1874)**. ⚠ Flag (29 September): the FS profile of her son William names her *'C. Louisa Webster'* — same woman presumed; the C initial is new and unexplained. This is the female-line Webster descent — the middle name "Webster" in the Abbott family exists because of *her*.

### 7.4 Guy Webster — War of 1812 [B]
b. ~1787 (Litchfield, CT per family records) – d. 1853. Served as **Ensign, Captain Tubb's Company, New York Militia, War of 1812**; early settler (with his family) of the Buffalo corridor — Concord, Erie County, NY. Married Lucretia Mason, 1 Oct 1831 context (the Abbott marriage of his daughter was the same day? no — Louise married Sylvester Abbott 1 Oct 1831; Guy & Lucretia's marriage was earlier). The 1915 Webster genealogy states plainly: **"Guy Webster, son of Elijah and Lois (Coe) Webster."**

### 7.5 Elijah Webster & Lois (Coe) — and the two Elijahs [B/A — caution]
The western-New-York Webster family of **Elijah Webster & Lois Coe** produced sons **Miles (m. Sally Morehouse), Edward, Horace, Uriah, Guy, Joseph**, and daughter Clara (family compilations), and **moved along the Seneca Falls → Oakfield → Albion axis** (Cleveland genealogy; John Pratt of Hartford genealogy). ⚠ **Two Elijah Websters exist in these records**: the elder (m. Lois Coe) — Guy's father — and a younger **Elijah Webster, b. 31 May 1805 at Litchfield, "son of Benjamin," m. Susan Cleveland Pratt** (1831, Seneca Falls). The younger may be the elder's son or nephew; the 1915 book's full text will settle the exact link. Either way, the family is **Litchfield Websters** — and Litchfield's West Cemetery stones preserve the founding couple of that line.

### 7.6 Deacon Benjamin Webster (9 Aug 1698 – 10 Jul 1755), Litchfield [A — 1915 genealogy; stones]
Son of **Deacon Jonathan Webster and Dorcas Hopkins**; married **Elizabeth Peck** (buried beside him; her stone: d. 1 Jul 1775, æ69). Deacon Benjamin was among Litchfield's founders; his will and the burying-ground inscriptions survive, including stones for sons **Elijah (d. 17 Aug 1784, æ22 — this is the *other* Elijah's namesake generation; the stone cannot be Guy's father, who lived to raise a family)** and James (d. 1754). FamilySearch carries the next generation: **Benjamin Webster Jr. (1769–1842)** with siblings **Elijah, Avis, Lucretia, Claudius** — the surviving Elijah of this branch being the best candidate for Guy's father. **Status: branch firmly placed; one father-son link still to pin from the 1915 book's full text.**

### 7.7 Deacon Jonathan Webster (b. 9 Jan 1656/7 – d. 1735) [A]
Son of **Robert Webster and Susannah Treat**; married **Dorcas Hopkins**, 11 May 1681. Children born in Hartford include **Jonathan Jr., Susannah (m. Thomas STEELE of Farmington — the Steele braid), Mary (m. John Brace), Mehitable (m. David Bidwell), Stephen, and Benjamin**. Note the double Webster–Steele intermarriage: the Farmington Steeles (Mercy Bradford's family) and the Webster family interlock twice.

### 7.8 Lieutenant Robert Webster (bapt. 17 Nov 1619, Cossington – d. 1676, Hartford) [A]
Son of Governor John; the **sole executor of his father's will**; married **Susanna Treat**, daughter of **Richard Treat** (founder of Wethersfield, one of Hartford's richest men). Remained in Hartford. His descendants were, per Hinman's 1846 *Puritan Settlers of Connecticut*, "the branch of Gov. Webster's family through whom Hon. Noah Webster traced his ancestry" — and through Jonathan, this document adds, the Abbott family does too.

### 7.9 Governor John Webster (bapt. 16 Aug 1590, Cossington, Leicestershire – d. 5 Apr 1661, Hadley, MA) [PR/A]
- Son of **Matthew Webster (b. ~1564 – bur. 13 Sep 1592), husbandman of Cossington**, and **Elizabeth Ashton** (m. 17 Apr 1587); Matthew's 1592 will — witnessed by his father-in-law and the parson — names his young son **John**, daughters Faith and Annis, brother John, and brother-in-law James Aston. John was orphaned at ~18 months.
- Married **Agnes Smith** (7 Nov 1609), daughter of **Richard Smyth of Cossington**, yeoman; the 1609 marriage settlement survives in the UK National Archives (her ancestry treated in Skillington's TAG 29, 1953).
- Emigrated by 1634 (he disposed of ~£1,000 of property — "three houses, a cottage, and about 100 acres"), Watertown → **founder of Hartford, 1636** (his name on the Founders Monument); Deputy Governor of Connecticut; **Governor, 1656**; led the dissenting "Hadley Company" to Massachusetts, 1659; died there 1661. The monument to him in Hadley was erected in **1818 by "his descendant, Noah Webster."**
- Children (baptized at Cossington): Matthew (1609/10), Margaret (1611), William (1614), Thomas (1616), **Robert (1619)**, Anne (1621), Elizabeth (1624), Faith (1627, d. infancy), Mary (1623, d. infancy).

### 7.10 The Cossington Websters — five generations in one village [PR]
Florence Skillington's *"The Ancestry of Governor John Webster"* (**The American Genealogist 24 (1948): 197–214**) traced, from parish registers (from 1558), seventy years of churchwardens' accounts, Leicester freemen records, and probate:
1. **John Webster I** (b. ~1485, d. before 20 Mar 1558/9), Cossington
2. **John Webster II** (d. 24 Jun 1575) — churchwarden; brother William a freeman of Leicester
3. **John Webster III** — prosperous yeoman; settled a third of his farm on his son at the 1587 marriage; d. 1594
4. **Matthew Webster** (d. 1592)
5. **Governor John** (1590)
**Wall: ~1485.** The old pedigrees pushing to "Webster of Lockington" and a Scottish origin are unproven tradition.

### 7.11 The Noah correction — stated once, plainly
Noah Webster (1758–1843) descends: Gov. John → **Robert → John (b. 1653, m. Sarah Mygatt) → Deacon/Captain Daniel Webster (m. Miriam Cooke, widow Kellogg) → Noah Webster Sr. (1722–1813, farmer, deacon, militia captain, justice of the peace; m. Mercy Steele 1749) → Noah Jr.**
The Abbott line descends: Gov. John → **Robert → Jonathan → Benjamin → … → Guy → Louise → Abbott.**
Both lines share **Robert**; they split at Robert's sons. **The user and Noah Webster Jr. are both 7th-generation descendants of Governor John Webster — distant cousins.** The family's tradition "direct to Noah" is thus a compression of a true statement: *direct to Governor John Webster — the same ancestor Noah had.* The middle-name tradition ("Webster" across four Abbott generations) is how a *cousin-branch* keeps the famous name alive.
*(For completeness: Noah Jr. had a brother **Abraham Kellogg Webster** whose son Frederick settled at Whitestown, Oneida Co., NY — the same county as the Abbotts; and Noah's son **Richard Webster** of Mauch Chunk, PA, appears in an 1848 letter. The historian **Paul Leicester Ford** was Noah's great-grandson through Noah's daughter Emily.)*

### 7.12 Collateral bricks in the Webster wall
- **Peck**: Deacon Benjamin's wife Elizabeth Peck descends from **Joseph Peck of Hingham** — who arrived on the **DILIGENT of Ipswich, 1638** (not the *Bevis* — an earlier hypothesis in this research, corrected).
- **Treat**: Richard Treat of Wethersfield descended from the Treats of **Wetherden, Suffolk**.
- **Steele braid**: Jonathan Webster's daughter Susannah married Thomas Steele of Farmington — the same Steele clan that married Mercy Bradford (below, §8.3).

## 8. Affinal and Collateral Lines (documented, but not the user's direct descent)

### 8.1 Bradford–Steele (Noah Webster's mother — the family the tradition may actually remember)
Governor **William Bradford** of Plymouth (bapt. Austerfield, Yorkshire, 19 Mar 1589/90 – d. 1657; son of William Bradford & Alice Hanson; Mayflower; governor 30+ years; author of *Of Plymouth Plantation*) married (2nd) **Alice (Carpenter) Southworth** — born probably at **Wrington, Somerset**, c. 1595, daughter of **Alexander Carpenter** (her sisters Juliana m. George Morton; Agnes m. Samuel Fuller; Priscilla m. William Wright — the Wrington Carpenter women all married into the Pilgrim circle). Their son **Major William Bradford (1624–1703)** m. Alice Richards; their daughter **Mercy Bradford (bapt. Boston, 2 Sep 1660)** m. **Sergeant Samuel Steele** of Farmington (b. 15 Mar 1651/2; son of John Steele & Mary Warner) in Hartford, 16 Sep 1680; their son **Eliphalet Steele (b. 23 Jun 1700)** m. Catherine Marshfield; their daughter **Mercy Steele (b. 1727, West Hartford)** married **Noah Webster Sr.**, 12 Jan 1749. **This is a fully documented Mayflower line (Society silver book MF22) — but it enters Noah Webster's family, not the user's.** It becomes the user's only if an as-yet-unfound Steele marriage exists in the Abbott/Webster collateral lines — one reason the "direct to Noah" story may have started.

### 8.2 The Steeles in England
The Steele line is traceable to **Fairstead, Essex**: **John Steele (b. 12 Dec 1591, Fairstead – d. 1653/4, Farmington/Fairfield)** m. **Rachel Talcott**; his father **Richard Steele (bap. 21 Apr 1549, Fairstead)**; the family reaches toward 1480 in the same parish [B — compiled pedigrees].

### 8.3 The kin-web (expanded, with relationship math and honest tags)

**A. Presidents — blood kin *if* the Allerton line proves [S — GSMD/Mayflower Society-documented lines]:**
- **Zachary Taylor (12th President)** — descends from Isaac Allerton **twice**: via Sarah Allerton → Elizabeth Lee (§6.1) *and* via the Virginia Allerton line; and from **William Brewster** via Fear (his 4th great-grandfather). He is the single blood descendant of the Lee–Allerton marriage, and Isaac Allerton's great-grandson through Sarah.
- **Franklin D. Roosevelt (32nd President)** — descends from Isaac Allerton via **Mary Allerton → Thomas Cushman → … → Elizabeth Cushman → Warren Delano → Sara Delano** (Allerton is his 6th great-grandfather per compiled kinship charts). FDR's *other* Mayflower lines (Richard Warren ×2, Degory Priest, Francis Cooke ×2, John Howland) are his own — the shared ancestor with the user is **Allerton only** (user via Remember/Maverick, FDR via Mary/Cushman — the sisters).
- *Not* your **Mayflower** kin (different passengers entirely): Grant (Richard Warren), Garfield (Billington), Coolidge (Doty), the Bushes' Mayflower lines (Howland/Cooke/Tilley/Samson). **John Adams** requires the careful phrasing: his *Mayflower* line is Alden–Mullins — but he **is** your confirmed Hawkes cousin through Susanna Hawkes & William Cogswell (§5.5, §8.3-G).

**B. Signers and the Founding:**
- **Richard Henry Lee & Francis Lightfoot Lee** — kin by marriage (§6). **Robert Treat Paine** (Signer) — **blood cousin through Richard Treat of Wethersfield**: Paine's mother Eunice Treat was daughter of Rev. Samuel Treat, whose father was **Governor Robert Treat** of Connecticut — the *brother of the user's Susanna Treat* (Robert Webster's wife). [S — Mass. Historical Society; CT State Library]
- The standard published lists of Richard Treat's other notable descendants include **both Presidents Bush, Thomas Edison, J.P. Morgan, Henry Ford II, Tennessee Williams, Treat Williams, Samuel Colt, Gideon Welles (Lincoln's Navy Secretary), W. Edwards Deming, and Stephen Crane** [B — published lists; individually unverified].

**C. Civil War:**
- **Robert E. Lee** — kin by marriage (§6.2); his blood cousin and wartime commander was the user's blood cousin Taylor (third cousins once removed, via Richard Lee).
- George B. McClellan — in the Brewster constellation (by-marriage).

**D. The word "maverick":**
- **Samuel Augustus Maverick (1803–1870)** — Texas Declaration of Independence signer, San Antonio mayor, and the man whose unbranded cattle gave the English language the word "maverick" (first cited 1867) — belongs to the Maverick clan of Awliscombe, Devon (Rev. John Maverick & Mary Gye's family). The exact link of the Texas branch is *unproven* (Greenlaw listed five candidates, *Register* 96/102) — logged, not adopted. If the user's line proves, the word descends from the user's extended family.

**E. Royal and Magna Carta descent — current honest status [PR]:**
- **Mary Gye**, wife of Rev. John Maverick (the user's 9th great-grandmother *if* the line proves): daughter of **Robert Gye & Grace Dowrish**; granddaughter of **John Gye & Mary Prowse** (the 1538–40 inquisition post mortem of "John Gey of Devonshire," manors of Poughill and Doddridge, survives at The National Archives).
- The often-cited line to **Henry III** (via Hunt's 1961 article, *NEHGR* 115:248–253 — Henry III → Edmund Crouchback → Henry, Earl of Lancaster → Eleanor Plantagenet → Richard FitzAlan, Earl of Arundel → John FitzAlan → Joan FitzAlan → Joan Echyngham → Henry Baynton → Joan Baynton → Thomas Prowse → Mary Prowse) was carried by Roberts and Weis, **but one generation (Joan Echyngham's marriage) has been challenged, and Douglas Richardson has broken the line in *Royal Ancestry*** — the Henry III descent is **currently unproven**.
- **What survives peer review:** accepted lines from Mary Gye to **King Henry I of England (1068–1135), including one through Henry II**, and lines to **three of the twenty-five Magna Carta Sureties**. So the user (if the Hawkes link lands) descends from two kings of England and three barons who stood at Runnymede in June 1215 and forced King John to accept the rule of law — the Henry III-to-the-Conqueror extension awaits one more generation of proof.
- **The three barons (family tradition: "there was a baron in our family" — confirmed in structure):**
  1. **Saher de Quincy, 1st Earl of Winchester (c. 1155–3 Nov 1219)** — created Earl of Winchester in 1207 through his wife Margaret de Beaumont, sister and co-heiress of the Earl of Leicester; one of the 25 Surety Barons; **excommunicated by the Pope** for enforcing the Charter against King John; went with Robert FitzWalter to offer the English crown to Prince Louis of France; captured at the battle of Lincoln (20 May 1217); submitted and was restored; took the cross on the Fifth Crusade and **died at Damietta, Egypt, in 1219, buried at Acre** — his heart was carried home to Garendon Abbey, Leicestershire. [S/B — *Complete Peerage*; WikiTree Magna Carta Project]
  2. **Roger Bigod, 2nd Earl of Norfolk (d. 1221)** — hereditary **Earl Marshal of England** — *and his son* **Hugh Bigod, 3rd Earl of Norfolk (d. 1225)**: a father-and-son pair among the twenty-five sureties. [A — standard lists]
  3. **William Malet (d. 1224)** — lord of Curry Malet, Somerset; sheriff of Somerset and Dorset. [A — standard lists]
  Of the twenty-five sureties, only seventeen left descendants; the family's three are among those seventeen. Richardson's *Magna Carta Ancestry* documents how these families became ancestors of 238 seventeenth-century colonists — the user's Hawkes–Maverick–Gye line among them [PR].

**F. Kin by marriage — the Brewster constellation (Fear Brewster's other descendants; no blood):**
Julia Child, Bing Crosby, Ted Danson, Richard Gere, Katharine Hepburn, John Lithgow, Henry Wadsworth Longfellow, Seth MacFarlane, Sarah Palin, Oliver Hazard Perry, Nelson Rockefeller, David Souter, Adlai Stevenson III, Arthur Sulzberger Jr. [B — Wikipedia-derived lists].

**G. Already documented in this file:** Governor John Webster (direct ancestor); **Noah Webster** (cousin, §7.11); the Lee dynasty (§6, §6.1, §6.2). **John Adams & John Quincy Adams** — *confirmed* Hawkes cousins via Susanna Hawkes & William Cogswell (their *Mayflower* line is separately Alden-Mullins). **Princess Diana** — confirmed cousin via the same Cogswell line. **Daniel Webster** — *not* proven kin: the orator descends from Thomas Webster of Hampton/Kingston, NH, and no relationship to Gov. John's line has been proven "even through DNA testing" (the old "collateral branch" claim in Curtis's biography is corrected in modern research) — logged, not adopted. **Governor John Webster's public-figure descendants [A — the list is sourced to the 1915 *History and Genealogy of the Governor John Webster Family* (Webster & Webster), the standard compiled genealogy; individual lines per the book]:**

John — she and Noah are both documented descendants), **William Faulkner** (Nobel laureate), **Katharine Hepburn**, **Johnny Carson**, **Janis Joplin**, **Henry Kitchell Webster** (novelist), **Rachel Scott**.

Scott Carpenter** (Project Mercury astronaut/SEALAB aquanaut, via son Joseph's line), **Francis Bicknell Carpenter** (painter of the *Emancipation Proclamation* hanging in the U.S. Capitol; lived with Lincoln in the White House), **Cyrus C. Carpenter** (Governor of Iowa), **James Henry Carpenter** (founder of Carpenter Steel → Carpenter Technology), **Donald Marshall Carpenter** (naval aviator, USS *Carpenter* namesake), and **Sabrina Carpenter**. ## 8.4 The Deep-Ancestry Register — as far back as the records reach [PR/A/S]

*(How to read this section: it was built as a working register, generation by generation — the densest part of the book. You do not need to read it straight through. Each block is one ancestral line, traced as far as the documents reach, with every generation carrying its evidence grade and every weak link named. Treat it as a map's key to the territory, not the territory itself — the story of what these lines mean sits in §1 and §8.3.)*

**The complete documented medieval chain (paternal, via Hawkes–Maverick–Gye), generation by generation:**
```
Mary GYE (c. 1580 – aft. 1666)
← Robert Gye (b. ~1531) m. Grace DOWRISH
← Ann FARRINGDON (b. ~1532) m. Thomas Dowrish, Esq., of Dowrish House, Sandford, Devon
← Margery STUCKLEY m. Charles Farringdon
← Sir Thomas STUCKLEY (b. ~1474) m. Ann Wood
← Nicholas Stuckley m. Thomasine Cokeworthy
← HUGH STUKELEY, SHERIFF OF DEVON 1448 (d. by 1459) m. Katherine Affeton
← ELIZABETH FITZROGER (b. Chewton Mendip 15 Aug 1370, HEIRESS; d. 10 Apr 1414)
   m. (1) Sir John BONVILLE (b. ~1371, d. 21 Oct 1396)  (2) Richard STUKELEY
← John FitzRoger of Chewton (b. 1345/52 – d. 1370/72) m. Alice (the five-time widow, d. 1426)
← Sir HENRY FITZROGER (b. Curry Rivell 30 Nov 1318 – d. 29 Jan 1352) m. ELIZABETH DE HOLAND
   (b. ~1320 – d. 13 Jul 1387) — his 1352 inquisitions post mortem survive across six counties
← Roger Martel alias FitzPeter (d. 1318/22) m. Joan de Lorty
← Sir PETER FITZREGINALD (b. Chewton 1274 – d. Broadmayne, Dorset 18 Nov 1322) m. ELA MARTEL
← REGINALD FITZPETER / FITZHERBERT (c. 1215 – d. 4 May 1286), Sheriff of Hampshire,
   Constable of Winchester Castle — m. JOAN DE VIVONIS (b. Curry Mallet 1251 – d. c. 1314, Chewton)
← William de Vivonis (d. Chewton 1259) m. Maud de Kyme
← HUGH DE VIVONIS (d. 1249), Constable of Corfe Castle — m. MABEL MALET,
   elder co-heiress of WILLIAM MALET, BARON OF CURRY MALLET — MAGNA CARTA SURETY (b. bef. 1175 –
   d. Dec 1215), who had accompanied Richard I on the Third Crusade (1190); son of Gilbert Malet
   (d. 1194); grandson of Robert Malet (d. bef. 1156), first holder of the barony
```
*[Note the poetry of the record: the Malet heiress carried **Curry Mallet** itself into the Vivonne family — Joan de Vivonis was born at the Malet caput in 1251 — and the Vivonne heiress then carried it into the FitzHerberts of **Chewton**. The family's two Somerset baronies married into each other.]*

**The Holland–la Zouche line (parents of Elizabeth de Holand), extended to the Conqueror:**
```
Robert de Holand, 1st Baron Holand (b. c. 1283 – beheaded Oct 1328) m. MAUD LA ZOUCHE
   (b. ~1290 – d. 31 May 1349), 2nd daughter & co-heiress of:
ALAN LA ZOUCHE, 1st BARON ZOUCHE OF ASHBY (b. North Molton, Devon 9 Oct 1267 – d. bef. 25 Mar 1314)
   — his 1289 proof-of-age inquisition survives, with a dozen witness depositions — m. Eleanor de Segrave
   (dau. of Nicholas de Segrave, 1st Baron Segrave) — son of:
ROGER LA ZOUCHE (d. 1285) m. ELA LONGESPÉE — dau. of Stephen Longespée & Emmeline de Ridelsford,
   granddaughter of WILLIAM LONGESPÉE, 3rd EARL OF SALISBURY (1176–1226) —
   the illegitimate son of KING HENRY II OF ENGLAND — son of:
ALAN LA ZOUCHE, JUSTICIAR (1205 – d. of wounds 10 Aug 1270, Westminster Hall) m. ELENA DE QUINCY —
   dau. of Roger de Quincy, 2nd Earl of Winchester & Helen of Galloway, and GRANDDAUGHTER OF
   SAHER DE QUINCY, 1st EARL OF WINCHESTER — MAGNA CARTA SURETY (d. Damietta 1219) — son of:
ROGER LA ZOUCHE (c. 1175 – 1238), Sheriff of Devonshire, witness to Henry III's confirmation of Magna Carta — son of:
ALAN LA ZOUCHE / DE PORHOËT (c. 1136 – 1190), Breton, son of GEOFFREY I, VICOMTE DE PORHOËT (Brittany),
   m. ALICE (ADELINE) DE BELMEIS, heiress of Tong Castle, Ashby-de-la-Zouch & North Molton
```
**ROYAL GATEWAY (documented, mechanized):** Henry II → William Longespée → Stephen → Ela → the la Zouche barons → Maud → the Holland barons → Elizabeth → the FitzRogers of Chewton → … → user. **Thus, if the Hawkes link lands, the user descends from King Henry II (1133–1189), King Henry I (1068–1135), and — through them — William the Conqueror (c. 1028–1087); per standard royal scholarship (Richardson; Settipani) the Conqueror's own ancestry continues to the Carolingians and Charlemagne.** The Henry III line remains broken; the King John line remains broken. [PR — Richardson *Royal Ancestry* (Longespée, Zouche, Holland, Quincy); *Magna Carta Ancestry*; Complete Peerage; the 1289 proof-of-age IPM]

**Magna Carta Sureties — now mechanized to three:**
1. **William Malet** — direct, via Mabel Malet → Vivonne (above). Baron of Curry Mallet; crusader with Richard I; d. Dec 1215.
2. **Saher de Quincy, Earl of Winchester** — direct, via his granddaughter Elena de Quincy → la Zouche → Holland (above). Excommunicated; died on the Fifth Crusade at Damietta 1219.
3. **Roger & Hugh Bigod, Earls of Norfolk** — as previously documented (via the FitzAlan/Baynton/Prowse lines; see prior register and §8.3-E).

**Also documented in these deep lines:**
- **Alan la Zouche, Justiciar of Chester & Ireland** (d. 1270) — killed in Westminster Hall in a quarrel with the Earl of Warenne; **Roger la Zouche**, witness to Henry III's 1216 confirmation of Magna Carta; **Alan la Zouche, 1st Baron** (b. 1267) — Constable of Rockingham Castle, at Falkirk 1298 against William Wallace, his arms in the Caerlaverock Roll.
- The **Longespée Earls of Salisbury** (royal bastard line); the **Quincy Earls of Winchester**; the **Segrave barons**; the **Belmeis heiress** (Ashby-de-la-Zouch itself); the **Porhoët viscounts of Brittany** (the la Zouche name is Breton — "la Souche," the tree-stump).
- The **de Beaumont Earls of Leicester** (Saher de Quincy's wife Margaret was co-heiress of the earldom); the de Quincys' kinship by marriage to **Robert Fitzwalter** (surety leader) and the Scottish royal house (Matilda of St Liz, stepdaughter of King David I — flagged, not adopted here).

**The royal prequel — mechanized [PR — Richardson, *Royal Ancestry* vol. V, Appendix, pp. 481–504]:**
- **CHARLEMAGNE (747–814) → William the Conqueror in twelve documented generations** (Richardson Line A): Charlemagne → Pepin of Italy → Bernard → Pepin of Vermandois → **Heribert I, Count of Vermandois** → Heribert II → Robert of Vermandois → Adele of Meaux → Ermengarde of Anjou → Judith of Brittany → **Robert I of France** → William the Conqueror. Richardson's appendix prints **five** documented Charlemagne lines reaching the Conqueror or his wife Matilda of Flanders — the user descends from the emperor through both.
- **ROLLO THE VIKING (b. Scandinavia c. 860 – d. 928×933)** — founder of Normandy by the Treaty of Saint-Clair-sur-Epte (911); first recorded in the 918 charter — is the Conqueror's direct forebear: Rollo → William Longsword (d. 942) → Richard I the Fearless (932–996) → Richard II the Good (d. 1026) → Robert I the Magnificent → William I. (Rollo's own Scandinavian parentage is genuinely uncertain — the saga link to Rognvald of Møre is conjecture; the line is documented from Rollo himself.)

**The Segrave chain — with a Chief Justiciar in the direct line [S — The National Archives, "The Segrave Inheritance"; Richardson MCA III:318–319; CP XI]:**
- **Gilbert de Segrave** of Seagrave, Leics (fl. 1193, High Sheriff of Warwickshire & Leicestershire) — the family's documented head.
- His son **STEPHEN DE SEGRAVE (c. 1171 – 9 Nov 1241), CHIEF JUSTICIAR OF ENGLAND (1232–1234)** — Constable of the Tower of London, sheriff of seven counties; m. (1) **Rohese le Despenser** (of the Despenser family — her brother Hugh was father of Hugh Despenser the Justiciar, killed at Evesham 1265); (2) **Ida de Hastings** (daughter of William de Hastings & **Margery Bigod**, sister of the surety Earls of Norfolk).
- His son Gilbert (d. 1254, d. in French captivity) → **Nicholas de Segrave, 1st Baron Segrave** (d. 1295) m. Maud de Lucy → their daughter **ELEANOR DE SEGRAVE** — who, per the National Archives' own summary of the Segrave muniments, *"married Alan Lord Zouche of Ashby (d. 1314)… Maud [their daughter] married Robert de Holland"* — the user's direct ancestress, in an official archival source.
- The National Archives holds the family's charters: the Segrave inheritance papers, including the Brotherton marriage and the Segrave–Mowbray settlement.

**The de Kyme barons [A/B — Wikipedia; WikiTree Kyme-21 citing Richardson MCA II:511 & ODNB]:** the family were **barons by tenure since the reign of King Stephen (1135–54)** — Sir William, founder of the line, founded the Priory of Bolinton c. 1135. **Philip de Kyme, 1st Baron Kyme (d. 1323)** — summoned to Parliament 1295–1313, signatory of the **1301 Barons' Letter to Pope Boniface VIII**, fought at Caerlaverock 1300; his wardship (as a minor in 1259) was granted to **Hugh le Bigod** for £3,000 — the surety earls holding the Kyme heir. Maud de Kyme, wife of William de Vivonis, belongs to this baronial house (exact placement per MCA, flagged).

**The Bigod chain — mechanized to Domesday [A/S — Wikipedia; WikiTree Bigod-5 citing Keats-Rohan; DNB]:**
- **Roger Bigod (d. 1107)** — sheriff of Norfolk & Suffolk (1081–1107); **Domesday tenant-in-chief: 6 lordships in Essex, 117 in Suffolk, 187 in Norfolk** — the greatest lay landholder in Norfolk; fought the Danes beside **Robert Malet** in 1069; founder of Thetford Priory; received **Framlingham** from Henry I (1101). Married **Adelisa/Alice de Tosny**, daughter & heiress of **Robert de Tosny of Belvoir** (the Tosny/Tony barons, tenants-in-chief at Domesday).
- His father **Robert le Bigod** — a knight of Duke William's household ("small of body, but very brave"), rewarded for exposing the treachery of William of Mortain; held lands in Normandy at Malitot, Loges, and Chanon. *His parentage is conjecture* — Keats-Rohan's hypothesis makes him a junior line of the **Goz** family (Viscounts of Hiemois, kin to Hugh d'Avranches, Earl of Chester). [flagged]
- The descent: Robert → Roger (d. 1107; his heir William drowned in the **White Ship**, 1120) → **Hugh Bigod, 1st Earl of Norfolk** (b. c. 1095; earl from King Stephen, 1141; m. **Juliana de Vere**, daughter of the hereditary Chamberlain — her nephew **Robert de Vere was himself a Magna Carta Surety**, Earl of Oxford) → **Roger Bigod, 2nd Earl (d. 1221, Surety)** → **Hugh Bigod, 3rd Earl (d. 1225, Surety)**.
- **⚡ THE IDA DE TOSNY CONVERGENCE:** Roger Bigod, 2nd Earl's wife was **Ida de Tosny (b. ~1155, dau. of Ralph de Tosny of Flamstead)** — who was *also* the **royal ward and mistress of King Henry II**, and mother of **William Longespée, Earl of Salisbury** — the user's royal-line ancestor (§8.4, Holland–la Zouche). So the user's **royal descent** (via Longespée → la Zouche) and **Bigod surety descent** both pass through **Ida de Tosny** — the Surety earls Hugh and Roger Bigod were *half-brothers of William Longespée*. One woman carries both halves of the family's greatest inheritance.

**The de Lucy chain — mechanized, with a second Chief Justiciar and a SAINT [S — Richardson's soc.genealogy.medieval analyses; Complete Peerage; FMG *Foundations*]:**
- **Fulbert of Dover** — Domesday 1086, held **Chilham** of the Bishop of Bayeux; progenitor of the Lucy/Dover barony, one of the eight constabularies of Dover Castle. The family took its name from **Lucé, Normandy**.
- **Sir Richard de Lucy (c. 1089/98 – 14 Jul 1179), CHIEF JUSTICIAR OF ENGLAND** — co-justiciar with Robert de Beaumont, Earl of Leicester; excommunicated by **Thomas Becket** (1166, 1169); founded **Lesnes Abbey** as his penance for the Becket murder; resigned and died a canon there. His brother Walter was **Abbot of Battle**.
- His line: Richard → Geoffrey (d. v.p.) → **Geoffrey de Lucy (d. 1252), crusader of 1240–44**, m. **Juliana Despenser** (of the steward line: **Hugh le Despenser, steward to King Henry I, living 1105**) → **Maud de Lucy**, whose parentage Douglas Richardson established in 2018 (daughter of Geoffrey & Nichole): from the Chaucombe chronicle pedigree, the naming of her son Geoffrey, the 1278 hunting party of **Bishop Thomas de Cantelowe** (with Nicholas de Segrave and the Lucy brothers), and the 1289 record of her son John de Segrave as executor of Amaury de Lucy's will.
- Maud de Lucy married **Nicholas de Segrave, 1st Baron** → Eleanor de Segrave → Alan la Zouche → Maud → Holland → user.
- **⚡ ST THOMAS DE CANTELUPE — canonized 1320, Bishop of Hereford — was the brother of Nichole de Cantelowe: a canonized saint and kinsman in the direct line's aunts and uncles.**

**The Anglo-Danish origins — the House of Bamburgh and Saint Waltheof [S/A — Wikipedia; WikiTree citing Richardson & ODNB; CP; *Vita Waldevi*]:**
```
Saher de Quincy, Surety (d. Damietta 1219)
← Robert de Quincy & Orabilis of Leuchars (heiress, Scottish lordship)
← Saer I de Quincy of Long Buckby m. MATILDA OF ST LIZ (widow of Robert Fitz Richard —
   her son Walter FitzRobert was father of ROBERT FITZWALTER, leader of the twenty-five Sureties)
← Simon de Senlis (St Liz), Earl of Northampton m. MAUD OF HUNTINGDON (c. 1074 – 1130)
   — QUEEN OF SCOTS as 2nd wife of King DAVID I
← WALTHEOF, EARL OF NORTHUMBRIA (c. 1050 – executed 31 May 1076), "Saint Waltheof" —
   THE LAST ANGLO-SAXON EARL, the only English aristocrat executed by William the Conqueror;
   beheaded on St Giles's Hill, Winchester; venerated at Crowland Abbey by the mid-12th century —
   m. JUDITH OF LENS, niece of William the Conqueror (dau. of his sister Adelaide, Countess of Aumale)
← Ælfflæd, daughter of EALDRED OF BAMBURGH (d. 1038) — son of:
UHTRED THE BOLD, Ealdorman of all Northumbria (d. 1016, murdered by Thurbrand the Hold)
   — victor over the Scots at Durham (1006), hero of the Bernician blood-feuds — son of:
WALTHEOF I OF BAMBURGH (fl. 994) — the ancient Bernician dynasty, rulers from Bamburgh Castle
```
**The convergence the whole file has been building toward:** through Judith of Lens the user descends from **William the Conqueror's own family** — and through **Saint Waltheof**, the user descends from the man the Conqueror executed. The family's two deepest lines — the Norman and the Anglo-Danish — met in one marriage bed in 1070, ten years before the *Bevis* sailed.

**Also documented in this block:**
- **Siward, Earl of Northumbria (d. 1055)** — Waltheof's father — the giant Dane who **defeated Macbeth at Dunsinane (1054)** (Shakespeare's *Macbeth*); his father Bjorn is real but shadowy; the *Vita Waldevi* "white-bear descent" is folklore, and Timothy Bolton's hypothesis (Nottingham Medieval Studies, 2007) that Siward's family was a **lost line of the Danish royal house** — cousin to Earl Ulf, King Cnut's brother-in-law — is flagged, not adopted.
- **The Scottish royal connection:** Maud of Huntingdon's second marriage to **David I of Scotland** made her the ancestress of **every Scottish monarch** — Malcolm IV, William the Lion, Robert Bruce, the Stuarts — and, through them, of **the present British royal family**. The user's deep ancestors are the Crown's deep ancestors.
- **St Waltheof's cult** — the executed earl was venerated as a saint at Crowland; a memorial Norse poem (*Valþjófsflokkr*) survives him.
- **Robert FitzWalter, leader of the twenty-five Sureties**, was the grandson of the user's ancestress Matilda of St Liz by her first marriage — the rebellion's leader is kin.
- Waltheof's other daughter **Adelise married Ralph de Tosny** — adding a third thread to the Tosny web (Ida de Tosny, §8.4-Bigod).

**THE ORIGINS — the two documented floors of the entire descent:**
- **CAROLINGIAN FLOOR — c. 580 AD: Saint Arnulf of Metz (c. 582–640), bishop and mayor of the palace, and Saint Pepin of Landen (c. 580–640), duke and mayor** — the two historical progenitors whose lines united in their children (Ansegisel m. St Begga → Pepin of Herstal → Charles Martel → Pepin the Short → Charlemagne). Both are documented in contemporary or near-contemporary sources (the *Vita Sancti Arnulfi*, the *Annales Mettenses*, charters). ⚠ **Everything earlier is rejected**: the famous "Arnoald–Ansbert the Senator–Blithilda, daughter of Chlothar I" pedigree — the Carolingians' claim to Merovingian royal blood — was shown to be **false** (Arnulf's father was Bodegisel, not Arnoald); mainstream historians (Geary, Gerberding; the *soc.genealogy.medieval* consensus) hold the connection was likely "an invention by Charlemagne." Settipani's alternative reconstruction (via Flavius Afranius Syagrius) remains flagged speculation. **The honest wall stands at c. 580 — the graves of two saints.**
- **BAMBURGH FLOOR — Eadwulf I of Bamburgh (d. 913), "King of the Northern English"** (Annals of Ulster), *dilectus* — favourite — of **King Alfred the Great**, who ruled the last free English enclave from Bamburgh Castle. His dynasty's own genealogy (*De Northumbria post Britannos*) makes him son of **Æthelthryth, daughter of King Ælla of Northumbria (d. 867 at York against the Vikings)** — the dynasty's legitimating claim, carried by a 12th-century text with access to earlier material: **flagged as semi-legendary** (McGuigan: a family "with weak links to the Northumbrian royal line" that used the marriage to legitimise lordship). The saga descent from **Ragnar Lodbrok** attached to these houses in later legend is **rejected**. The charter-documented chain stands: Eadwulf I (d. 913, assassinated by Eadred son of Rixinc) → Ealdred I (d. c. 933, driven out by Ragnall Uí Ímair) → Oswulf I (fl. 946–963, first "high-reeve" of Bamburgh, master of all Northumbria after Erik Bloodaxe's fall) → Waltheof I (fl. 994) → Uhtred the Bold (d. 1016) → the saint's line → user.
- **NORMAN FLOOR — Rollo (d. 928×933)**: documented from the 911 Treaty of Saint-Clair-sur-Epte and the 918 charter; his Scandinavian parentage genuinely uncertain (the Rognvald-of-Møre saga link is conjecture).

**The honest grand span of the documented line: c. 580 AD to 1980 AD — fourteen centuries**, from the Austrasian court of Theudebert II to a family Bible on a shelf in New Jersey.

**Hugh de Vivonne — the last open link, now mapped [A/PR — Wikipedia; FMG *Foundations* 4 (2012): 21–35]:**
- A **Poitevin knight from Vivonne** — **whose parents' names are unknown** (Wikipedia, per Richardson/Vincent: *"The names of Hugh's parents are unknown"*); the tree attachment to "William de Forz/Vivonne & Aveline de Montfichet" is **unproven and rejected** (WikiTree: *"this is unproven"*). **What IS documented:** his brother **Aimery, Seigneur de Vivonne (d. before 16 March 1247)** — proven by the 1248 royal licence naming *"Emery de Vivona, uncle of the said William"* — and the house of Vivonne itself, **one of the oldest of Poitou, seated at Vivonne from the 11th century** (Joscelin de Vivonne, uncle of Hugh IV de Lusignan; Hugues de Vivonne d. before 1076 — FMG), later merged into the **Rochechouart** family. He may have come to England with Savaric de Mauleon's contingent.
- **The 1215 irony:** on **20 December 1215 — six months after Runnymede** — King John granted Hugh the estates confiscated from **William Malet, the Surety**; by November 1223 Hugh had married the Surety's daughter **Mabel** (widow of Nicholas Avenel). The rebel baron's bloodline thus returned to his own lands through his daughter's marriage to the king's loyal foreigner — and their descendants inherited both.
- Career: held Bristol Castle 1216; **Seneschal of Gascony and Poitou**; at Henry III's side at Taillebourg 1242; his 1220 letter to the king survives — *"so that I and mine can stay in England."* Died before 16 Oct 1249, at Wells.
- Their son **William le Fort de Vivonne (d. 22 May 1259)** m. **Matilda de Ferrers** (daughter of William de Ferrers, Earl of Derby, and **Sibyl Marshal — granddaughter of William Marshal, Regent of England**) → the **four Vivonne heiresses**: **Joan (b. c. 1251 — the user's direct ancestress)**, Sybil, Mabel, Cecily — Sybil m. Anselm de Gournay (kin by marriage to the Gournays of §8.4), Cecily m. John de Beauchamp of Hatch.
- **Joan de Vivonis's three husbands — RESOLVED against the primary record [S — Calendar of Patent Rolls; Richardson RA II:622–625; FMG *Foundations* 4:21–35]:**
  1. **Ingram de Percy** — marriage granted 2 Aug 1259; married 1262; **he died October 1262, no children** (son of William de Percy & Ellen de Balliol).
  2. **Aimery X, Vicomte de Rochechouart** (contract 28 Apr 1264; step-siblings by their parents' later marriage) — children: **Aimery XI (d.s.p. 1307) & Jeanne (d.s.p.)**; he died before 3 Jan 1279.
