  3. **Sir Reynold Fitz Peter** (m. before 3 Jan 1279, probably by 1272) — **four sons and two daughters: PETER FITZREYNOLD (b. c. 1274, d. 18 Nov 1322 — Lord of Chewton, the user's direct line)**, Reynold, Matthew, William, Isabel, Beatrice.
  Joan died **1 June 1314**; her IPM (writ 8 June 1314) spread across Bedfordshire, Southampton, Essex, Berkshire, Somerset, Dorset — the Somerset and Dorset lands passing to her sons **Reginald and Peter**. Her long widowhood was spent largely in France (letters of protection to go overseas 1290, 1292, 1301, 1307).

**The Tosny line — documented to the Archbishop's family (late 900s) [A — Wikipedia *House of Tosny*; Complete Peerage XII/1: 755–7]:**
- The house begins with **Raoul I of Tosny (fl. ~1000, d. 1024/25)** — son of **Hugh de Calvacamp**, brother of **Hugh III, Archbishop of Rouen (942–989)**, who granted the family its lands from the church's holdings. (The later claim, inserted by Orderic Vitalis, that the Tosnys were "of the lineage of Malahul, *uncle of Rollo*" — flagged as a 12th-century pedigree-building interpolation.)
- **Roger I de Tosny "the Spaniard" (b. ~990, d. 1038/9)** — banner-bearer of all Normandy; four years fighting in the Spanish Reconquista; rebelled against the boy-duke William on the ground that "a bastard ought not to rule"; killed by **Roger de Beaumont** in 1040 with two of his sons.
- **Raoul II de Conches (d. 1102)** — the Norman **standard-bearer at Hastings** (a candidate for the figure on the Bayeux Tapestry); received **Flamstead** and 35 manors; m. Isabelle de Montfort. His descendants: Raoul III (m. **Adelise, daughter of Waltheof of Huntingdon — the SAINT's own line entering the Tosny web a second time**), down to **Raoul/Ralph V de Tosny (d. 1162) m. Margaret de Beaumont, daughter of the Earl of Leicester**.
- **IDA DE TOSNY (b. ~1154, d. 1226)** — the convergence woman — was documented by a **primary discovery: William Longespée's own charter naming "Comitissa Ida, mater mea"** ("Countess Ida, my mother"), found in 1979; and by Marc Morris's *The Bigod Earls of Norfolk* (2005). Royal ward; given to Roger Bigod at Christmas 1181 with three manors restored. She descends from the **Beaumont Earls of Leicester on both sides** (Marguerite of Leicester × Constance de Beaumont in successive generations).

**The Marshal–de Clare–Irish-royal branch — Maud de Ferrers mechanized [S/A — *Calendar of Patent Rolls*; Richardson; *Historia* & encyclopedia entries]:**
- **Maud de Ferrers (b. ~1230, d. 12 Mar 1298)** — daughter of **William de Ferrers, 5th Earl of Derby (d. 1254; gout-ridden, died thrown from his litter while crossing a river; buried at Merevale Abbey)** and **Sibyl Marshal (c. 1201 – d. bef. 1238), 3rd daughter and co-heiress of William Marshal, Earl of Pembroke** — "the greatest knight in Christendom": landless fourth son who rose by hostage-taking and unflinching loyalty to become **Regent of England (1216–1219) for the boy Henry III, and a named guarantor of Magna Carta's regency** — and **Isabel de Clare, heiress of Pembroke and Leinster**.
- **Isabel de Clare (c. 1174–1220)** — daughter of **Richard "Strongbow" de Clare, 2nd Earl of Pembroke (d. 1176), conqueror of Ireland**, and **Aoife (Eva) MacMurrough of Leinster (b. 1145 – d. 1188)** — daughter of **Diarmait Mac Murchada (Dermot MacMurrough), King of Leinster**. **The user's deep line therefore includes the Irish royal house of Uí Cheinnselaig** — through the most famous marriage in Irish history.
- Maud's three husbands, in order: **(1) Simon de Kyme** of Lincolnshire (d. by 30 Jul 1248 — hence her style "Maud de Kyme"); **(2) William de Vivonne/de Forz** of Chewton — the user's line; **(3) Amaury IX, Vicomte de Rochechouart**.
- **The Ferrers–Chester branch:** William 5th Earl's mother was **Agnes of Chester (d. 1247), daughter of Hugh de Kevelioc, 3rd Earl of Chester, and Bertrade de Montfort of Evreux** — so the Ferrers earls carry the **Meschine earls of Chester** (Ranulf le Meschin, 1st Earl, son of Ranulf de Briquessart, Viscount of the Bessin — the *same deep Bessin root* as Alice de Belmeis in the la Zouche line: the Meschines enter the family twice) — **and the Montfort–Evreux blood of Simon de Montfort's kin.**
- The Ferrers male line itself descends from **Henry de Ferrers (Domesday commissioner; castle of Tutbury)** — the family is Domesday-documented.
- Sibling notes: Maud's sister **Sibyl Ferrers m. Francis de Bohun of Midhurst** — a line from which **Daniel Boone** descends (per compiled pedigrees; flagged [B/NA]); her sister **Eleanor m. (2nd) Roger de Quincy, 2nd Earl of Winchester** — the surety earls once more (a famously tangled step-relation flagged in the sources). William Marshal's parents were **John FitzGilbert the Marshal & Sibyl of Salisbury** (the d'Evereux–Salisbury line).

**The de Clare line — from the ducal house itself [PR/A — Altschul, *A Baronial Family in Medieval England: The Clares*; J.H. Round, DNB "Clare"; 1911 Britannica]:**
- **GODFREY (GEOFFREY) OF BRIONNE (d. 1015) — eldest illegitimate son of RICHARD I "THE FEARLESS," Duke of Normandy** — the Conqueror's great-grandfather; granted Brionne; Count of Eu by his half-brother Duke Richard II after 996. **[A THIRD documented descent into the pre-Conquest Norman ducal house.]**
- His son **Gilbert "Crispin," Count of Brionne (c. 1000 – assassinated 1040)** — one of the young Duke William's guardians (1035); murdered by Ralph de Gacé's faction while peaceably riding near Echauffour; his sons Richard and Baldwin fled to the court of Baldwin V of Flanders and were restored by the Conqueror's marriage to Matilda.
- **Richard fitz Gilbert de Clare (d. 1090)** — granted Bienfaite and Orbec; in England **176 lordships (95 in Suffolk)**, Clare Castle and Tonbridge Castle; **JOINT CHIEF JUSTICIAR OF ENGLAND** with William de Warenne (the family's *third* Chief Justiciar); suppressed the revolt of 1075; m. **Rohese Giffard** (of the Longueville Giffards — 30 ships at the invasion, Earls of Buckingham, Marshals of England).
- The descent: Richard fitz Gilbert → Gilbert fitz Richard (d. 1115, first hereditary Marshal of England) → Richard fitz Gilbert (d. 1136) → Gilbert, created **Earl of Pembroke (1138)** → **Richard "Strongbow" de Clare, Earl of Pembroke (d. 1176), conqueror of Ireland** → his daughter & heiress **Isabel de Clare** m. **William Marshal** → Sibyl Marshal → Maud de Ferrers → William de Vivonne → Joan → FitzRogers → user.
- Kin note: Richard fitz Gilbert's daughter married **William de Montfichet** — "their descendant was one of the twenty-five barons of Magna Carta" (Montfichet surety).

**The Galloway line — the Norse-Gaelic lords [S/A — POMS (King's College London); Wikipedia *Helen of Galloway*; DGNHAS *Transactions* (Kippax/Lacy analysis); CP XII/2: 752–3]:**
- **Helen of Galloway (b. ~1208 – d. aft. 21 Nov 1245, buried Brackley)** — daughter & co-heiress of **Alan, Lord of Galloway (d. 1234), High Constable of Scotland**, by his first wife — her mother's identity is debated: CP leaves her unnamed; Richardson's argument (from the Kippax descent) makes her a daughter of **Roger de Lacy of Pontefract, Constable of Chester**; Turton's older "of the Isles" assignment is flagged. Helen's marriage made Roger de Quincy **Constable of Scotland**.
- The Galloway dynasty: Alan ← **Roland (Lochlann) of Galloway (d. 1200)** m. **Elena de Morville** (of the constable Morvilles of Lauderdale) ← **Uchtred of Galloway (d. 1174 — blinded and murdered at the orders of his brother Gilbert)** m. **Gunnild of Dunbar** — the house of **Fergus of Galloway (d. 1161)**, the greatest of the **Gall-Gaidhil — the Norse-Gaelic** lords who held a semi-independent principality in southwest Scotland.
- **Royal Scottish kin (again):** Alan of Galloway's second wife was **Margaret de Huntingdon** (daughter of David, Earl of Huntingdon — grandson of King David I); their daughter **Devorguilla of Galloway married John de Balliol — and their son became KING JOHN BALLIOL OF SCOTLAND (r. 1292–96).** Helen's half-nephew was a crowned king of Scots; through Devorguilla, the user descends from the Balliols' kin-network of the Scottish succession.

**ALFRED THE GREAT — the Anglo-Saxon royal floor, documented [S/A — Symeon of Durham; *De obsessione Dunelmi*; CP; Scots Peerage]:**
- **Gunnild of Dunbar** (wife of Uchtred of Galloway — the Galloway-line ancestress) was daughter of **Gospatric, Earl of Dunbar/March** — whose mother **Ealdgyth was daughter of Uhtred the Bold** and his wife **Ælfgifu, daughter of King Æthelred II "the Unready."**
- **Thus, via one woman — Ælfgifu — the line ascends: Gunnild → Gospatric → Ealdgyth & Ælfgifu → ÆTHELRED II (r. 978–1016) → Edgar the Peaceful → Edmund I → Edward the Elder → ALFRED THE GREAT (849–899), King of Wessex.** The Wessex royal house, documented in the regnal tradition of the Anglo-Saxon Chronicle.
- **The pre-Alfred genealogy, honestly graded [S/A — the Parker Chronicle's 855 pedigree; Stenton; Heather Edwards ODNB; Naismith]:** Alfred → Æthelwulf → **Egbert (d. 839, first King of all England)** → **Ealhmund, King of Kent (documented by charter of 784)** → *Eafa and Eoppa — named nowhere else in any source* (uncertain links) → **Ingild, brother of King Ine of Wessex — documented by the Chronicle's own 718 annal** → Cenred → Ceolwald → Ceawlin → Cynric → **Cerdic (d. 534, the Chronicle's founder — possibly himself of British, even Romano-British stock: "Cerdic" is the Welsh *Ceredic/Caradog*)** → then the pedigree mounts "through some nine generations of dim, mysterious beings" to **Woden** — the god — and, by borrowing from the Bernician genealogy, to Geat and finally, "by the device of a son born to Noah in the ark," to Adam. *The honest floor stands at Ingild (fl. 718); above him the links thin to legend, and the document says so.*
- The descent of Elizabeth II from the same line is carried in the standard tables (51 generations from Cerdic) — the user's Wessex kin and the Crown's are one.
- Gospatric himself is one of the great figures of the north: **maternal grandson of Uhtred the Bold; claimed descent from Crínán (possibly of Dunkeld, father of King Duncan I — the *Macbeth* connection, challenged by modern historians and flagged); BOUGHT the earldom of Northumbria from William the Conqueror in 1067**; deprived 1072; granted Dunbar by Malcolm III; ancestor of the Earls of Dunbar & March (to 1435) — and, through Uchtred and Æthelred, kin to both the Bamburgh dynasty and the house of Wessex.

**BRIAN BORU — the Irish high-kingly floor [A/S — Byrne, *Irish Kings and High-Kings*; Wikipedia; DIB]:**
- Dermot MacMurrough's **great-grandmother Derbforgaill was a daughter of Donnchad, King of Munster — and granddaughter of BRIAN BORU (d. 1014), High King of Ireland, victor of Clontarf.** The blood of Ireland's greatest high king runs in the line.
- The male-line Uí Cheinnselaig kings are annal-documented to **Domnall mac Cellaig (d. 974)**; the dynasty's claim to the ancient line of Crimthann mac Énnai (d. late 400s) is flagged as legend. *Father-flag:* Diarmait's father is Donnchad mac Murchada (d. 1115, the king buried with a dog) per the standard sources; a conflated "Enna" tradition is noted and rejected.
- Compiled kin-lists through Aoife (flagged [B/NA]): George Washington, Marie Antoinette, Lord Edward Fitzgerald, Robert Emmet, Charles Darwin, Winston Churchill.

**The Évreux–Gloucester block behind Bertrade de Montfort [S/A — FMG *MedLands*; CP; Richardson]:**
- Bertrade de Montfort, wife of Hugh de Kevelioc, Earl of Chester: her father **Simon III de Montfort, Count of Évreux** was son of Simon II and **Agnes d'Évreux** — daughter of Richard, Count of Évreux, granddaughter of **Robert, Archbishop of Rouen — son of Richard I of Normandy**: the **Évreux ducal descent** (the Archbishop was brother to the Tosny's patron-duke Richard II). Her mother was a **Maud** — parentage uncertain per FMG; the Gloucester assignment in some charts is *flagged*.
- Hugh de Kevelioc's own mother was **Maud FitzRobert of Gloucester — daughter of Robert, 1st Earl of Gloucester, illegitimate son of King Henry I**: **a FIFTH documented royal line** (Henry I again, via the Gloucester earls).
- The Ridlesford in-laws: Emmeline de Ridlesford's grandfather **Walter I de Ridelesford (d. ~1200) rode in Strongbow's own 1169 expedition** — granted twenty fiefs in Kildare, built Bray Castle, garrisoned Gisors in 1173–4; his son **Walter II, Chief Baron of Leinster**, was at the Curragh in 1234. Emmeline's first husband was **Hugh de Lacy, Earl of Ulster** — another earl in the family. The de Ridelesfords' Irish barony ended with Christina de Marisco's 1290 petition to Edward I.

**The Porhoët (Breton) floor — Josselin Castle, c. 990 [A/B — Wikipedia *Porhoët*; WikiTree Porhoët-17; the 1108 Joscelin charter]:**
- **Guithenoc (c. 990–1040)**, born at Guilliers — the first recognized **Vicomte de Porhoët** — built **Josselin Castle** on the River Oust and named it for his son. The castle *remains in the family's line to this day — described as the longest continuously held private estate in the world.*
- The line: Guithenoc → **Josselin (1025–1074)** → **Odo/Eudon I (1049–1092) m. Anne de Léon** → **Geoffrey de Porhoët (1082–1141) m. Hawisa Fergant** — and Geoffrey's son, recorded in the Wikipedia article itself: *"Alan de Porhoët, younger brother of Odo II, left for England and became **Alan la Zouche**, great-grandfather of two English barons"* — **the user's direct ancestor**.
- The family's reach: a cadet branch founded the **House of Rohan** (Viscounts of Rohan — later Dukes of Montbazon and French princes); the senior line **contested the Ducal Crown of Brittany** (Odo II, regent for Conan IV, son-in-law of Duke Conan III — deposed by Henry II's invasion of 1156, which razed Josselin); and the English branch = the Barons Zouche of Ashby-de-la-Zouch — the la Zouche name is Breton: *"la Souche,"* the tree-stump.

**The Dál Cais floor — Cennétig's line, c. 896 [S/A — DIB; *Annals of Ulster*; Byrne, *Irish Kings and High-Kings*]:**
- The male line runs: **Brian Boru (d. 1014)** ← **Cennétig mac Lorcáin (b. c. 896, d. 951)** — King of Dál gCais/Thomond, styled *rígdamna Caisil* ("king-material of Cashel") — ← **Lorcáin mac Lachtna** (king of the Uí Thairdelbaig branch, fl. 934). The annals remember Cennétig losing four sons in his wars of ascent; the *Annals of Ulster* for Clontarf calls Brian *"the Augustus of the whole of north-west Europe."*
- The dynasty's own origin-tradition (the Uí Thairdelbaig claim to the ancient Déisi stock) is flagged legend — the **documented male floor stands at c. 896**, with Brian's mother Bé Binn ingen Urchadh adding the Uí Briúin Seóla of Connacht.
- Kin note: the Ó Cinnéide (O'Kennedy) surname descends from Cennétig — the Dál Cais stock is shared with the Kennedy family of later centuries [B — flagged]; Brian's third great-granddaughter was **Gwenllian ferch Gruffydd**, the Welsh patriot princess [A — flagged].

**The Beaumont–Meulan line — and the French royal house [A/S — Complete Peerage (via Sharp); the Newberry ancestry; 1911 Britannica; Geni pedigree]:**
- **Thorold/Thurolf de Pont-Audemer (b. c. 950, d. c. 979)** — the first known lord of Pont-Audemer, installed by **Bernard the Dane, Rollo's own companion**; married Duvelina des Arques. (The Harcourt family springs from the same root.)
- **Humphrey de Vieilles (b. c. 980, d. 28 Sep 1044)** — founder of the abbeys of Preaux, St. Leger, St. Acqueline — m. **Aubreye de la Haye**, heiress of the Forest of Brotonne.
- **Roger de Beaumont (b. 1010/22, d. 1094)** — Seigneur of Pont-Audemer, Beaumont-le-Roger, Brionne and Vieilles — contributed **60 vessels to Duke William's invasion fleet** — m. **Adeline/Avelina de Meulan**, daughter & heiress of **Waleran III, Count of Meulan** (the Meulan counts join the web).
- **Robert de Beaumont, 1st Earl of Leicester (1046–1118)** — **co-Justiciar of England with Richard de Lucy** (the family's *fourth* Chief Justiciar; the two men governed England together under the Conqueror) — m. **Isabel de Vermandois, granddaughter of King Henry I of France** (daughter of Hugh Magnus, Duke of France, and Adelaide of Vermandois): **the Capetian royal line of France joins the documented descent** (Hugh Capet → Robert II → Henry I → Hugh Magnus → Isabel → the Leicester earls → user).
- **⚡ The double-Beaumont convergence:** Isabel de Beaumont (Robert's daughter) married **Gilbert de Clare, 1st Earl of Pembroke — she was STRONGBOW'S OWN MOTHER**; and Margaret de Beaumont married Ralph de Tosny V — **Ida's line**. The house of Leicester thus enters the family twice — and Ida de Tosny descends from it on both sides.

**The de Vere wall — honest and low [A — EB1911; Wikipedia/FMG; Keats-Rohan]:** Aubrey I de Vere (d. 1112), Domesday chamberlain and tenant-in-chief, came from **Ver, near Bayeux** — part of the ducal demesne included in Duchess Judith's dowry of 1026 and granted to the family within thirty years ("Aubri de Ver occurs in 1058"). **His origins are obscure — the late-medieval "Alphonsus" father and the claims of descent from the Conqueror's sister are rejected as pedigree-building** (Keats-Rohan). The documented de Vere wall stands at c. 1058, with the family itself later Earls of Oxford (1142–1703) and hereditary Great Chamberlains — Robert de Vere, 3rd Earl, was one of the twenty-five Magna Carta sureties.

**Three more documented floors — Meulan (947), Gournay (989–996), Senlis (c. 1030):**

- **MEULAN [S/A — FMG *MedLands*; the De Gruyter Brill study of Waleran I]:** the county begins with **Liegardis, Countess of Meulan and Mantes** — a charter of **October 947** ("Liutgarde, widow of Count Raoul") — and **Waleran I de Chartres**, her second husband; their line runs Galeran I (d. before 985/87) → Galeran II → Hugues I (d. after 25 Aug 1005) → **Galeran III (d. 8 Dec 1069)**, whose daughter **Adeline married Roger de Beaumont** and whose son Hugh's death (1081) passed Meulan to the Beaumont earls — the user's line.
- **GOURNAY [A — *Three Hundred Years of a Norman House* (1867), from the family charters]:** *"Eudes, the follower of Rollo"* and *"Hugh, the fortifier of Gournay"* are tradition; the **first lords known by charter are RENAUD & ALBERARDA — a charter of 989–996, contemporaries of Richard the Fearless.** The line: Renaud → Hugh I → **Hugh II "Old Hugh" (1036–1074)** → **Hugh III m. Basilia Flaitel** (widow of Raoul de Gacé — son of Robert, Count of Évreux and Archbishop of Rouen: the ducal connection again) → **Gerard de Gournay, the Crusader (d. c. 1104), who married Edith de Warenne** — daughter of **William de Warenne, 1st Earl of Surrey** (companion of the Conqueror, d. of wounds 1088) and Gundrada. **⚠ CORRECTION (kept in the file's spirit of honesty):** the old claim that Gundrada was a daughter of William the Conqueror — inscribed even on her 1845-rediscovered lead coffin at Lewes — is **refuted** (Stapleton 1846; Freeman, *English Historical Review* 1888; modern consensus): the Lewes Priory assertions are self-interested monastic fabrications, and Gundrada was **Flemish — sister of Gerbod the Fleming**, Earl of Chester 1070–71. The Conqueror-descent through this line is therefore **not established** and the earlier "third descent" note is struck; the Warenne kinship stands through the baronial line alone. Gerard's English barony (Caistor caput) rooted the Gournays of the West; the **Gurney family of Norfolk descends from this house to this day.** The line runs to the barons of Beverston → **Milicent de Gournay, Countess of Évreux** (wife of William de Cantelowe) → Nichole → Maud de Lucy → the Segraves → user.
- **SENLIS / ST LIZ [A — Wikipedia; WikiTree citing Anselme, Cawley's FMG, and ODNB]:** **Simon I de Senlis, Earl of Northampton and Huntingdon (d. La Charité-sur-Loire, 1111×1113)** — builder of Northampton Castle and the surviving round church of the Holy Sepulchre; witness to Henry I's coronation charter (1100); crusader; prisoner of Louis of France in 1098. His parentage carries an honest flag: son of **Laudri de Senlis, sire of Chantilly and Ermenonville** (Anselme; Wikipedia) *or* of **"Ranulf/Randel le Riche"** (ODNB; Cawley's recent research) — possibly the same man under variant names. The DNB legend — offered the widowed Judith of Lens, refused *"on account of his lameness,"* married instead her daughter **Maud of Huntingdon** — is preserved as story. Maud de Senlis married (1) Robert Fitz Richard of Little Dunmow (the de Clares) and (2) **Saer de Quincy — the user's direct line.**

**The Irish king-list floor — Crimthann mac Énnai (d. 483) [B/NA — the Book of Leinster king-list (*Ríg Hua Cendselaig*); FMG Ireland]:**
- The Uí Cheinnselaig royal genealogy — preserved in the Book of Leinster from genuinely older material — names the user's male-line kings in an unbroken list: **Crimthann mac Énnai (d. 483) → Brandub mac Echach (d. 605, the famous king) → the seventh- and eighth-century kings (Cú Chongelt d. 724; Sechnassach d. 746) → Cellach Tosach (d. 809) → Cairpre mac Diarmata (d. 876) → Fáelán (d. 894) → Tadg mac Fáeláin (d. 922) → Cináed (d. 935) → Cellach mac Cináeda (d. 947) → Donnchad mac Taidg (d. 965) → Domnall mac Cellaig (d. 974 — the annal-documented wall) → … → Murchad mac Diarmata (d. 1070) → Donnchad (d. 1115) → Diarmait (d. 1171) → Aoife.**
- **Honest grading:** the seventh century onward is annal-documented; the fifth-to-sixth-century names (Crimthann's generation) are semi-legendary, carried by the medieval king-list tradition. The dynasty's continuity is real long after Aoife: Diarmait's son Domhnall Caomhánach (d. 1175) founded the **Caomhánach (Kavanagh)** line, **Kings of Leinster in name into the fifteenth century and chiefs claiming the kingship into the modern era.**

**The Bessin viscounts mechanized — with a ducal marriage at the root [A/B — the Register of Wetheral Priory; Orderic Vitalis; the Durham *Liber Vitae*; FMG]:**
- **Anschitil, Viscount of the Bessin — married a daughter of Duke Richard III of Normandy** (the Conqueror's first cousin) — the ducal blood in the Bessin viscountal house, documented via the chronicle tradition.
- His line: Anschitil → **Ranulf I, Viscount of the Bessin m. Alix of Normandy** → **Ranulf de Briquessart, Viscount of the Bessin m. Margaret d'Avranches (sister of Hugh d'Avranches "Lupus," Earl of Chester — the Goz kin)** → **Ranulf le Meschin, 1st Earl of Chester of the second creation (d. 17/27 Jan 1129), founder of Wetheral Priory** — commander of the royal army in Normandy 1124; his earldom came when his cousin Richard d'Avranches drowned in the **White Ship (1120)**. His wife Lucy of Bolingbroke (heiress of the Spalding/Taillebois/Roumare lands — her own parentage per Keats-Rohan's *Prosopon* study) carried the Lincolnshire inheritance; his sister Agnes married Robert de Grantmesnil (the family of Pernelle de Grantmesnil, wife of the 3rd Earl of Leicester — yet another convergence).
- This root now explains the family's **double Meschine descent**: the line surfaces in the la Zouche branch through **Alice de Belmeis** and in the Ferrers branch through **Agnes of Chester** — two branches of the same Bessin viscountal tree, both documented to Anschitil.
- Flag: Ranulf le Meschin's first marriage to **Maud de Vere** (daughter of Aubrey de Vere) appears in the compiled pedigrees — the de Vere connection recurring; second marriage Lucy. Logged.

**The Goz wall — the Chester line's documented floor [A/S — DNB; Complete Peerage III:165; the Hiémois charter tradition]:**
- **Hugh d'Avranches "Lupus"** (c. 1047–1101), Earl of Chester — was son of **Richard le Goz, Vicomte d'Avranches** (d. 1082; the father who contributed 60 ships to the invasion fleet) — son of **Turstin Goz, Viscount of the Hiémois** (the rebel against the boy-duke William in 1040, reconciled by his son's loyalty) — son of **Amfrid/Ansfrid the Dane** (first Viscount of the Hiémois, d. c. 970). **The documented Goz floor stands at Ansfrid the Dane, c. 970** — with the pedigree tradition ("Hrolf Turstain," fl. early 900s) flagged beyond.
- Hugh's own maternity is honestly unknown: the old claim that his mother was **Emma de Conteville, half-sister of William the Conqueror, is rejected** (C. P. Lewis, ODNB: "unsatisfactory evidence"; David Douglas: "no evidence that such a person ever existed"); the St Werburgh manuscript's "son of a Duke of Brittany and nephew of the Great William" is adjudged romanticized. Hugh's wife was **Ermentrude de Clermont** (daughter of Hugh, Count of Clermont-en-Beauvaisis, and Margaret de Roucy — the Roucy-Montdidier counts join the web).
- **The Bigod conjecture re-stated at its root:** Keats-Rohan's hypothesis makes Robert le Bigod (Roger Bigod's father) *"a junior branch of the Goz family descended from Turstin I's son Robert"* — i.e., **if** the conjecture holds, the Surety Bigods and the user's Chester (Goz) line share **Turstin Goz** as common ancestor — kinsmen through the same vicomtal house. Flagged, not adopted.
- The Chester succession: Hugh Lupus → his son **Richard d'Avranches (drowned in the White Ship, 25 Nov 1120)** → his cousin **Ranulf le Meschin** — the user's line.

**The Warenne wall — documented to the 1020s [A/S — Loyd, *Yorkshire Archaeological Journal* 31; Keats-Rohan NMS 37; C. P. Lewis, ODNB]:**
- Ralph de Warenne appears in charters from **1027–1035** (with wife Beatrice; the 1059 charter names Emma as mother of William — Keats-Rohan's two-generation reconstruction resolving the conflict) — from the **hamlet of Varenne**, Seine-Inférieure. William de Warenne, the younger son (b. ~1035, d. of wounds 24 Jun 1088, first Earl of Surrey), became the Conqueror's kinsman by marriage — Gundrada the Fleming — and one of the greatest magnates of the realm. The possible Gunnora-kin connection (a niece of Duchess Gunnor's family — Keats-Rohan's cautious identification of Beatrice as a daughter of Tesselin, vicomte of Rouen) is flagged, not adopted.

**The Montfort line — a Capetian grantee of c. 990 [S/A — Britannica; Wikipedia *Amaury I*, *Lords of Montfort-l'Amaury*; the armorial genealogy]:**
- **Guillaume de Montfort, of Hainaut** — the first lord — **grandson of Regnier II, Count of Hainaut, cousin of Regnier IV of Mons** — came to France after Regnier III's rebellions, and was **invested with the lordship by King Hugh Capet** (the castle built 996, for Robert the Pious, against the counts of Blois); by 1003 he had married an heiress of Montfort. The Hainaut line — the House of Reginar, ancestors of the House of Brabant — carries a *possible* Carolingian-Lotharingian descent (flagged as tradition).
- His son **Amaury I (c. 1003 – d. c. 1053)** — the town's namesake — m. **Bertrade de Gometz**; the family's own medieval claim of descent from **Judith, daughter of Charles the Bald** (through Baldwin Bras-de-Fer of Flanders) is flagged as origin-legend.
- The descent runs through Simon I (m. Agnes d'Évreux — daughter of the Count of Évreux) to **Simon III, Count of Évreux**, father of **Bertrade de Montfort** (wife of Hugh de Kevelioc — the user's line).
- Kin notes: the Montforts intermarried the web repeatedly — Amaury III's daughter Agnes married **Waleran IV de Beaumont, Count of Meulan** (the user's Beaumont family); the family's later fame — **Simon IV de Montfort, the Albigensian crusader**, and Simon V, founder-figure of the English parliament — and the Breton Montfort dukes (1365–1514, through Yolande) ending in **Anne of Brittany, twice Queen of France**.

**The Valois–Vexin line — Carolingian at the root, and a hero of the chansons [S/A — Wikipedia *Counts and dukes of Valois*; FMG *MedLands*; Flodoard]:**
- The Valois counts of the Carolingian age were **Pepin I and Pepin II, Counts of Vermandois and Valois — sons of Bernard, King of Italy, grandson of Charlemagne** — and **Herbert I of Vermandois (893–895), Bernard's other son** — *the very line of Richardson's Charlemagne Line A.* The later counts — Ermenfroi (895–919), Ralph I of Ostrevent (915–926) — are of "disputed origin" per the standard lists.
- **Ralph II de Cambrai (926–943, killed 944)** — **Liegardis of Meulan's FIRST husband** — is the historical original of the epic hero of the *Chanson de Geste* *Raoul de Cambrai*; Flodoard records his death invading Vermandois, and Alberic of Trois-Fontaines calls him *nepos ex sorore* of King Louis IV — kin to the Carolingian royal house (flagged, source unidentified). His line passes to Walter I, Walter II the White, and Ralph III/IV of Valois — whose daughter **Adele of Valois married into the Meulan line**, closing the loop: *Liegardis's two marriages bound Valois, Vexin, and Meulan into one inheritance that descended to the Beaumonts — and to the user.*

**The Giroie–Grantmesnil block — Orderic Vitalis's greatest story, in the direct line [S — FMG *MedLands* quoting Orderic Vitalis and Guillaume de Jumièges]:**
- **Giroie, Lord of Échauffour (d. before 1035) m. Gisla, daughter of Thurstan de Bastembourg** — *the Bastembourg marriage*: the same Bessin-vicomtal clan from which the Chester Meschines descend (§8.4, Bessin), intersecting the family yet again.
- Their daughter **Hawise Giroie** married (1) **Robert de Grantmesnil** (killed in battle 17 June 1040, in the great feud their family waged with the Bellême/Talvas clan) — children: **HUGH DE GRANDMESNIL (1032–1094), commander at Hastings, Domesday tenant-in-chief (100 manors, 65 in Leicestershire), governor of Leicester and sheriff of two counties** — and (2) **Guillaume d'Évreux, son of Archbishop Robert (the Évreux ducal line)** — their daughter **Judith married Roger, Count of Sicily** (the Hauteville).
- The line runs Hugh de Grandmesnil (d. 1094) → his son Robert → his son **William** (the name given by Pernel's own charter; the Leicester Abbey narrative's "Hugh" is rejected by the Complete Peerage as fictitious) → **Pernel/Petronilla de Grandmesnil (d. 1212), heiress of the Norman honour of Grandmesnil, wife of Robert de Beaumont, 3rd Earl of Leicester** → their daughter **Margaret of Leicester m. SAHER DE QUINCY, the Magna Carta Surety** — the user's line. *(Correction kept in the file: the user descends from Hawise Giroie's FIRST marriage — the Grantmesnils — not the Évreux second marriage.)*
- **The story inside the story:** the same feud that killed Robert de Grantmesnil also produced the blinding and mutilation of his brother-in-law **William fitz Giroie** at the wedding of William I Talvas, and the poisoning of **Arnold d'Échauffour** by **Mabilia** (wife of Roger de Montgomery) — and, at the feud's origin, the **assassination of Gilbert "Crispin," Count of Brionne — the user's OTHER direct ancestor** (§8.4, de Clare), whose orphaned sons fled to Flanders. The user's two Norman houses stood on opposite sides of the generation of knives: the Giroies feuded for the Crispins' blood, and their descendant married the Surety.

**The Giffard block — Gunnor's own kin [A/B — Wikipedia; WikiTree Bolebec-30 quoting *NEHGR* 75 and the Staffordshire Collections]:**
- **Osbern de Bolebec, Lord of Longueville m. a sister of Gunnor, Duchess of Normandy** (Wevia/Avelina — the Crépon sisters, daughters of the forester of Saint-Vaast-d'Équiqueville): by this marriage the Giffards were *"first cousin twice removed of William the Conqueror"* (Wikipedia). *(The two-generation reconstruction and the Wevia/Avelina/Duvelina sister-assignment debate are flagged per the WikiTree/NEHGR analysis.)*
- **Walter Giffard** — **thirty ships** at the invasion; offered the honor of carrying Duke William's standard at Hastings (declined — an old warrior who wanted both hands free); barony of Long Crendon (107 manors); **a Domesday commissioner**; founder of St Michel de Bolbec priory (1079) — m. **Ermengarde, daughter of Gerard Flaitel**.
- Their daughter **ROHESE GIFFARD married Richard fitz Gilbert of Clare — the mother of the line that produced Strongbow** (§8.4, de Clare): so the user descends from the Giffards through the Clare marriage, and the Giffard kin-of-the-duke connection runs in the family through Gunnor's sisters.

**The Breton ducal connection — Hawise Fergant [S — the UMich genealogy (S-grade); Wikipedia *Porhoët*; the Morice charters]:**
- **Hawise "Fergant"** — wife of **Geoffrey de Porhoët** and mother of **Alan la Zouche** — was **daughter of ALAN IV "FERGANT," Duke of Brittany (d. 1119) and ERMENGARDE OF ANJOU** (daughter of Fulk IV, Count of Anjou). **Your direct ancestress was a daughter of the Duke of Brittany** — and by her mother, of the house of Anjou, the Plantagenets' own ancestral dynasty. Alan IV himself took the Cross and went on the First Crusade; the Breton ducal house (Cornouaille) and the vicomtal Porhoët intermarried into the same blood that crossed to England as the la Zouches.
- The family's Breton orbit also produced the era's most lurid scandal: **Eudo II de Porhoët claimed in 1168 that Henry II had impregnated his daughter Alix 'treacherously, adulterously and incestuously; for the king and Eudo's wife were the offspring of two sisters'** — Alix de Porhoët was Henry II's mistress (logged as contemporary allegation, Given-Wilson & Curteis).
- Odo I's wife, **Anne/Emma de Léon** (daughter of Guihomar II, Viscount of Léon) was buried at the Josselin priory in 1092 — her funeral witnessed by three bishops and a gathering of barons, per the Morice charters.

**The Flaitel line — seneschals of the ducal court [S — the Winchester PhD thesis on the Giffards; the St Wandrille pancarte]:**
- **Gerard Flaitel (d. c. 1047)** — in the court circle of Duke Richard II and Duke Robert I — his daughter **Ermengarde married Walter Giffard** (the standard-refuser, §8.4-Giffard). His family made gifts to St Wandrille in the 1082×1087 pancarte alongside Walter's — the churches of Chambois, Courbépine, Betthonis, Longueil — with his sons **William (Bishop of Évreux), Anscherius, Robert and Albertus** named. The documented Flaitel floor stands at Gerard, c. 1047.

**The Clermont–Roucy block — and another Capetian doorway [S/A — Complete Peerage III:165; FMG; Richardson RA]:**
- **Hugues de Clermont (1030–1101)**, seigneur of Clermont, Creil, Gournay, Luzarches, Mouchy and Breuil-le-Vert — son of **Renaud de Creil (d. 1088), Great Chamberlain of France** — married **Marguerite de Ramerupt (de Roucy), daughter of Hilduin IV, Count of Roucy and Montdidier (d. 1063), and Adelaide de Roucy** (daughter of Ebles I, Count of Reims and Roucy, and **Beatrice of Hainaut — daughter of Regnier IV, Count of Hainaut, and Hedwige Capet, daughter of King Hugh Capet: the French royal line enters yet again**, through the same Reginar-Hainaut house as the Montforts).
- The sister-marriages: **Ermentrude de Clermont married Hugh Lupus, Earl of Chester** (the user's Chester line); her sister **Adeliza de Clermont married Gilbert fitz Richard de Clare** (the user's de Clare line). Both of the user's great Norman houses married daughters of the same Clermont–Roucy couple.
- The Roucy counts themselves (Hilduin II, III, IV) are documented to the mid-900s; the deeper Carolingian claims attached to the house are flagged as the family's own tradition.

**The Morville block — the constables of Scotland [S/A — ODNB; Weis AR 38-25; Turton]:**
- The family takes its name from **Morville, in the Cotentin**. **Hugh de Morville (d. 1162), hereditary Constable of Scotland** — founder of **Dryburgh Abbey** (1150) and Kilwinning — married **Beatrice de Beauchamp**; their son **Richard de Morville (d. 1189), Constable of Scotland 1162**, married **Avice/Hawise de Lancaster** (daughter of William I de Lancaster, Baron of Kendal — the Taillebois line — and Gundred de Warenne, the Fleming's granddaughter). Their daughter **ELENA DE MORVILLE (1166–1217), sole heir, married Roland (Lochlann) of Galloway (d. 1200)** — son of Uchtred of Galloway and Gunnild of Dunbar — **the user's Galloway line; the hereditary constableship passed with her.** The family's dark footnote, honestly separated: the Westmorland Morvilles (Hugh of Knaresborough) were among **Thomas Becket's killers** (1170) — kinsmen, not the direct branch.
- The Complete Peerage's Chester note adds that the first earl's hereditary barons included the constable/marshal line "whose descendants took the name of De Lacy" — the Lacy thread at the Chester root.

**The Lucy–Taillebois block — Keats-Rohan's solution, and two rejected pedigrees [S — Keats-Rohan, *Antecessor Noster* (Prosopon 2, 1995); CP; the Croyland-forgery scholarship]:**
- **Lucy of Bolingbroke** — wife in turn of Ivo Taillebois, Roger fitz Gerold, and **Ranulf le Meschin, Earl of Chester** (the user's line, mother of Ranulf de Gernon) — was, per Katherine Keats-Rohan's definitive study, **daughter of Thorold (Turold), the English sheriff of Lincoln holding before the Conquest, and a daughter of William Malet** — the Surety's family intersecting yet again.
- **Two rejected claims, kept in the file as warnings:** the story that Lucy was a daughter of **Ælfgar, Earl of Mercia** (sister of Edwin and Morcar) derives from the **Croyland Chronicle's first section — 'now known to be a later forgery from the 14th century'** — and the Coventry *genealogia fundatoris*, 'a dubious document'; the Peterborough version shares the contamination. Peter of Blois's curse on Ivo Taillebois ("the accursed lineage of that wicked man perished by the axe of the Almighty") is preserved as the chronicler's venom, not genealogy.
- Lucy's daughter **Beatrice** m. Ribald of Middleham (half-brother of Alan Rufus) — the Richmond honour kin.

**THE DEEPEST FLAGGED LAYER — "Descent from Antiquity" (the Kelley–Settipani reconstruction) [S — Don Stone's soc.genealogy.medieval chart; Kelley, *NEHGR* 101 (1947); Settipani, *Les Ancêtres de Charlemagne* (1989); the consensus: "no Descent From Antiquity is accepted as established at this time"]:**
- *Plausible but unproven:* the Carolingian line may continue — Flavius Afranius Syagrius (fl. 345–382; Praefectus Urbi of Rome 381; **Consul 382**; Praetorian Prefect of Italy; buried at Lyons beneath a monument "not quite a full bowshot" from the church) → his daughter m. Ferreolus → **Tonantius Ferreolus (b. ~405, d. after 469), Praetorian Prefect of Gaul from 451** — the senator whose diplomacy helped break Attila's siege of Arles after the Catalaunian Plains — m. **Papianilla, niece of Emperor Avitus** (the general-emperor who had helped defeat Attila) and cousin to the other Papianilla, wife of **Sidonius Apollinaris**, last of the Roman poets of Gaul — → Tonantius the younger → Ferreolus of Narbonne (m. St Dode of Rheims) → Ansbertus → [the disputed Merovingian link] → Arnulf's forebears.
- **The weak links are named, not hidden:** generations 6–8 rest on the same spurious *Genealogia Karolorum* that modern historians dismiss (Schieffer); Moriarty called the Syagrius line "very problematical"; the Arnoald/Blithilde claim is dismissed; Arnulf's father was probably Bodegisel — Settipani's own alternative runs through Mummolin of Neustria and Mundéric. **The deepest honest statement: the family plausibly descends, by a reconstruction no authority accepts as proven, from the Roman senatorial consuls of Gaul in the 4th–5th centuries — the age of Sidonius, Avitus, and the last days of Roman rule.**

**THE ORIGINS TABLE — where every line ends:**
| Line | Documented floor | Hypothetical (flagged) | Legendary (rejected) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Carolingian/Frankish | c. 580 (St Arnulf, St Pepin of Landen) | Syagrius the Consul, 382 (Settipani/Kelley) | Merovingian blood (dismissed); **Francio & King Priam of Troy** (Fredegar, c. 660) |
| West Saxon | 718 (Ingild) · 784 (Ealhmund) | — | Cerdic (semi, d. 534) → **Woden → Geat → Noah → Adam** (ASC myth) |
| Northumbrian | 913 (Eadwulf I) | King Ælla's line (dynastic claim, flagged) | **Ragnar Lodbrok** (saga) |
| Irish | 896 (Cennétig) · 974 (Domnall) · 700s (annals) | Crimthann's dynasty (graded 483) | the **Milesians of Spain** (Lebor Gabála); Oengus Turbech |
| Breton | c. 990 (Guithenoc of Josselin) | — | **Conan Meriadoc** (the Trojan-Welsh founder-legend) |
| Norman | 918 (Rollo) | Rognvald of Møre (conjecture) | **Odin & the Æsir**; Fornjot (Snorri's euhemerized "Turks of Troy") |
| Danish | Siward's father Bjorn (shadowy) | Danish royal kin (Bolton's hypothesis, flagged) | the **white-bear descent** (Vita Waldevi folklore) |

*The medieval world kit-bashed every nation out of Troy — Fredegar's Franks under Priam and Francio, Brutus for Britain, the Milesians for Ireland, Snorri's Norse gods as Trojan refugees — and the modern scholarly verdict on the whole apparatus is the one this file has carried since its first page: legend, faithfully recorded, never adopted.*

**The Reginarid (Hainaut) line — probable Carolingian, honestly unconfirmed [A/S — Wikipedia; FMG *MedLands*; the Taylor & Francis Lotharingian genealogies]:**
- The Montforts' Hainaut line runs to **Reginar I "Longneck" (c. 850 – d. Meerssen, between 25 Aug 915 and 19 Jan 916)**, head of the Reginarids — **"probably" the son of Gilbert (Giselbert), Count of the Maasgau, and an unnamed daughter of Emperor Lothair I** — a marriage born of Gilbert's *abduction* of the princess in 846 (reconciled to Lothair in 848, *Annales Fuldenses*). **The honest flags, verbatim from the sources:** the mother's name is unknown (Hiltrude, Bertha, Irmgard, Gisela are candidates — none verified); *"the primary source which confirms his parentage has not yet been identified"* (FMG); the *Annales Hanoniæ*'s pedigree passage "must be incorrect"; the Carolingian tie, if real, would give the Montforts (and the user) **a fifth Carolingian descent — probable but unconfirmed.**
- His career is fully documented: lay abbot of Echternach (897–915), Maastricht, Stablo-Malmedy; **fought ROLLO — the user's other deep ancestor — beside Radbod of Frisia** (Guillaume de Jumièges: *"Rainier au long cou duc de Hasbaigne et du Hainaut et Radbold prince de Frise"* against the Viking); regent of the kingdom in the 877 Capitulary of Quierzy; broke with Zwentibold; led the magnates who elected Charles the Simple. His descendants — Reginar II (Count of Hainaut 924–931) → Reginar III → **Reginar IV (d. 1013) m. Hedwig, daughter of King Hugh Capet** (the Roucy/Capetian line of the Clermont block) → the Counts of Hainaut, Leuven (Lambert I, d. 1015, founder of the House of Leuven/Brabant) and the Montforts' own line.
- *Note the web's ironies: the user's Hainaut ancestors fought the user's Norman founder; the user's Porhoët kin feuded the Plantagenets; the family's medieval lines fought each other for three centuries.*

**The Angevin (Ingelgerian) line — the Plantagenets' own root [A — Wikipedia; the academic study of Angevin historiography; Bachrach, *Fulk Nerra*]:**
- **Ingelger (d. 886/898)** — "the first documented member of the Angevins" — a ninth-century charter names him father of Fulk the Red; a *"semi-legendary soldier of fortune who carved out an estate for himself in the Loire valley"*; viscount of Orléans, prefect of Tours; m. Adelais of the lords of Amboise (whose uncles were the archbishop of Tours and the bishop of Angers). His putative father **Tertullus** — and Tertullus's father **Tortulfus** — rest on the debated *Gesta Consulum* material: *"No dates have ever been attested."*
- **The most honest sentence in the medieval record:** Count **Fulk Rechin (d. 1109)**, writing his family's history in 1096, admitted he could record the deeds *"neither of Ingelger nor of Fulk the Red nor of Fulk the Good — because they lived so long ago that we do not even know where they are buried."* His own mythology — a descendant of Charlemagne bestowing the county, the counts seizing Anjou "from the hands of pagans" — is flagged as the family's invention.
- The line, once solid: Ingelger → **Fulk I the Red (first Count of Anjou, 898–941)** → Fulk II the Good → Geoffrey I Greymantle → Fulk III "Nerra" the Black (987–1040) → Geoffrey II Martel → [cognatic] → **Fulk IV the Ill-Tempered (1067–1109) m. Bertrade de Montfort** → **Fulk V, King of Jerusalem** & **Ermengarde of Anjou (m. Alan IV of Brittany — Hawise Fergant's parents, the user's direct line)** → Geoffrey V Plantagenet → Henry II. **The user descends from the sister of the King of Jerusalem and the aunt of the Plantagenet founder.**

**The Cogswell block — documented to ~1484, and a first-cousin marriage resolved [A/B — WikiTree Cogswell-72/73 citing the Great Migration and Jameson's *Cogswells in America* (1884)]:**
- **Margaret Cogswell** (wife of Moses Hawke, 1659) was the daughter of **Deacon William Cogswell (b. Feb 1619/20, Westbury Leigh, Wilts – d. 15 Dec 1700, Chebacco/Ipswich)** — who married, at Lynn in 1649/50, **SUSANNA HAWKES — daughter of Adam Hawkes himself.** **The flagged question resolves as a first-cousin marriage: Moses Hawke (son of John Hawkes & Rebecca Maverick) married Margaret Cogswell (daughter of his aunt Susanna Hawkes & William Cogswell) — both grandchildren of Adam Hawkes.** William Cogswell is also the documented ancestor of **President John Adams** (and of Diana, Princess of Wales, per the earlier Cogswell finding).
- The Cogswell English line: John Cogswell (1592–1669; the *Angel Gabriel* wreck survivor; "The Mylls called Rippond," Frome Selwood — Cogswell-owned cloth mills still operating in 1884) ← **Edward Cogswell** ← **Robert Cogswell (b. 30 Nov 1510 – d. 7 Jun 1581), woolen-cloth manufacturer** ← the Leigh, Wiltshire family documented to **~1484**. John's wife was **Elizabeth Thompson**, daughter of Rev. William Thompson, vicar of Westbury, & Phillis Rycharde Lychston.
- Family color: the 1675–77 suit *Cogswell v. Cogswell* (nephew v. uncle over the sea-drowned brother's estate — nephew lost, paid the costs); five Cogswells among the twenty signers of the Rev. John Wise petition for Goodwife Proctor at the Salem witch trials; the family coat of arms "wrought most exquisitely with silk on heavy satin," lent to a stranger in the 1880s and never returned.

**The Maverick block — three generations of clergy, documented [S/A — NEHGR 69 (1915) Elizabeth French; the ordination books; *English Origins of the Mary & John Passengers*]:**
- **Robert Maverick, clerk, of Awliscombe (d. 1573)** — described in the parish records as a "clerk" (cleric); his predecessor in the Awliscombe church was installed in 1554; of yeoman stock, origins unknown.
- His son **Rev. Peter Maverick *alias Bull*** (b. ~1550; ordained deacon in the Bishop's Palace chapel, Exeter, 1573/4; instituted vicar of Awliscombe 1580; d. a violent death c. 1616) — the alias never explained; the best surmise is that Peter was a step-son who bore his step-father's name. Married **Dorothy Tucke** (daughter of tenants of the Mayor of Exeter, 1577).
- Their son **Rev. John Maverick (1578–1636)** — the Dorchester founder. Brothers: **Rev. Radford Maverick** (b. 1561; vicar of Ilsington — the guardian who raised the orphaned **Mary Gye** and performed her marriage to his nephew John in 1600) and Alexander, Nathaniel, et al.
- **Mary Gye's gateway status [S — Richardson MCA; the WikiTree Magna Carta Project]:** Mary Gye is a documented **Gateway Ancestor** with a **badged Richardson trail to Magna Carta Surety Roger le Bigod** (MCA II:325–326), further trails to sureties **Saher de Quincy and William Malet**, and accepted royal lines to **Henry I (including via Henry II)** — the Henry III line remains broken (the Echyngham/Baynton generation). Her 1508-IPM forebears **John Guy & Elizabeth Spencer** (the Spencer-of-Devon link) remain flagged "proof lacking."
- **Kin notes:** through her Stukeley kin, Mary Gye was connected to **Sir Lewis Stukeley**, who married **Frances Monck — aunt of George Monck, Duke of Albemarle**, the kingmaker of the Restoration (her son Samuel's later position as royal commissioner plausibly ran through this kinship — flagged); and a later Maverick kinsman, **Samuel Maverick (1753–1770) of Boston, was one of the five victims of the Boston Massacre**.

**The Cushman block — documented to 1475, and a Flemish origin [PR/A — NEHGR 68:181 (French); PGM; WikiTree Cushman-136; the 2018 *Mayflower Descendant* household-inventory article; Thomas Fuller's *Church History*]:**
- **Robert Cushman (bap. 9 Feb 1577/8, Rolvenden, Kent — mis-transcribed in the register as "Robert Dutcheman" – d. 1625, London)** — the **Chief Agent of the Plymouth Pilgrims (1617–1625)**, the organizer of the Mayflower voyage itself: he and John Carver negotiated the patent, and when the *Speedwell* failed he stayed behind and sent the Mayflower on; he crossed instead on the ***Fortune* (November 1621)** with his son Thomas, **preached the famous sermon of 9 December 1621 — "The Sin and Danger of Self-Love and the Sweetness of True Friendship"** — obtained the colonists' signatures on the articles they had refused the year before, and returned to England 13 December, dying in 1625 before he could make Plymouth his permanent home. The memorial on Burial Hill, Plymouth, records it all. His wife **Sara Reder/Reader** (married 31 July 1606 at St. Alphege, Canterbury — "dwelling within the precincts of Christ Church," the cathedral close); his step-brother was **Joseph Tilden** (through his father's marriage to Ellen Cowchman Evernden).
- The English line: Robert ← **Thomas Couchman (1538 – d. by 14 Feb 1585/6, Rolvenden) m. Elinor Hubbard (1568)** — his **household inventory published in the *Mayflower Descendant* (2018)** ← **Thomas Croucheman (bap. 9 Mar 1512, Hawkhurst – d. 14 Jun 1567)** ← the Rolvenden line documented to **1475**.
- **The name and the origin tradition:** per Fuller's *Church History*, the Couchmans were **cloth-makers, Flemish in origin, who came to Kent at the invitation of King Edward II, c. 1336** — the Flemish weaving migration. (Flagged as the Fuller tradition; the documented wall stands at 1475.)
- Robert Cushman's line continues: son **Elder Thomas Cushman (1607/8–1691)** — Bradford's ward; ruling elder 1649–1691; m. **Mary Allerton** (the last Mayflower survivor) — the user's Allerton-granddaughter line through Sarah Cushman & John Hawkes.

**The Townsend note — the Lynn family [A/B — the 1887 chart; *Genealogy of the Townsend Family*]:** **Susannah Townsend**, wife of **John Hawkes (b. 1704/5, Lynn – d. 1748)** — son of Moses Hawke (1659–1709) & Margaret Cogswell per the sourced reconstruction (the 1887 chart's intermediate generation slightly conflated; the Mayflower Society's line runs instead through **Moses (1699–1760) m. Susannah Hichins** — both flagged). The Lynn Townsends descend from **Thomas Townsend, who came to Lynn in 1634**.

**The confirmation from the Great Migration-based profile [B — WikiTree Hawkes-406, citing the Mayflower Index and *Hawkes Talks* vols. 5 & 11]:** *"When he married, in both instances he chose brides who were descendants of Mayflower passengers… thus all descendants of John Hawkes by either his first or second marriage may claim Mayflower ancestry. Those descended through the first wife Rebecca Maverick ALSO have the lineage through Mary Gye… traced directly back to two Magna Charta Barons — (1) the Baron William Malet and (2) Baron Saher De Quincy. Still earlier generations include King Henry the First of England, William the Conqueror, and Charlemagne."* — the file's own conclusions, independently stated by the family's own researchers, citing the same Weis *Ancestral Roots* pedigrees.

**The Brewster block — documented floor: William Brewster b. ~1505, Bentley cum Arksey, Yorkshire [A/B — Mayflower Society profile; WikiTree Brewster-131/4 citing the MHS *Proceedings* and Hunter's *Collections*]:**
- Elder **William Brewster (b. ~1566, Scrooby – d. 10 Apr 1644, Plymouth)**, the only college-educated Pilgrim (Peterhouse, matriculated 3 Dec 1580), Secretary to Davison, postmaster of Scrooby, ruling Elder — son of **William Brewster (b. ~1535, Doncaster – d. summer 1590)**, whom **Archbishop Grindal appointed bailiff and receiver of Scrooby Manor for life on 4 Jan 1575/6** — *"our trusty and well-beloved servant"* — postmaster of the royal post on the Great North Road; m. (1) **Mary Smythe**, widow of John Simkinson of Doncaster, daughter of William Smythe of Stainforth (sister of John Smythe, alderman of Hull); (2) Prudence.
- The grandfather: **William Brewster (b. ~1505, Bentley cum Arksey, Yorkshire – d. bef. 1558) m. Maud Mann (1510 – aft. 11 Oct 1558)** — the documented floor. **The widely copied "Galfridus Brewster b. 1380" / "John Brewster of Rushmere, 1279" pedigrees are formally rejected as unsourced** (WikiTree's research note); the family was Yorkshire postmaster-gentry, not armigerous stock.
- Elder Brewster's wife **Mary — surname genuinely unknown**: the Wentworth theory disproved; Wyrall, Stubbe, Butler, Smith all unproven. **Fear Brewster (b. ~1606 — Isaac Allerton's second wife) was named for her great-aunt Fear Brewster**, who died unmarried c. 1608. He died at Plymouth 10 April 1644, aged ~78, leaving over 400 books.

**The Eaton block — Mayflower stock, and a Welsh claim [A — *History, Genealogical and Biographical, of the Eaton Families* (archive.org full text)]:**
- The Eaton family into which **Polly Eaton** (b. Dec 29, 1782) was born includes **FRANCIS EATON — carpenter of the Mayflower, signer of the Compact, d. 1633** — whose descendants intermarried the other Pilgrim houses: his son **Samuel Eaton m. Martha Billington** (daughter of **John Billington**, the Mayflower passenger executed for murder) and **Elizabeth Fuller** (daughter of Rev. Samuel Fuller), with **Alden** blood through other branches (*"granddaughter of John Alden and Priscilla (Mullins) Alden"*). A cousin in the family papers bore the given name **Maverick Eaton** (Revolutionary service) — the Hawkes–Maverick name floating through yet another family. Polly's own branch (the Francis Eaton line of Plymouth/Kingston vs. the John Eaton of Dedham line, *Elizabeth & Ann* 1635) is **unopened** — flagged for the future.
- The Eaton book also carries the family's claimed Welsh pedigree — **the Eytons of Shropshire, to Madog Eyton (d. 1331, buried at Gresford)**, through the Welsh genealogies of Llewelyn ap Griffri — flagged as the book's own pedigree, unverified against modern standards.

**The Breton royal line — documented to Nominoe, d. 851 [S — FMG *MedLands* citing the *Annales Bertiniani*, the Chronicle of Nantes, and the Redon annals; Britannica; Wikipedia]:**
- Hawise Fergant's line runs through the **Dukes of Brittany**: **Alan IV "Fergant" (c. 1063 – 13 Oct 1119, buried at Redon) — Duke of Brittany 1072–1112, "the last Breton-speaking Duke"** — son of **Hoel II (d. 13 Apr 1084) & Hawise of Rennes** (daughter of Alan III of Brittany & Bertha of Blois; regent for her son until 1084). Hoel II was of the **House of Cornouaille** — eldest son of **Alain Canhiart (d. 1058), Count of Cornouaille, m. Judith of Nantes.**
- Behind them: the Cornouaille counts ascend to **Alan II "Barbetorte" (d. 952)** — the restorer who expelled the Vikings (r. from 937) — grandson of **Alan I "the Great" (d. 907) — crowned KING of the Bretons at Grandmeau** (r. 888/890–907) — and to the dynasty of **NOMINOE (d. 7 March 851, buried at Redon Abbey)** — the emperor Louis the Pious's *missus imperatoris* in Brittany (appointed 831), victor over Charles the Bald at Ballon (845), founder of the Breton state and *Tad ar Vro* — "Father of the Country" — whose death the *Annales Bertiniani* record as *"Nomenogius Britto."* His son **Erispoë** (defeated Charles at Jengland, 851; murdered 857) and grandson's generation (**Salomon**, d. 874; Pasquitan & Gurvand, d. 877×888) carry the line; the Viking occupation (913–937) interrupts; the dynasty is restored and descends to the Cornouaille counts.
- **Honest flags:** the generational links inside the dynasty between Nominoe's sons' generation and Alan I have the usual early-medieval gaps (kinship asserted by the Chronicle of Nantes rather than proven charter-by-charter); Nominoe's own father, **Erispoë I, Count of Poher** (b. ~770s), is named in the chronicle tradition — flagged. The dynasty itself, however — Nominoe through Alan IV — is documentary, annal by annal.
- **The depth table gains a line: the Breton royal house now stands at 851 — the family's deepest *royal* line after the Carolingians (580), and deeper than Wessex (718), Bamburgh (913), the Dál Cais (896), or the Normans (918). The user descends from the founder of the Breton state.**

**The Tosny block — documented floor: Hugues I de Calvacamp, b. c. 890 [S/A — the *Acta Archiepiscoporum Rothomagensium*; Orderic Vitalis; the Fécamp hoard coin; the Tosarn tombstone; Complete Peerage XII/1: 754; the Pattou study (2012)]:**
- **Hugues I de Calvacamp (b. c. 890 – d. c. 980)** — the *Acta* calls the family of "illustrious stock"; the modern scholarship (Stewart, Pattou, Keats-Rohan) considers the family **French/Frankish of the Île-de-France rather than Norman** — the "Calvacamp/Calvocampo" name itself analyzed as a humble "bare field" appellation. **Orderic Vitalis's interpolation, making the Tosnys "of the lineage of Malahulc, uncle of Rollo," is flagged as 12th-century pedigree-building**, exactly as for the Allertons.
- His sons: **Hugues II (b. by 915, monk of St-Denis; Archbishop of Rouen 942–989 — a coin of the Fécamp hoard minted "in the name of Hugh the Dane," datable to the 980s, and the reused tombstone at Tosarn naming "Hugh, soldier of Richard, king of the Normans," survive him)** — and **Raoul I (b. c. 915, first Seigneur de Tosny — the archbishop's gift of the church's Tosny lands; in 991 he witnessed the accord between Duke Richard I and King Æthelred II — the first surviving international treaty in Norman history).**
- The line runs through **Roger I "the Spaniard" (d. c. 1040, four years in the Reconquista; killed by Roger de Beaumont; his widow Godehildis m. Richard, Count of Évreux) → Raoul II (d. 1102; m. Isabelle de Montfort — the "War of the Beautiful Women" against Helvisa d'Évreux) → Raoul III (m. Adelise/Alice, daughter of WALTHEOF, Earl of Northumbria — the saint's line) → Raoul IV (m. Margaret of Leicester) → Ida de Tosny (mistress of Henry II; mother of WILLIAM LONGESPÉE — the user's royal line) — and, in a collateral branch, ADELISE/ALICE DE TOSNY married ROGER BIGOD, 1st Earl of Norfolk: the user's Surety-Bigod line is kin to the Tosnys by marriage.**
- **Depth-table addition: the Tosny line stands at c. 890 — the family's second-deepest documented line after the Carolingians (580), beside Rollo (918) and the Bretons (851).**

**The Brewster–Mann refinement — Hunt's articles as the authority [PR — John G. Hunt, *TAG* 41:1–5 (1965) and *NEHGR* 124:253 (1970); the GSMD Silver Book (2014) names these "the current state of research"]:**
- **Maud Mann (b. ~1510, Doncaster)** — daughter of **John Mann**, sister of **Christopher Mann** (whose will, 13 Jan 1558, names her and is witnessed by William Brewster and John Simkinson); Alice, widow of Christopher Mann, is named in the 1558 will of **Thomas Simkinson, alderman of Doncaster**.
- The kinship **proven by a will**: John Smythe of Hull's will (8 Aug 1592) names *"my nephews William Brewster, John Smythe and Thomas Simkinson"* as executors — proving Elder Brewster's mother **Mary Smythe was the sister of the alderman of Hull**, and his half-brother **Thomas Simkinson** of Hull (to whom the Leiden Brewster gave power of attorney in 1609).
- The grandfather: **William Brewster, b. ~1505–1510** (Bentley cum Arksey / Doncaster / Hatfield variants flagged); the unsourced deeper pedigrees remain rejected. Prudence (the second wife of the Scrooby bailiff) — maiden name unproven (Peck/Perkins postulated).

**The Valois–Meulan correction — Liegardis's real family [S/A — FMG *MedLands* citing Rodulfus Glaber & her own 978 charter; Wikipedia *Ralph II of the Vexin*]:**
- **Ralph II of the Vexin (Count of the Vexin, Amiens & Valois, 926–943 — built the fortress of Crépy-en-Valois; killed 943×944 fighting the Vermandois — the historical original of the *Chanson de Raoul de Cambrai*)** — son of **Ralph I de Gouy, Count of Ostrevent (d. 926) & Eldegarde of Amiens** (daughter of Ermenfroi) — married **Liegardis**, and died without issue by her.
- **Liegardis (b. before 925 – d. 14 Nov. after 985, buried Chartres, Abbaye de Saint-Père)** — daughter of **HERBERT, COUNT OF MEULAN (Trecensis)** — her own 978 charter names *"patris mei Heirberti, Trecassini comitis."* She married secondly **THIBAUT OF BLOIS — "le Tricheur," founder of the great house of Blois** — by whom her sons **Odo, Count of Blois, and Hugh, Bishop of Auxerre**. *(Source conflict flagged: Rodulfus Glaber makes her childless by her first husband; the Meulan genealogy makes her the mother of the Meulan counts — both logged.)*
- The loop closes: the Valois counts descend from the **Carolingian Vermandois** — **Pepin I & Pepin II, Counts of Vermandois and Valois (sons of Bernard, King of Italy — Charlemagne's grandsons), and Herbert I (893–895), Bernard's other son** — the *exact* line of Richardson's Charlemagne Line A — then through Ermenfroi ("of disputed origin") to the Gouy counts (Ralph I d. 926 → Ralph II d. 943) → Walter I → Walter II "the White" → Ralph III → **Ralph IV (whose third wife was Anne of Kiev, the dowager Queen of France)** → Simon de Crépy → Adele of Valois → the Meulan counts → Adeline of Meulan → Roger de Beaumont → the user's line. *Valois, Vexin, Meulan, Blois, and Vermandois — the whole Oise-line of counties braided into one inheritance, rooted in Charlemagne's grandsons.*

**The Capetian–Robertian line — documented floor: Robert the Strong, d. 2 July 866 [S/A — the *Annales Fuldenses*; the *Historia Francorum Senonensis*; Flodoard; Settipani's Robertian studies; Wikipedia]:**
- **Hugh Capet (b. c. 941 – d. 24 Oct 996), founder of the Capetian dynasty — every King of France from 987 to 1848 descends from him** — son of **Hugh the Great (b. c. 898 – d. 16 June 956), Duke of the Franks, the most powerful man in West Francia** — and **Hedwig of Saxony**.
- **Hugh the Great** — son of **ROBERT I OF FRANCE (b. 15 Aug 866, posthumous – d. 15 June 923, killed at the Battle of Soissons), King of West Francia 922–923** — and **BEATRICE OF VERMANDOIS (c. 880 – after 26 March 931), daughter of Herbert I of Vermandois & Bertha of Morvois** — *a Carolingian, a male-line great-granddaughter of King Bernard of Italy through Charlemagne's son Pepin* (per the *Abbreviatio* of Frater Andreas and Alberic of Trois-Fontaines) — the Vermandois loop again, closing Charlemagne's Line A from the other side.
- **Robert I** — son of **ROBERT THE STRONG (c. 830 – 2 July 866)** — Charles the Bald's *missus dominicus* for Maine, Anjou and Touraine (853), Marquis of Neustria — **killed at the Battle of Brissarthe defending Francia against the joint Breton-Viking army of Salomon of Brittany and the chieftain Hastein; the *Annales Fuldenses* hail him "a second Maccabaeus"** — and **Adelaide of Tours (b. c. 820 – d. by 886; her epitaph preserved in a poem of Walahfrid Strabo)**, daughter of Hugh of Tours. **The documented floor of the Capetian line stands at Robert the Strong, d. 866** — with the son-of-Robert-III-of-Worms / Hesbaye-origin links flagged as conjectural (Settipani's Rhine-clan hypothesis).

**The Ottonian line — the user's other new royal house [A/S — Widukind of Corvey's *Res gestae Saxonicae*; the *Vitae* of St Matilda; Wikipedia]:**
- **Hedwig of Saxony (c. 910 – 10 May 965)** — third wife of Hugh the Great (m. 937, arranged by her brother), **regent of the Robertian estates for the boy Hugh Capet (956–c. 967) alongside her brother Archbishop Bruno** — daughter of **HENRY I "THE FOWLER" (c. 876 – 2 July 936), King of Germany from 919, founder of the Ottonian dynasty, victor over the Magyars at Riade (933), buried at Quedlinburg** — and **ST MATILDA OF RINGELHEIM (c. 894/97 – 14 March 968), daughter of Dietrich, Count of Westphalia, & Reinhild of Denmark; founder of Quedlinburg Abbey; canonized** (patroness of widows, large families, and the falsely accused).
- **Hedwig's siblings — the user's great-uncles and great-aunt of that generation: OTTO I THE GREAT, King of Germany and first Holy Roman Emperor (962); St BRUNO THE GREAT, Archbishop of Cologne & Duke of Lorraine; Gerberga, Queen of France (m. Louis IV); Henry the Quarrelsome, Duke of Bavaria.** The Liudolfing male line (Otto the Illustrious d. 912; Liudolf d. 866) is documented; the deeper Saxon stem (the Billung/Widukind claims) is flagged legend.
- *The user descends from Hugh Capet through two daughters — Hedwige (m. Regnier IV of Hainaut, the Roucy/Hainaut line) and, through Hugh Magnus's daughter Isabel of Vermandois, the Beaumont line — and from Henry the Fowler & St Matilda once, through Hedwig.*

**The Liudolfing floor — Liudolf of Saxony, d. 866, "the oldest verified member" [S/A — the *Annales Xantenses*; Widukind of Corvey; the FMG; Wikipedia]:**
- **Liudolf (c. 805/820 – 11/12 March 866), Count of Saxony, count of Eastphalia, *dux orientalis Saxonum*** — named *"Liudolfus comes"* in the *Annales Xantenses* at his death; founder of Brunshausen (852, with Pope Sergius II's approval; removed 881 to become Gandersheim Abbey), where he is buried. The *Chronica sancti Pantaleonis* (12th c.) puts him at the head of the Ottonian pedigree.
- His wife **Oda of Billung (d. 17 May 913 — the Vitae give her an incredible 107 years)**, daughter of **Billung, a Frankish princeps, and Aeda** — the Billung ancestors of the later Saxon dukes. Their children: **Bruno, Duke of Saxony (d. 880, killed fighting the Danes — progenitor of the Brunonids), Otto the Illustrious (father of Henry the Fowler), Liutgard (m. Louis the Younger, son of Louis the German — a Carolingian marriage), Hathumoda (first Abbess of Gandersheim), Gerberga, Christina.** His own parents — a Count Bruno (d. ~830s) and a Gisla — are semi-documented and flagged.
- And the Babenberg thread: Otto the Illustrious's wife **Hedwiga** — daughter of **Henry of Franconia, the Babenberg duke, and Ingeltrude** — is a *great-great-granddaughter of Charlemagne* (per the FMG): the house of Babenberg (the future margraves of Austria) enters the line here. The claimed descent of St Matilda from the Saxon hero Widukind remains flagged legend.

**The Breton push beyond Nominoe — Saint Judicael (c. 590 – 657), graded [S — the Cartulary of Redon (AD 869); the *Liber Historiae Francorum*; Fredegar; the Chronicle of Nantes; the FMG; the Brest academic study (2024)]:**
- In the Redon cartulary of **869**, the noblewoman **Roiantdreh** — adopting King Salomon as heir after her son Owain's death — **details her paternal ancestry over eight generations: *"Jedechael begat Urbien, Urbien begat Judon, Judon begat Custentin, Custentin begat Argant, Argant begat Judwal, Judwal begat Louenan, Louenan begat Roiantdreh"*** — eight named forebears reaching a *Jedechael* whom **modern historians (including Alan J. Raude) identify as King Judicael**, on the strength of the Domnonée royal name-cluster (de la Borderie's dissent logged).
- **SAINT JUDICAEL (c. 590 – 16 Dec 657)** — high king of Domnonée, uniter of Domnonée and Broërec — is documented in the Frankish sources: his **submission to Dagobert I at Clichy (c. 636–37)** with a tribute of 500 wild horses and sundry hawks and falcons; his retirement to the monastery of Saint-Méen, where he died (feast 16 December). His 11th-century *Life* states *"all the princes who reigned in Brittany since Judicael were descended from this king"* — the dynasty's own claim.
- **Honest grade: the line to Nominoe stands documented from 851; the eight-generation Roiantdreh pedigree (a primary source of 869) with the Judicael identification is *probable, not proven* — carried in the file at that grade**, with the century-long silence the Brest study itself concedes ("what happened in Brittany for the century that followed is unknown as there are no records").
- A meeting of the user's two deepest lines: **Alan II Barbetorte (d. 952) — whose grandson continues the user's Breton descent — was reared from infancy at the court of Æthelstan of Wessex** (the *Vita* quoted by William of Jumièges: "brought up from infancy with Æthelstan, Edward's eldest son"), where the Breton exiles prepared the Viking reconquest of 936–939. Wessex and Brittany — the family's two island dynasties — shared one household.

**THE ROBERTIAN STEM — CANCOR, d. 771: the convergence of the Capetian and Ottonian-Babenberg lines [A/S — the *Annales Nazariani*; the Cancor memorial stone at Lorsch (764); Wikipedia *Popponids*; the FMG]:**
- The Popponids/Babenbergs and the Capetian Robertians are **one stem**: **Poppo I of Grapfeld (d. 839–841)**, founder of the Babenberg dynasty, was the grandson of **Heimrich, Count in the Upper Rheingau (740–795) — son of CANCOR (d. 771)** — the Robertian count. **Cancor and his mother Williswinda founded Lorsch Abbey (764), where the Cancor memorial stone — among the oldest noble monuments of Germany — still stands. The documented floor of BOTH royal houses stands at Cancor, d. 771** — "an early collateral line of the Robertians related to the French royal family of the Capetians."
- The chain to the user: Cancor (d. 771) → Heimrich (740–795) → Poppo I (d. 839/41) → [Christian I, c. 870 — a gap] → **Henry of Franconia (d. 886 — the *Annals of Fulda*'s *princeps militiae* of Louis the Younger; killed defending Paris against the Vikings in 886) & Ingeltrude (possibly a daughter of Eberhard of Friuli — the Unruoching Carolingians, flagged)** → their daughter **Hathui (Hedwig) (d. 903) m. Otto the Illustrious of Saxony — "mother of King Henry the Fowler"** → Henry the Fowler → Hedwig of Saxony → Hugh Capet → the user. The Babenberg Feud (902–906) nearly exterminated the house — Henry's brothers killed, Adalbert beheaded at Hatto's word — but Hathui's marriage had already carried the blood into the Ottonian line.
- *The two great royal houses of the user's tree — the Capetian kings of France and the Ottonian emperors — converge at Cancor of the Robertians, d. 771.*

**The Ringelheim refinement — St Matilda's own family [A/B — the Vitae; Weis AR 141-18; the FMG; flagged pedigrees]:**
- **Dietrich of Ringelheim (d. 8 Dec 917)**, Count in the Westphalian Hamaland — son of **Reginhart, Count of Ringelheim, and Matilda** (the Immedinger counts of the lower Elbe; her grandmother Matilda was abbess of Herford where the young queen was raised). His wives — **Gisela of Lorraine** (daughter of **King Lothair II of Lotharingia** — a Carolingian, Charlemagne's great-great-granddaughter) and **Reinhild (Ragnhilde), of Danish-Frisian descent — probably a daughter of King Godefrid of Haithabu**, the Danish king — with the mother-assignment of St Matilda *disputed between the sources* (both logged). The biographers' claimed descent from the Saxon hero Widukind remains flagged legend; her sister Frederuna married Count Wichmann the Elder (Billung).

**The de Lacy block — documented floor: Hugh de Lacy of Lassy, b. c. 1020 [A — Wikipedia *De Lacy*; Complete Peerage; the libbyashworth charter study]:**
- **Hugh de Lacy, Lord of Lassy, Calvados (c. 1020 – d. Hereford 27 March 1085)** — *"the name is first recorded for Hugh de Lacy"*; his feudal lord was **Odo, Bishop of Bayeux — the Conqueror's half-brother and the patron of the Bayeux Tapestry**; m. **Emma de Bois-l'Évêque** (daughter of Ilbert, Marshal of Normandy).
- His sons rode with the Conquest in two trains: **Ilbert de Lacy (1045, Lassy – 1093, Pontefract)** — in Bishop Odo's party at Hastings; 150+ lordships; **Pontefract Castle** (park eight miles in circumference); participant in the harrying of the north — progenitor of the **Barons of Pontefract, Lords of Bowland & Clitheroe** (Kirkstall Abbey, the Clitheroe keep); and **Walter de Lacy** (in William fitz Osbern's party) — progenitor of **Ludlow Castle** and the **Lords of Meath in Ireland** (Trim Castle). Hugh's sons held their Lassy lands jointly under Norman *parage* — their mother Emma's own surviving charter (22 acres at Montmain to the nuns of St Amand) names her "mother of Ilbert de Lacy."
- The kin-by-marriage surety: the de Lacy barony passed through the female line (Albreda de Lisours) to **Roger FitzEustace, who took the de Lacy name and whose son JOHN DE LACY, 2nd Earl of Lincoln, was one of the twenty-five MAGNA CARTA SURETIES — chosen alongside his cousin Robert de Vere, Earl of Oxford** — the user's kin-web gains its sixth surety family (by marriage); the line later married into the **Duchy of Lancaster** (Thomas of Lancaster, beheaded at Pontefract 1322 — the duchy's origin).

**The Orabilis resolution — the mother of the Surety [S/A — Wikipedia; WikiTree Quincy-78 citing Richardson & Cokayne; the FMG]:**
- **Orabilis (Orabella) FitzNess of Leuchars (b. c. 1135, Fife – d. by 30 June 1210)** — daughter and **heiress of Nes fitz William, Lord of Leuchars** (the Scoto-Norman lordship with lands in Fife, Strathearn and Lothian) — married **Sir Robert de Quincy (c. 1140 – bef. 1208), Justiciar of Lothian** — and the marriage **produced SAHER DE QUINCY, 1st Earl of Winchester — THE MAGNA CARTA SURETY BARON, the user's direct ancestor** (through his granddaughter Elena de Quincy, wife of Alan la Zouche). The marriage was later formally dissolved (Robert m. secondly Eva — of Galloway kin per some, logged).
- **Through Orabilis the Surety carried the blood of the Scoto-Norman Leuchars lords; through his grandmother MAUD DE SENLIS — daughter of MAUD OF HUNTINGDON, the daughter of WALTHEOF — he carried the sainted earl's line a second time** (the St Liz connection of §8.4-Bamburgh).
- The Surety's siblings and children carried the kin-web further: a sister married into St Andrews; a daughter **Lora m. William de Valognes, Chamberlain of Scotland**; **Hawise m. Hugh de Vere, Earl of Oxford**; a son **Robert m. **The Hastings resolution — two branches, one forged cartulary, one royal napery [S — the FMG *Domesday Corrections* (Lancaster & Bevan, 2016); Clarence Smith's study (1966–68); C.N.L. Brooke's forger-identification; Dugdale citing the Glover collection]:**

Hawise de Guerres → Robert de Hastings (d. 1189) m. Maud de Flamville → Alice, heiress of Little Easton (m. Godfrey de Louvain, brother of the duke of Louvain).** Keats-Rohan's grafting of the dapifer line onto this house is disproved (William fitz Robert d. 1162 ≠ the dispenser d. c. 1182).

**The Vivonne detail — cousins who were sureties, and "a daughter of a hundred Earls" [S — the FMG *Foundations* article (Schooling, 2012)]:**

And the Athelney Register's note on Cecily survives: *"this recalcitrant lady… a daughter of a hundred Earls,"* deriving descent from *"Sibilla, co-heiress of William Marshall, William de Ferras Earl of Derby, Hugh de Vivonia, and William Malet — men of great renown in ancient times."*

**The Clifton wall — Gervase de Clifton, fl. c. 1180–1216, with the Alvaredus claim rejected [A — the University of Nottingham; A. C. Wood's Thoroton-based notes]:**

Roger de Samlesbury, c. 1200) descends from the Walter son of Osbert de Clifton of the 1194 fine.

Anceline of Montfort-sur-Risle)** → **Anquetil d'Harcourt — the first seigneur of Harcourt known under that title (Pipe Rolls 1130; m. Ève de Boissey)**. **The Danish royal line — Gorm the Old, d. 958×64, and the Siward hypothesis [A/S — Adam of Bremen; the Jelling stones; Wikipedia; Bolton's *Nottingham Medieval Studies* article (2007)]:**

His father **Harthacnut** — who *"came from Northmannia"* (Normandy!) to seize power per Adam of Bremen — is semi-legendary; the saga line through **Sigurd Snake-in-the-Eye to Ragnar Lodbrok is rejected** as legend.

If it holds, the user's line (Waltheof's) is kin to **Gorm's line — Harald Bluetooth, Sweyn Forkbeard, Cnut the Great, and the Estrithson kings of Denmark.** The bear-descent folklore (the *Vita Waldevi*'s *Ursus*) is rejected as Germanic folklore, like Ragnar.

**The Welsh royal line — documented floor: Merfyn Frych, d. 844 [S/A — the *Annales Cambriæ*; the *Annals of Ulster*; the *Brut y Tywysogyon*; the Harleian Genealogies; the Dictionary of Welsh Biography]:**

The Harleian genealogies — the oldest Welsh genealogical collection — *were possibly compiled in Rhodri's own reign* (858, Abergele). The deeper claim to **Cunedda Wledig (c. 450, the founder-hero of Gwynedd)** rests on the *Historia Brittonum* tradition — flagged as legendary-era.
