**The Etichonid line — documented floor: Eticho, 1st Duke of Alsace, d. 20 Feb 690 [S — the FMG citing the charters of 673/677/692; the *Vita Germani*; the Hohenburg chronicle]:**
- **Eticho (Adalrico/Adalrich), son of Leudesius, *maior domus*** — named *"Chadicho duce"* in Childeric II's charter of 4 March 673 (succeeding Boniface as duke in 666); the *Vita Germani* names him among the mighty of his day; the dynasty he founded — the **Etichonids, dukes of Alsace from the 660s to the 10th century** — is the root from which medieval tradition derives the **Habsburgs, the house of Baden, the house of Lorraine, the counts of Flanders, and the Eguisheim-Dagsburgs** ("one of the most notable noble families of the early Middle Ages").
- The line to the web: Eticho (d. 690) → Adalbert I (d. 722) → the Sundgau counts → **Liutfrid II (d. 802) → HUGH OF TOURS (d. 837, "the Timid" — Count of Tours & Sens, ambassador to Constantinople in 811 to renew the Pax Nicephori, father-in-law of the Emperor Lothair I)** → his daughter **ERMENGARD OF TOURS m. Emperor LOTHAR I — mother of three Carolingian kings (Louis II, Lothair II, Charles of Provence)** — and (per the traditional, now-disputed identification) Adelaide of Tours → Robert the Strong.
- **Saint Odile of Alsace (b. c. 662 – d. c. 720) — the patroness of Alsace — was Eticho's daughter**: a canonized aunt of that remote generation.
- **⚠ The honest correction (kept, as always):** the identification of **Adelaide of Tours** — Robert the Strong's wife — as Hugh of Tours's daughter rests on *"some misunderstandings in the Chronicle of Saint-Bénigne"* (Settipani); the Werner/Mistral alternative makes her a daughter of **Odo of Orleans & Ingeltrude — sister of Queen Ermentrude** — which would make Robert the Strong the *brother-in-law of Charles the Bald*. Both views are logged; the Etichonid line itself (to Eticho, 690) stands documented regardless, through Ermengard's imperial marriage.

**Adelaide of Aquitaine's line — the scholarly balance, and Rollo's daughter [S — Stewart Baldwin's *Henry Project*; the Lot/Merlet/Settipani vs. Bouchard debate]:**
- Hugh Capet's wife: **"probably the daughter of duke Guillaume I(III) 'Tête-d'Étoupe' of Poitou and Aquitaine (d. 963)"** — the balance of three independent strands (Richer's *Historiae*; the *Translatio sancti Maglorii* — *"filla Pictavorum comitis, de progenie Karoli magni imperatoris"*; the Bourgueil charter), with Constance Bouchard's dissent logged.
- Her mother: **Gerloc/Adele of Normandy — daughter of ROLLO** (per the same balance) — the Norman thread entering the Capetian house.
- The Ramnulfid dukes behind William: William III ← Ebalus Manzer (d. 935) ← **Ranulf II (d. 890) ← RANULF I (b. 820 – d. 19 Oct 866, Count of Poitiers 835, Duke of Aquitaine 852 — dying of wounds from the Battle of Brissarthe, the very battle where Robert the Strong fell)** — the documented floor of the house, *"considered a possible son of Gerard, Count of Auvergne, and Rothild, daughter of Louis the Pious"* — a Carolingian marriage, carried at its flagged grade. The *Translatio*'s claim that the Poitou counts were "of the lineage of Charlemagne" rests on this thread.

**The last pre-floors — Leudesius, the Billungs, and the Unruochings [S/A — the FMG; the mayors-of-the-palace lists; the Henry Project; the Cysoing testament]:**
- **Leudesius — the historical Neustrian MAYOR OF THE PALACE OF 675** (*"Leudesius (675), chosen but later deposed"* — between Wulfoald and Ebroin's return), named by the FMG as Eticho's father — with the caveat logged: *"his parentage is unproven in any contemporary 7th-century charter."* The Merovingian-princess claim for Eticho's wife **Berswinda** — daughter of **King Sigebert III of Austrasia**, sister of King Dagobert II, per the *Cronica Hohenburgensis* (*"filiam sororis sancti Leodegarii, sororem videlicet regina"* — niece of the martyred St Leogar and sister of the queen) — is flagged as the chronicle tradition; the *Chronicon Ebersheimense* instead names her father Garinus, a count of Poitiers.
- **The Billung line — and the Liudolfings' own Carolingian charter:** **Billung, *princeps*** — *"little is known of him beyond his name"* (perhaps the Billung of the Fulda donations of 837 and 863); the Wenskus/Hlawitschka conjectured parents (Count Bernhard & Christina — an onomastic argument) logged at its weak grade. But his wife **Aeda** carried, per their daughter's own foundation charter of 885, royal blood: ***"Oda comitissa, Pipini regis Italiæ ex filia neptis, Hliudolfi Ducis vidua"*** — **"the widowed Countess Oda, great-granddaughter of King Pepin of Italy"** — a CAROLINGIAN descent (the Lombard line) entering the Liudolfing house through Aeda; the FMG's honesty preserved: *"the accuracy of this document is not known."* The falsely attributed children (the Egbert/St Ida conjecture) are rejected.
- **The Unruochings — Eberhard of Friuli (d. 16 Dec 866×7) m. GISELA, daughter of Emperor Louis the Pious & Judith** — a Carolingian imperial marriage; his testament of 867 (the famous Cysoing will, dividing Lombardy, Anappes, Cysoing, Vitry and a great library among Unruoch, **BERENGAR — later BERENGAR I, KING OF ITALY, the last Carolingian crowned by the Pope**, d. 924 — Adalard, Rudolf, Engeltrud, Judith & Heilwich) is a document of the first rank. **The claim that his daughter Engeltrude married Henry of Franconia — grandmother of Henry the Fowler — is REJECTED by the modern scholarship** (Werner/Hlawitschka: Gisela would not have arranged Engeltrude's burial if she were then married); the Henry-the-Fowler-mother question stays open. Eberhard's own paternity (Unruoch II / Bérenger / Hunroch — the theories) and his mother Engeltrude (possibly a daughter of Beggo, Count of Paris — the Robertian link) are logged.

**THE ARNULFING PRE-FLOOR — the FMG's own verdicts [S — FMG *Merovingian Nobility*, Family of Arnulf; the *Gesta Episcoporum Mettensis*; WikiTree's blunt summary]:**

The full Ripuarian pedigree above them (Mellobaude → Richemir → Theodemir → Clovis the Ripuarian → Childebert → Siegbert the Lame → Cloderic the Parricide → Munderic → Bodegisel I & Palatina → Bodegisel II & Oda) is a named fantasy: *"may be a genealogical fantasy to flatter the Carolingians... none of whom are attested in a near-contemporary source."*

[655/65]); and the Chaume/Settipani theory that Ansegisel's daughter **Doda (Chrothechildis/Rotilde) married King Theoderic III — becoming Merovingian queen-regent** — is carried at its flagged grade. **St Wandrille of Fontanelle** (d. 665, aged 96 per his *Vita* — exaggerated) was Pepin's cousin (*"Walchisus consobrinus Pippini"*).

BRUNO OF SAXONY (c. 830/840 – Candlemas Day, 2 Feb. 880), Duke of Saxony 866–880, *dux orientalis Saxonum* (perhaps *dux totius Saxonum* per Widukind)** — eldest son of Liudolf & Oda of Billung; called ***ducem et fratrem reginæ*** — "the queen's brother" (his sister Liutgard married Louis the Younger, whom he supported against Charles the Bald). **Killed at the Battle of Lüneburg Heath/Ebsdorf fighting the Norsemen — probably a Danish remnant of the Great Heathen Army that Alfred had shattered** — in a crushing midwinter defeat: the bishops of Minden and Hildesheim and twelve counts with him, the *Annales Fuldenses* naming each; Thietmar adds that he drowned in a flooded river during the retreat (2 Feb.). Succeeded by his brother **Otto the Illustrious** — grandfather of Henry the Fowler. A 877 charter of Louis the Younger names *"Brun et Otto nostri fideles comites…Liutolf genitor eorum…Gerbirg soror eorundem comitum."* **Relics translated c. 1160 by the Dannenberg counts to Ebstorf Abbey — feast February 2; patron of Brunswick; legend's founder of that city and forebear of the Brunonid counts (the 1002 royal candidate Brun I) — with the FMG-flagged caveat that some "Brun" references may belong to an earlier margrave, "Brun the Younger."**
- **ST. BRUNO THE GREAT (b. May 925, Cologne – d. Oct. 11, 965, Reims), Archbishop of Cologne 953–965, Duke of Lorraine 954–965 — the *archdux* of his biographer Ruotger** — youngest son of Henry the Fowler & St. Matilda; educated at Utrecht under Bishop Balderich; Otto's chancellor from 940; abbot of Lorsch and Corvey, where he restored Benedictine observance; consecrated at Cologne 25 September 953 as Otto's answer to the rebellion of Ludolf and Conrad the Red — then Duke of all Lorraine, "the most powerful man after Otto, not just in Germany but beyond." **Regent of West Francia: guardian of King Lothair from 954 and of Hugh Capet from 956 — brother-in-law to both their dead fathers and maternal uncle to both boys** — and from 962 co-regent of the Empire with William of Mainz, guardian of the child Otto II. **He died at Reims on a peace mission between his two quarrelling nephews — Lothair of France and Hugh Capet — and lies in his own foundation of St. Pantaleon, Cologne, where Theophanu was later entombed beside him; feast October 11; his *Vita* by Ruotger is counted among the best medieval biographies.**
- **The kinship, made exact:** the two saints are *uncle and nephew to each other* — Thietmar calls the Ebsdorf martyr the **"great-uncle" of the Archbishop of Cologne**. Both are the user's kin through different siblings of one dynasty: **St. Bruno of Saxony was the brother of the user's direct ancestor Otto the Illustrious; St. Bruno the Great was the brother of the user's direct ancestress Hedwig of Saxony.** And the *Henry Project* quotes the contemporaries: Hugh Capet and his brother came to the king *"mediante avunculo ipsorum Brunone"* — "by the mediation of their uncle Bruno" — the same Bruno who was regent while Hedwig, his sister, held the boy Hugh's household. Flodoard calls Hugh *consobrinus* — cousin — of King Lothair. The dynasty governed two kingdoms with a brother, a sister, and two saints.

**The Brunos' lines — backward and forward [S/A — Wikipedia *Brunonids*; FMG; Britannica *Billung dynasty*; the 2025 ancient-DNA preprint]:**

*And the maternal kin — the Billungs:* **Oda of Billung's dynasty ruled Saxony for five generations — Hermann Billung (d. 973), Otto's sword against the Slavs, founder of Lüneburg St. Michael; Bernhard I (d. 1011); Bernhard II (d. 1059); Ordulf (d. 1072, m. Ulfhild of Norway); Magnus (d. Aug. 1106, m. Sophia of Hungary) — extinct 1106**, the duchy passing through Magnus's daughter **Wulfhild m. CHRODOARA** — the aunt herself, of the Chrodoinides clan (Tholey, Wissembourg, Beaulieu). ## 9. The Hannay Thread — Clan Hannay of Sorbie

**Family tradition:** "Our relatives were the Hanna clan of the Sorbie castle." — identified as **Clan Hannay of Sorbie Tower** ("the Old Place of Sorbie"), Wigtownshire, Galloway, Scotland.

**Documented clan history [A/B — clan records, published pedigrees]:**
- The name appears on the **Ragman Roll of 1296** (Gilbert de Hannethe submitted to Edward I); the clan's sway over the Machars of Galloway was such that the region was called **"Machars Hannay."**
- **Sorbie**: Odo Hannay, first recorded owner (mid-1400s) → Robert (sheriff-depute of Wigtownshire, 1498) → Patrick (murdered 1543) → **Alexander A'hanna**, who built the surviving tower house c. 1550–1580 while feuding with the Murrays of Broughton, the Kennedys, and the Stewarts.
- **1601: the Hannays were outlawed**; the estate was broken up (sold 1626 to Sir Patrick Agnew); the diaspora ran through **Ulster** to America — hence the variants **Hanna, Hannah, Hanney, Hannay**.
- The tower survives to the second floor; the **Clan Hannay Society** (chief: Dr. David Hannay of Kirkdale, descended from a younger Sorbie son who acquired Kirkdale, 1532) stewards its restoration. Published pedigrees: ***Hanna of Castle Sorbie, Scotland, and Descendants*** and ***The House of Hanna*** — both free on archive.org; the latter opens: *"All bearing the name are undoubtedly to be traced to a lowland clan of Scotland, whose chief had his residence in Sorbie Castle."*

**THE HANNAY DEEP TRACE — the line that goes way back (29 September dive) [NA — electricscotland.com (Anderson's *Scottish Nation*), scotclans.com, clanhannay.org]:** the clan — *'a surname originally **Ahannay**, also met with as **De Anneth**, belonging to an old family in Galloway, supposed to be of Scandinavian origin'* — is first documented in **1296, when GILBERT DE HANYETHE appears on the RAGMAN ROLL among those swearing fealty to Edward I** (he chose John Balliol, the local lord, against Bruce). *'The family early obtained the lands of Sorby or Sorbie, from which the parish is called,'* and held them until the late 1600s. The documented members read like the family's own stories: **John de Hanna, 1424; Robert Hannay of Sorbie, son of Odo, 1488; Alexander Hannay of Sorbie, 1500**; the arms in Sir David Lindsay's emblazoned volume; **the tower of Sorbie, built from James IV's reign** — *'anciently a tower of some strength, now a picturesque ruin'*; **Patrick Hannay sat for Wigtown in 1581**; **Sir Patrick Hannay the poet, whose 1622 verses were eulogized by a dozen hands — one naming the clan's legendary pride: *'Thy Ancestors were ever worthy found, Else Galdus' grave had grac'd no Hannays ground'*. ldus, the Galgacus of the Caledonians, whose grave the clan's tradition claimed on their own lands**; **Dean James Hannay of St Giles', at whom Jenny Geddes threw her stool on July 23, 1637**, opening the riots against the liturgy; **Sir Robert Hannay of Mochrum, created a Nova Scotia baronet in 1630**, whose daughter Jane's blood 'flows in the noble houses of Hamilton and Abercorn'; the Kirkdale branch founded by **Alexander Hannay in 1582** — the present chiefly line (Sir Samuel Hannay, d. 1790 → the present chief of that Ilk). The fall: the long feud with the **Murrays of Broughton** ended in the Hannays being **outlawed in 1601**; the tower and lands were lost around 1640; the diaspora ran to **Ulster** and the world. **The name itself** — 'possibly deriving from the Gaelic O'Hannaidh, or Ap Shenaeigh' — is carried as Gaelic-or-Scandinavian, unweighed [B]. **THE FULL DOCUMENTED CHAIN (29 September dive) [A — the Ragman Roll text; MacKerlie, *History of the Lands and Their Owners of Galloway* I:455; Anderson, *Scottish Nation* II:450; Agnew, *Hereditary Sheriffs of Galloway*; the *Hanna of Castle Sorbie* and *House of Hanna* books, archive.org]:** at Berwick in 1296 the Roll records not one but TWO: *'Gilbert de Annethe and Gilbert a'Hannay, both of Sorbie, Galloway, appeared before the herald and signed the Ragman Roll of Landed Gentry and attached the seal containing the Hanna Coat of Arms'* — the arms: **three roebucks' heads azure, collared and belled, motto *'Per ardua ad alta'* — 'Through difficulties I seek higher things'** (Nisbet gives the crest a Crusader origin); Gilbert de Annethe was then *'one of the clerks of King Edward I (whether he was Norman or not is open to argument)'*. The clan's stances: for Balliol against Bruce (suffering Edward Bruce's raid, 1308), against the Douglas tribute, with the king at the Douglas stabbing (1452) and the battle of Wigton (1455). The named line: **Fynlaus Ahanna, Canon of Whithorn, 1390; John de Hana, shipmate of King James II, 1424; Dougall Ahanna, granted Capenoch, 1457; Rev. Gilbert Hanna, Chaplain of the Scottish Parliament, 1466; Andrew Hanna of the Royal Archer Bodyguard of the Scots in France, 1469; ODO HANNAY — first of the lairds, granted the Sorbie lands by KING JAMES IV, 1488–1494 [S-MacKerlie]; Robert 1494–1500; Alexander; JOHN 1524–1544, m. (MARGARET) STEWART, daughter of Sir Alexander Stewart of Garlies (the Stewarts of Garlies → Earls of Galloway — kin of the Scottish royal house); Patrick 1544–1581 (Parliament); Margaret the heiress (through whom Sorbie passed to the Stewarts); Donald (Gustavus Adolphus's service, 1620); Alexander (Kirkdale, 1582 — the present chiefly line).** **The progenitor of tradition: PATRICK A'HANNA, c. 1250, southwest Scotland — 'earliest known name of the present-day Hanna family'** [B — the family genealogy's own claim, carried at its grade]. ****The name's origin — the scholarly battery, all quoted:** Dr. Black (*Surnames of Scotland* [A]): *'Ahannay, now Hannay, may be for "ap Sheanaigh," son of Senach'*; Agnew [A]: *'of Pictish descent… an aspirated form of Sennach… "sean," old, venerable'*; Anderson: *'supposed to be of Scandinavian origin'*; W. S. Hanna: *'in Norse, Hani; in Danish, Henne; in Saxon, Hane'* — Galloway itself was Brittonic-Pictish, ruled by an Anglo-Saxon kingdom c. 700–900, then Gaelicized-Viking after 900. The Ulster diaspora (Plantation 1608–1620) and the American stream (*'many Hannas came with' Rev. Thomas Clarke's Presbyterian companies, Pennsylvania–New York–Virginia, 1730–1760* — the probable vein of the Albany Hannays). **GRADE: the clan's documented floor is the Ragman Roll, 1296; its traditional floor Patrick a'Hanna c. 1250; the user's bridge to it (Andrew Hannay, 1733–1808, below) is [B] — the way-back line the family remembered is real as far as the record reaches, and the record reaches eight centuries.**

**The American bridge [B — FamilySearch Library catalog]:** **Andrew Hannay, b. 1733 in Galloway**, eldest son of Andrew Hannay & Margaret McCaw, **emigrated 1774 aboard the *Gale of Whitehaven***, settled first at Bethlehem, then **Westerlo, Albany County, New York** (d. 1808). His descendants spread through **Albany, Schoharie, and Oneida counties** — his son **David (b. Westerlo 1784 – d. 1866; m. Hannah Ter Bush)** fathered fifteen children, among them **Henry T.B. Hannay of Annsville, ONEIDA COUNTY** — the *same county*, one town over, from the user's Abbott–Webster ancestors at Hanover. Even Andrew's third wife was named **Hannah Wendell**.

**The two "splits" named by the family:**
1. **At Parke**: Dr. Roger Parke Jr. of Hopewell (Hunterdon Co., NJ — the user's grandmother's line) married **Susannah Hannah Robinson**, b. **1668, Hexham, Northumberland, England** — border country within sight of Galloway — daughter of **Thomas Robinson of Crosswicks Creek, NJ (testator 1690; his will left "the younger Roger Parke" a colt)** and his wife **Susannah** (who remarried soon after, per Quaker records). **Her maiden name is the open name.** The "Hannah" preserved in her daughter's name is precisely the kind of marker a Hanna–Robinson marriage would leave. ⚠ One FamilySearch profile conflicts (born 1687 Gedney, Lincolnshire — a probable same-name merge; the Hexham identification is the one Parke Society researchers support). Next record: the Robinson marriage entry (Quaker monthly meeting records, Nottingham/Chesterfield).

Park's 1902 'Thomas de Parke, Master of the Hunt to the Conqueror' origin tale is the classic huntsman fabrication — **rejected** — though its one honest kernel stands: the name occurs in Domesday as *Parck* and *De Parce* [NA]. **The decisive next reads:** the Cartmel/Frith parish registers (Allen's 1606 baptism and 1636 marriage); the **Visitation of Northumberland, 1615** (St. George) — the heraldic visitation that would carry a Hexham/Allendale Parke pedigree if one was ever recorded; and Ege's pages for the American generations.

Sir Percival Scrope Marling, V.C., d. 1936) — the parish where the Parkes sat was Despenser land, then Arundel land, then crown land. The register is fully transcribed online (FreeReg, St George King's Stanley; usgenealogyexpress carries the marriages vol. 1573–1595), and the Gloucestershire Genealogical Database holds Glos wills 1541–1858 — the direct reads are open. Larue, to Miss **Jane Parke**, both of Delaware township'* — the name Jane Parke in active use in the county a century before the user's grandmother; and the Stonebraker–Cooper account documents the 1735 Hopewell exodus (the 'Coxe Affair'): John Parke fled with Thomas Smith to the Shenandoah and then the Yadkin, father of George Parks of Back Creek/Rowan — the westward Parke thread [NA]. Aug. 14, 1698), Michael (June 1702, 'Christening witnesses: Michael Killmer the younger'), Simon (June 18, 1702 – d. Mar. 28, 1788, Little Nine Partners, Dutchess Co., NY), Dorothea Elisabetha (1706)** — the PALATINE family that crossed to the Hudson Valley: **Red Hook, Rhinebeck, Germantown, Gallatin (Dutchess and Columbia counties, NY)** — the West Camp, Saugerties, Athens churches filling the record (St Paul's Zion Lutheran Red Hook 1730–1904; Rhinebeck Reformed; Gallatin Reformed; Katsbaan; St Thomas Churchtown) — with the whole Palatine web itemized: the Tinkeys, Hollenbecks, Pulvers, Knickerbockers, Hosfraads, Shaver/Schaefer, Shaver, Teator/Teeder, Spoor, Berner — and the second and third Kilmer generations spreading to Washington, Otsego, Wayne, and Fulton counties (George 'in War of 1812,' buried Worchester, Otsego). **THE KILMER FLOOR — GEORG KILLMER, b. 1621, ALTENGRONAU (29–30 September dive) [B — WikiTree Kilmer-121 and the freepages killmerdm book, quoting Gary Horlacher's transcription of the Altengronau Parish Register]:** the line's documented head: **GEORG1 KILLMER (b. 1621, Altengronau, Hessen – bur. July 22, 1706, 'age 85') m. CATHARINA (MILIUS) KILLMER (b. abt. 1625 – abt. 1714)** — their sons incl. **FRANCISCI KÜLMER (1646 – bur. Feb. 1714), MICHAËL, JOHANNES (1663 – m. Dorothea Elisabetha 1666–1730), and JOHANN GEORG (b. abt. 1673 – aft. 1728 — 'aka Killmer, Kuhlman'),** the father of the New York emigrants (Anna Elisabetha 1698, Michael 1702, Simon 1702, Dorothea 1706). The whole Altengronau web is register-quoted (the Roden, Schuster, Fehl, Milius, Karbel, Zirckel witness families; the Schüster and Feinbach marriages; Frantz Killmer of Duttenbrunn 1701–1777 who stayed). **Behind 1621 — FOUR MORE GENERATIONS, tree-carried (29–30 September dive) [A-FS — carried at tree grade until the register confirms]:** **MICHEL KILLMER (b. 1510, Altengronau – d. 1570), son of JOHANN ANDREAS GEORG KILLMER (b. ~1480)** — the FamilySearch profile extends the line four generations behind Georg (1621), through the Sinntal/Main-Kinzig district's own naming; flagged [A-FS], the Altengronau register read is the confirming document. **THE HOMELAND'S OWN HISTORY [A/B — Wikipedia; the *Historisches Lexikon Bayerns*]:** Altengronau lies in the FREIGERICHT — the Free Court district between the Main and the Kinzig: 'In the 12th century the Freigericht was established by Emperor FRIEDRICH BARBAROSSA… excused taxes and obligatory service,' governed by its elected LANDRICHTER — per the founding legend (1184): the Emperor, ambushed by the Rannenburg knights beside the stream, freed by the armed farmers; 'This stream, colored red by the blood, shall be named Rotenbach'; 'You should not have to obey anybody but your emperor… only one load of hay with a rooster sitting on it' yearly — the free farmers' court lasted until Maximilian I granted it to Mainz and Hanau in 1500 (confirmed by Charles V, 1523); because of the Mainz lordship the parish stayed CATHOLIC — which is why the Killmer baptisms stand in the Catholic register while the witness families (Steyerbach, Roden, Schuster, Schüster, Feinbach, Fuss, Zirckel, Milius, Wirth) intermarry around them. **THE AMERICAN GENERATIONS, REGISTER-QUOTED (the killmerdm pages read in full):** GEORGE W. KILLMER SR. (b. June 10, 1764, Red Hook – d. September 27, 1843, Gallatin); WILLIAM E. KILMER JR. (Aug. 20, 1783 – Mar. 2, 1855, Red Hook; m. Catherina Bathrick 1805); ELISABETH (b. Feb. 6, 1758, Gallatin; father Georg Killmer, mother EVA GERTRAUT DINGS); Adam (b. Nov. 2, 1754 – Apr. 2, 1835; m. Margaretha Jagger); Lucinda (Dec. 2, 1805 – Apr. 11, 1881, Jackson Corners); Peter (1785, s/o Wilhelm & Gertjen Pulver; m. Mary Pulver — children Charles H. of Copake 1812, Harriet 1815); and the Copake/Gallatin/West Camp church books quoting every baptism to 1906. **JOHANN GEORG'S NEW YORK RECORD — THE SUBSISTENCE-ROLL PROOF (29–30 September dive) [B-WikiTree Kilmer-121, quoting the rolls; S-form]:** the Palatine rolls carry him year by year: *'Jurich Kelmer was naturalized on Jan. 17, 1715/16. Hans Jury Kolemer was on the roll of the Independent Company of the Manor of Livingston on Nov. 30, 1715. Georg Kolmer, with wife and children, were at Hessberg around 1716/17. The mark of Jury Coolmer was recorded on an old manuscript dated 1718/19 (Livingston Papers, Reel #4). Jury Coelmer/Coolmer was a Palatine Debtor in 1718, 1719, 1722 and 1726.'* And the identification is PROVEN by roll arithmetic: *'From 4 August 1710 to 25 March 1712 the family is listed as 4 people over the age of 10, 3 children under the age of 10. The next roll, dated 24 June 1712, lists 6 over 10, 1 under ten — two of the children turned ten between March and June of 1712. This fits exactly to be the twin sons Simon and Michael, whose birthday to turn 10 was 18 June.'* Born Jan. 15, 1673 (Altengronau), confirmed 1688; m. (1) **EVA ZIRCKEL** (abt. 1696), (2) **ANNA MARGARETHA SCHEICHER** (June 10, 1709, Altengronau); nine children: Anna Elisabetha (m. **Kuhn**), Michael, Simon, Johannes, Dorothea Elisabetha, Philipp, Abraham, Georg, Catharina. **AND THE JOHNSTOWN FAMILY, IN THE GROUND (29–30 September dive) [A — the Johnstown Cemetery transcription, Fulton Co. FREDERICK BARNETT KILMER (1851, CONNECTICUT – 1934) — Johnson & Johnson's Scientific Director from 1889, inventor of its baby powder, author of the first commercial first-aid manual (1901)** — and **ANNIE ELLEN KILBURN (1849–1932), writer and composer** (her own 'Trees' setting, her 'Lullaby for a Baby Fairy'); Joyce named for two priests of Christ Church, New Brunswick; Rutgers 1904–06, Columbia A.B. 1908; m. **Aline Murray** (1908); 'Trees' in *Poetry*, August 1913; sergeant, 165th; killed at Seringes-et-Nesles; Croix de Guerre presented to his mother, widow, and son Kenton; the family home still stands (a small museum). - **Atkinson** (Emily MACKLIN Atkinson, m. James Dunton Carpenter Jr.): **THE CLAN AUTHORITY FOUND (29–30 September dive) [A — Jno. B. Atkinson, *The Atkinsons of New Jersey, from the records of Friends meetings and from offices of record in the state*, Burlington Bee Print, 1890 — FULL TEXT at archive.org (atkinsonsofnewje00atki)]:** the book opens with the immigrants: ***'two young men among the passengers of the ship Antelope, just arrived from Belfast, Ireland' — 23d day of the 8th month (October), 1681, at Philadelphia — 'tall, stalwart Scotchmen… wearing the same plain Quaker garb' — JAMES and THOMAS ATKINSON*** (James, one account says, from Drogheda). **James m. HANNAH NEWBIE**, widow of Mark Newbie, 1684, at her house in Newton Township, GLOUCESTER COUNTY — *'here the first Friends' meeting in Gloucester county was held… Mark Newbie had his bank, probably the first institution of that character in America.'* **WILLIAM ATKINSON m. ELIZABETH CURTIS** (daughter of Thomas Curtis) at Burlington, **9 March 1686**, the Quaker marriage certificate printed in the book — the SAME William of the Burlington will calendar (1694 bondsman; 1698 the Cross will's sons-in-law Jonathan & Thomas Curtis 'children of Wm. Atkinson'; 1706 guardian). Later Atkinsons in the calendar: Samuel (witness 1710), **Joseph & Hannah Atkinson of Springfield** (witnesses 1726). The compiler's own line — George T. & Elizabeth B. Atkinson of **MULLICA HILL**, whose GOLDEN WEDDING (Feb. 5, 1890) prompted the book. **THE BOOK'S SPINE — THE COMPILER'S OWN LINE [A — the introduction, verbatim]:** *'Having in my possession the Quaker certificates of the marriages of FIVE GENERATIONS of Atkinsons, it was not very difficult to go back another generation to WILLIAM ATKINSON, who married ELIZABETH CURTIS, in Burlington, the ninth of March, 1686. I am indebted to JOHN CLEMENT, of Haddonfield, New Jersey, for most of the information contained in this sketch… Chas. S. French, of Philadelphia, aided me.'* — the Mullica Hill line runs through **WILLIAM** (the Burlington founder), not James: the main genealogy of the book is William's descent. **THE SECOND GENERATION, IN FULL [A — the 1890 book, pp. 18–35, as cited in the Shinn Family history]:** **JOHN ATKINSON (son of William & Elizabeth Curtis) m. HANNAH SHINN** (daughter of James Shinn), intentions 1st mo. 1716, married Nov. 21, 1716, forty-eight witnesses — their children: **Hannah (1719, m. Cowperthwait), SAMUEL (1721, m. Esther Evins, 2) Elizabeth Conrow), James (1724), Abigail (1726, m. Jonathan Eldridge 1750), Elizabeth (1731, m. Eleazer Fenton 1753), Patience (1733), John (1735), David (1737), Moses (1739)**. **MICHAEL ATKINSON (son of William) m. HOPE SHINN**, May 1720, at James Shinn's house (Michael d. 1746, Hope 1761, both with wills): **Rachel (m. Abraham Kille 1739), Levi, Job, Elizabeth (m. William Jones 1747), Mary, Jonathan, Hannah (m. Shadock Pancoast), Michael (d. 1772, no issue), Amos**. And the third intermarriage: **Francis Shinn m. Elizabeth Atkinson** (the third child of James Shinn into the William line). **THE FAMILY WENT WEST — THE KENTUCKY REMOVAL (29–30 September dive) [B — WikiTree Atkinson-3249, citing the 1900/1910 censuses and the Evansville Courier]:** the golden-wedding family left Mullica Hill: **JOHN BOND ATKINSON (1840–1911, the compiler), d. in the KENTUCKY–INDIANA orbit** — the 1910 census places *'ANNA N. ATKINSON in the household of JOHN B. ATKINSON, MAGISTERIAL DISTRICT 5, HOPKINS COUNTY, KENTUCKY'*; his obituary ran in the **EVANSVILLE COURIER (Vanderburgh Co., INDIANA), September 22, 1911** — the same county where George C. married Fannie Miller in 1878. George Tatum's six children, complete: **JOHN BOND (1840–1911, the compiler), LYDIA TOWNSEND (m. Comly), TOWNSEND ATKINSON, CAROLINE M. (m. Farren — the Farren name recalling Swarthmore's Caroline Farren Atkinson, class of 1909!), GEORGE C. (1852–1933), WARREN (1856–1913, m. Ella S. Simon)**. So the candidate-household is a KENTUCKY-INDIANA branch of New Jersey Quaker stock — and Emily (b. ~1879, after the Indiana marriage) may well have been born in the west, then sent east to school among the Jersey Atkinsons (Swarthmore, Woodbury 1902, the Montclair marriage) — the pattern of a western-born daughter of an eastern family, carried at candidate grade [flagged — the household's 1900 census location and children-list remain the two reads]. **THE EARLINGTON CONFIRMATION (29–30 September dive) [A — the Swarthmore register and the 1909 *Halcyon* yearbook, both verbatim]:** *'CAROLINE FARREN ATKINSON (Mrs. Newell Gilder Alford) (Hist.) — EARLINGTON, KY'* (the register) and *'Caroline Farren Atkinson, K A Θ, EARLINGTON, KY., German'* (the yearbook) — the second Atkinson in the Swarthmore register is OF **EARLINGTON, KENTUCKY — THE VERY TOWN FROM WHICH JNO. B. ATKINSON COMPILED THE 1890 BOOK** (his title-page address!). So the Swarthmore path explains itself: **the Earlington Atkinsons sent their daughters to Swarthmore — Caroline Farren (1909) eight years behind Emily Macklin (1901)** — the cousins' college, the Quaker college, the family pattern. If Emily's household is George C. & Fannie's, Caroline Farren Atkinson is her first cousin — both granddaughters of George Tatum & Elizabeth Townsend Bond [flagged — the Caroline Farren Atkinson profile's parentage is the confirming read]. **AND THE MILLER FAMILY, FANNIE'S PEOPLE [NA — the Civil War Governors of Kentucky record, a primary-source index]:** *'JAMES Q. MILLER (Trigg County, Kentucky, farmer) — Father of Ida E. Miller, Ira Miller, Bettie Miller, Alice Miller, **FANNIE MILLER**, Eliza Miller, and Effie Miller'* — Fannie Miller's FATHER NAMED: **JAMES Q. MILLER of Trigg County**, farmer on the 1850 census, with the seven children listed — the bride's family documented in the same Kentucky orbit (Cadiz, Trigg Co.). **THE COMPILER'S CHAIN, COMPLETE — AND THE COMPILER'S OWN NAME REVEALED (29–30 September) [B — WikiTree-2759]:** *'GEORGE TATUM ATKINSON, b. Oct. 26, 1810, MULLICA HILL, son of John Atkinson & ELIZABETH BORTON — m. ELIZABETH TOWNSEND BOND'* — golden wedding Feb. 5, 1890 — **JNO. B. ATKINSON, THE COMPILER = JOHN BOND ATKINSON** (the 'B.' his grandmother's name), and George Tatum's children in full: **'JOHN BOND ATKINSON (the compiler)… GEORGE C. ATKINSON 1852–1933; m. Fannie W. Miller (1856–1940); WARREN ATKINSON 1856–1913; m. Ella S. Simon (1863–1920)'** — **ELIZABETH TOWNSEND BOND: the TOWNSEND name in the compiler's own grandmother** [flagged — her mother a Townsend; the name the user's family carries three times over]. George Tatum an elder of the Mullica Hill Meeting (the Harrison Township history). **THE CLAN'S ENGLISH DEPTH — HOUGH'S PMHB ARTICLE, READ (29–30 September dive) [S/A — Oliver Hough, 'Atkinson Families of Bucks County,' PMHB 30 (1906): 57–79, 220–237, 332–347, 479–502, JSTOR 20085333]:** the Bucks County stock resolves into TWO FAMILIES. **(1) THE THOMAS ATKINSON FAMILY — YORKSHIRE:** **THOMAS ATKINSON, the Quaker minister**, came to Bucks County by 1682 (Falls MM), took up William Penn's promised 500 acres, and d. 1687 — his widow **JANE (BOND) ATKINSON was an early Quaker MINISTER who married secondly WILLIAM BILES**, the notable New Jersey proprietor; their sons **SAMUEL (1685 — the Chesterfield founder, above), ISAAC, and WILLIAM (born in Yorkshire)**. Thomas's brother: **CHRISTOPHER ATKINSON — the Norwich-gAoled QUAKER PAMPHLETEER of 1654–55**, whose printed titles Hough lists: *'The Sword of the Lord DRAWN, and furbished against the man of Sin… By one whose Name in the flesh is Christopher Atkinson, who am one that the world doth scornfully call a Quaker' (London, 1654); 'David's Enemies discovered… by Christopher Atkinson, GEORGE WHITEHEAD' (1655); 'The Testimony of the everlasting Gospel witnessed through sufferings, by Christopher Atkinson, RICHARD HUBBERTHORNE, and James Lancaster'; 'ISHMAEL, and his MOTHER, cast out into the WILDERNESS… Given forth from the Spirit of the Lord in us that do suffer in Gaol of NORWICH for the truth's sake'* (1655) — an Atkinson writing beside the founders of the Society of Friends, imprisoned for the Truth a generation before the American migration. **(2) THE CHRISTOPHER AND JOHN ATKINSON FAMILY — SCOTFORTH, LANCASHIRE:** **JOHN ATKINSON & SUSANNA (HYNDE)**, sailing from Lancaster in the ship ***Britannia*, 1699 — BOTH DIED ON THE VOYAGE**; their three children taken by the aunts **ALICE & MARY HYNDE** to Bucks County under the care of Middletown MM — the guardian **WILLIAM STOCKDALE** (m. Alice Hynde 1703); the children: **WILLIAM (b. Mar. 31, 1687, Scotforth – d. 1754, Upper Dublin; m. Phebe Taylor; will proved Jan. 1, 1755, leaving £200 each to daughters Susanna Davis, Phebe Walton, Hannah Hughs), JOHN (b. Nov. 25, 1695, Scotforth – d. Jan. 1752, Upper Makefield; the WEAVER — his will: 'my black mare Saddle and Bridle and also my GREAT BIBLE' to son William, the best bed and pewter to daughter Mary, 'looms and gears' to the weaver-sons; the 200-acre Manor of Highlands farm, £50 from the London Company, the pear tree still standing in 1901, the farm in Atkinson hands to the sixth generation), MARY (m. John Stockdale)**. The accompanying 1682 Settle MM certificate families: **HAYHURST, WAIN, CROASDALE, WALMSLEY, STACKHOUSE** — Yorkshire Quakers to Bucks. **THE NJ–PA JUNCTION — RESOLVED [B — WikiTree Atkinson-1856 citing Hough and the MM records]:** Thomas the Antelope brother IS Hough's Yorkshire Thomas: *'In 1681 Jane and Thomas Atkinson obtained a certificate from the Friends Meeting at BEAMSLEY, Yorkshire, and they came to New Jersey but soon after relocated to Bristol township, Bucks County'* — one man, both books. And his parentage: **THOMAS = son of REV. JOHN ATKINSON of KIRKBY MALHAM, Yorkshire, and MARY CANBY (of Fishlake)** [B-WikiTree] — the Yorkshire clerical family. Thomas m. **Jane Bond at KNARESBOROUGH, 4 June 1678** (Knaresborough MM register); his will (Aug. 10, 1687; proved Aug. 31): *'Wife Jane executrix, to sell 100 acres bought of Joseph English; 100 acres to brother JOHN ATKINSON; children ISAAC, WILLIAM, and SAMUEL'* — witnesses Joseph Kirkbride & Richard Londes. The children: **ISAAC (b. 2 Mar. 1679, Yorkshire; later of Nottingham Twp., Burlington Co., 1712); WILLIAM (b. ~1681 — CORONER OF BUCKS COUNTY, councilman of Bristol, m. 1704 MARY HOUGH, daughter of Richard Hough of Macclesfield — 'Quaker of some notoriety'); SAMUEL (b. July 17, 1685 — the Chesterfield founder, above).** Hough's verdict on the OTHER family: *'Although apparently no relation to the Yorkshire Thomas Atkinson, it seems very likely that the Lancashire Thomas Atkinson was related to Christopher and John Atkinson'* — the two stocks kept apart, per the scholar. **A THIRD THOMAS, logged:** John Shinn's will (Jan. 1711/12) names *'son [son-in-law] THOMAS ATKESON and wife SARAH'* (Shinn) — the Burlington branch's Thomas, in the same will that carries William & Elizabeth Curtis's daughter-line. **THE YORKSHIRE GRANDPARENTS — NAMED [B — WikiTree Atkinson-772]:** the minister Thomas's brother **JOHN ATKINSON was baptized 26 JUNE 1636 at KIRKBY MALHAM, son of ADAM ATKINSON and his wife ISABELL (FRANKLAND)** — the parents named from the parish register: **Adam & Isabell (Frankland) Atkinson of Kirkby Malham** — the Yorkshire depth now stands two generations above the Valiant Sixty's brother, at tree-grade [B], the Kirkby Malham register read the confirming document. **THE CLERICAL-DYNASTY ORBIT [NA — the Goldhanger research]:** six related clerics of the name — the Rev. Christopher Atkinsons of Thorp Arch and Elland (1713–1774; 1773–1843), the Trinity tutor & ornithological artist (1755–1795; his watercolour albums sold for £24,000), the Goldhanger curates and the Rev. John Christopher Atkinson (1814–1900), rector of Danby, ornithologist and antiquary — a documented Atkinson clerical dynasty, logged as orbit, weighed at zero against the Kirkby Malham line until the registers are read. **THE HUDDERSFIELD ORBIT [B — the NZ *Atkinson Saga*]:** the Huddersfield family — **JOSEPH ATKINSON (1701–1772) of Bradley Mill** (fulling and corn mills, the wills quoted in full: *'I give to my said sons Michael and Richard Atkinson all my share… of the two Corn Mills called the Kings Mill… and Shorefoot Mill…'; the bequests to daughters Elizabeth Firth, Mary Tinker, Hannah and Sarah*), Kirkheaton church and the five Atkinson tablets (burned in the 1886 fire, re-cut in brass 1890), **Thomas Atkinson the friend of REV. HENRY VENN** (the *Life of Venn* letters), and the family's New Zealand branch (Frederick & Harriet Kenyon Atkinson, Waimate North, Aspley House) — orbit, documented, unconnected to our line [B]. **CHRISTOPHER ATKINSON — THE FULL DOSSIER (29–30 September dive) [A — Wikipedia; B — WikiTree; NA — the *Quaker Studies* research note, with his confession verbatim]:** b. KENDAL, WESTMORLAND, bapt. Aug. 21, 1631, son of Robert — one of the **VALIANT SIXTY**, the first Quaker missionaries; received aid from the poor-preachers' fund; at BOLLAND, Yorkshire, 1653, with Thomas Vears — *'received by James Bond, a poor man'* (the Bond family again); **SEPTEMBER 1654: he and two female Friends crossed to FRANCE — perhaps the FIRST Quaker mission to mainland Europe**; the Norwich mission with Whitehead, Hubberthorne, Lancaster, Simonds; then the fall: the affair with **Ursula**, servant of Thomas Simonds, in Norwich Gaol — his confession, in his own hand: *'Whereas from the truth I am departed through the deceitfulness of sin… The sin which I am guilty of, is that of having the carnall knowledge of a Woman… the vengeance of the Lord lyeth heavy upon me, which is my just reward'* — witnessed by John Stubbs, William Caton, Thomas Simonds; disowned by the Norwich Friends; **James Nayler** defended him against Thomas Welde's *'prophetical imposter'* pamphlet; the ESCAPE from Norwich Gaol, July 4, 1655 — *'pretence to go to the Post-house for a Letter'* — and the wanted poster: *'a little low man, somewhat round shouldered, with little or no hairs on his face… his Clothes of a greenish gray colour… about the age of thirty years… escaped out of the City Gaole in Norwich'*; **George Fox's verdict: 'that dirty man'**; and 'no clear information about his subsequent life.' The clan's founding generation, with the warts the file has carried since its first page: collected, not adopted. **SAMUEL ATKINSON (1685–1775) — parentage now resolved [B — WikiTree citing Hough]:** son of **THOMAS ATKINSON & JANE BOND** (his brothers Isaac & William 'born in Yorkshire'); his mother the minister, m. (2) William Biles. **THE CHESTERFIELD BRANCH'S FOUNDER, AT [S] (29–30 September dive) [S — his will, the obituary, and the Chesterfield MM minutes, via the jeffsgenealogy compilation citing Hough's PMHB article and the FHL film 20,382]:** **b. July 17, 1685, BRISTOL TOWNSHIP, BUCKS Co., PA** — d. Feb. 21, 1775, Chester Twp., Burlington Co.; the newspaper obituary, verbatim: *'On Tuesday morning, the 21st. ult, departed this life, in Burlington County, New-Jersey, SAMUEL ATKINSON, in the 90th year of his age, and on the Thursday following his remains were deposited in Friends burying-ground at Moores-Town'* (*Pennsylvania Gazette*, Mar. 1, 1775). m. **RUTH STACY (BEAKS)** at CHESTERFIELD MONTHLY MEETING, September 12, 1714 — the certificate from FALLS MM, the marriage at the house of Mahlon Stacy, the bride's brother [S — the meeting minutes]; the couple at William Beaks's Nottingham property, then 238 acres bought in Chester Twp., 1719, moving with certificate to Gloucester (Newton) MM — and *'the Chester Monthly Meeting [was] established, 1720 or 1721 — the advent of his family probably being the cause of establishing the new congregation.'* His will (1769, proved Apr. 13, 1775), in full: *'Son, Thomas, 5 shillings. Son, Samuel, negro man called Adam… Daughter, Rebeckah, negro boy, called Lott… Daughter, Ruth, negro boys, called Noah and Andrew… Whereas, Governor WILLIAM PENN (as I have been very well informed) did promise my deceased father, if he would take up 500 acres of land within his Province, he would give him a lot in Philadelphia with Liberty land, and my father did take up 500 acres… and he died soon after, and the Proprietor did lay out a lot, which I have seen in a map of the City, with my father's name thereon… I give the same to my 2 above said daughters. Executors — son-in-law, Joshua Bispham, and Thomas Say.'* [S — a will that names Governor Penn's promise to the father]. The children: Thomas, Samuel, Rebeckah, Ruth, + the Bispham marriage. **Also logged: the western branch [NA — Linda's pages]:** the FIVE BROTHERS ATKINSON — Charles, James, Mitchell, William, and Isaac — who settled at the mouth of Sunfish Creek (Monroe Co., OHIO) about 1800 (Cameron/Jamestown laid out by James, 1837); their father **CORNELIUS ATKINSON**, the Frontier Ranger, m. Mary (Stephens) Cross — the WESTWARD branch. **And the book, reprinted [NA — Higginson Books] — *The Atkinsons of New Jersey*, available in hardcover reprint.** **THE BOOK'S OWN TEXT — THE INTRODUCTION AND JAMES'S STORY, VERBATIM (29–30 September dive) [A — the 1890 book, archive.org]:** the introduction carries the compiler's confession of method: *'The celebration of the Golden Wedding of my father and mother, Geo. T. and Elizabeth B. Atkinson, of Mullica Hill… created a desire to know more of the family… I am indebted to John Clement, of Haddonfield… Chas. S. French, of Philadelphia… Many of the Atkinsons, of New Jersey, were staunch Quakers in the early day.'* James's story, in full: the landing at Dock Creek by the Blue Anchor Tavern, October 23, 1681 (old style 8th month — the book itself warns *'the eighth month under the old style meant October'*); the marriage at Hannah Newbie's house 1684 — *'this place is deserving of some notice, for here the first Friends' meeting in Gloucester county was held… Mark Newbie had his bank, probably the first institution of that character in America… The house stood… about two miles from Camden City, near the north branch of Newton Creek'*; James a representative to Salem Quarterly Meeting and the Yearly Meetings of 1689–93. **THE GLOUCESTER ROUTES, NAMED [A — the FamilySearch Gloucester Co. guide]:** the EGLINGTON CEMETERY records (Clarksboro), the Gloucester County Historical Society's vital-records indexes, the Surrogate's will index 1691–1900, and the death records — the repositories for Emily's family, one Woodbury visit each. **NEW CLAN NAMES FROM THE INDEXES (29–30 September):** **JOHN CROUCH ATKINSON of Clayton, Gloucester Co.** (father of Garfield, 1880–1882, with Mary Richards [A-Ancestry]); **KEMPER ATKINSON, b. ~1875 NJ, of WOODBURY** [NA-MyHeritage — the Woodbury name at Emily's generation, flagged]; and the Haines-book intermarriages: Samuel Atkinson m. ## 11. Open Seams — The Five Documents That Close This Case

Ranked by effort:

1. **WikiTree → "Edmund Hawks Sr."** (Petersham 1780 – Oswego 1850; manager Stu Wilson) — read the attached father. *One minute.* If the profile links Daniel & Rhoda (Perham), the Allerton line is established at tree level.
2. ***Vital Records of Petersham, Massachusetts, to 1849*** — Births → HAWKS: "Edmund, s. of ___ & ___, Oct. 9, 1780." *Free on archive.org; five minutes.* This is the single most valuable page in the whole investigation.
3. **The Hawkes family Bible** (at the user's parents') — the family-record page and signatures. One photograph may close items 1, 2, and the Hannah question simultaneously.
4. ***Genealogical and Memorial History of the State of New Jersey*, vol. 3 — the Carpenter entry** — names John Carpenter's father (Providence vs. Rehoboth/*Bevis*).
5. **Thomas Robinson's marriage record** (Quaker MM records, NJ/England) — the maiden name behind "Susannah Hannah" = the Sorbie link.

**Institutional shortcuts:**
- **General Society of Mayflower Descendants — free "Lineage Match" service**: submit the ancestor list (Mary Hawkes → James Dunton Carpenter Sr. → Jr. → William Atkinson → James Edmund I → user, plus the Daniel Hawkes chain). If any segment matches a verified application, that segment comes pre-proven. Allerton silver book: **MF17**; Brewster descendants are documented separately (Sarah Allerton's line).
- **Adam Hawkes Family Association** (mmais@sbcglobal.net): free research help; their files include decades of "Hawkes Talks" newsletters covering exactly these NY lines.
- **The Oswego Palladium, 1832–1851**: 818 issues browsable free — John Carpenter's own editorials, and possibly the 1848 wedding notice.

| Thomas Hawke of Essex, martyr 1555, as ancestor of the Hawkes line | **THE STORY CONFIRMED IN FULL [S]; THE BLOOD-LINK UNPROVEN — carried as the family's own printed tradition (the 1887 *Gleanings*: 'their blood mingled with that of the successors of Thomas Hawkes').** The man: Thomas Hawkes (b. 1531), a gentleman, a retainer of John de Vere, Earl of Oxford, at Earls Colne Priory, 'well esteemed and loved of all the household,' with his own house on Market Hill, Coggeshall, Essex (later 'Constantynes'). Arrested for refusing to have his infant son baptized 'after the popish manner'; condemned by Bishop Bonner, Feb. 9, 1555; **burned at Vicarage Field, West Street, Coggeshall, June 10, 1555** — his promised sign: the consumed body *'lifted its arms and clapped them over its head three times before sinking down into the flames'* (Foxe, *Book of Martyrs*). His fellow Coggeshall sufferers: Nicholas Chamberlain, Thomas Brodehill, Richard Web, William Bamford, John Wallet, Thomas Osborne, Thomas Osmond, and the women Cicley Warren and Christianna Pepper — the **Warren** name among them, logged as a curiosity only. **What is NOT found:** the martyr's parentage, and the fate of the unbaptized son — the blood-link the tradition claims is therefore unweighable, carried at the tradition's own grade. |

Jan. 27, 1776; births — HAWKS, Salley, d. James and Olive, Nov. 24, 1776; HAWKS, William, s. James and Olive, Oct. 3, 1778; marriage — Nancy Hawks and Sanford Babbit, June 15, 1789;** plus collateral entries (Abijah Hawks Jr. & Louis Frizel, Jan. 5, 1792; Edwin Hawkes of Templeton & Fanny Johnson, Apr. 22, 1840; Mrs. Ann C. Hawkes & Elias Ayres of Barre, Nov. 22, 1843). The 1780 birth stood on a **torn leaf** — the volume shows torn dates throughout ("17—[torn]" on the very HAWKS page) — or occurred at Hardwick, the mother's town. **ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 1: THE DANISH AND FRISIAN HOUSES, DOCUMENTED (28 Sept 2026) [S/A — FMG MedLands DENMARK; Wikipedia; the Brill *Redbad* chapter]:**

**THE DANISH HOUSE:** **HALFDAN, son of HARALD, King of Haithabu, and Imhild** — the FMG: Halfdan is shown in the *Europäische Stammtafeln* as King Harald's son, and Einhard's *Annales* (812) record *"Anulo nepos Herioldi quondam regis"* — 'Anulo, grandson of the former king Heriold' — *"which, assuming that 'nepos' should here be translated as grandson, indicates that this may be correct"* — the file carries the grandfather-hypothesis at exactly that flag. Halfdan's sons: Hemming (d. Walcheren 837), Anulo & Reginfrid (the 812 battle in which 10,940 died), **and HARALD 'KLAK' (c. 785 – d. 844×852) — King of the Danes 812–814 and 819–827, who was BAPTIZED AT MAINZ IN 826 with EMPEROR LOUIS THE PIOUS AS HIS GODFATHER, with over four hundred Danes, and was given the county of Rüstringen in Frisia as a refuge** (the Royal Annals, the *Vita Hludovici*, and the *Annales Xantenses* all record it — and St. Ansgar, the Apostle of the North, accompanied him home as missionary). Driven out by Horik I in 827, he died in exile — *"killed by them on nothing more substantial than the suspicion of potential disloyalty."* **The saga tradition (*Ragnars­sona þáttr*, *Heimskringla*) makes Klakk-Harald the father of THYRA 'DANNERROG' — wife of GORM THE OLD — which would bind every later Danish king to his blood; the FMG treats this as legend — flagged, not adopted.** *And the St. Matilda thread tightens further: Godfrid Haraldsson — the FMG lists him among Harald Klak's baptized sons — is the very Godefrid of Haithabu (d. 885) whose daughter the *Europäische Stammtafeln* guessed (and the FMG rejected) as St. Matilda's mother Reinhild: the Danish line now runs father-to-son into the family's own contested ground.*

**THE FRISIAN HOUSE:** **ALDGISL (Aldegisel) — THE FIRST FRISIAN MONARCH ATTESTED IN HISTORICAL SOURCES** (c. 650/77–679), who received Bishop Wilfrid of York at his court in 678/9 and let him preach — succeeded, per the sources' own hedge (*"Radbod, whom they say to be the son of king Aldgisl, but we have no knowledge of that"*), by his son **RADBAD (REDBAD), KING OF FRISIA c. 680–719 — the last independent Frisian king, who DEFEATED CHARLES MARTEL AT COLOGNE IN 716** and whose baptism-refusal before St. Wulfram — *"he would rather go to Hell and see his ancestors again than spend eternity with a paupers' few"* — became medieval Europe's most quoted story about the fate of the unbaptized. **His daughter THEUDESINDE married GRIMOALD THE YOUNGER, son of PEPIN OF HERSTAL, in 711 — the documented Frisian–Carolingian marriage** — and Grimoald's son Theudoald (b. c. 708) may be theirs, carrying Frisian royal blood into the succession. Radbad's death in 719 'was met with a sigh of relief throughout Frankish Europe.' The Frisian kings were chosen warlords rather than hereditary monarchs — the file notes the office as well as the blood. *And St. Matilda's mother Reinhild, 'born of Danish and Frisian stock' — the two houses this mission documented — remains the thread that ties the family to both thrones, at the grade the scholarship assigns.*

**ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 2: LOTHARINGIA AND IVREA, DOCUMENTED (28 Sept 2026) [S/A — Columbia Epistolae; Wikipedia; FMG; WikiTree]:**

**LOTHARINGIA — the divorce that scandalized Christendom:** **Lothair II of Lotharingia (c. 837 – 7 Aug. 869)** — forced to marry **Teutberga of the Bosonids** (daughter of Boso the Elder, sister of Hucbert), but long bound to **WALDRADA — *"a noble daughter of the Eticho clan"* (Columbia Epistolae) — the late *Vita Sancti Deicoli* making her kin to Eberhard II of Nordgau, of the ETICHONIDS — the family's own deepest house*** [flagged as a late source]. The divorce case — Teutberga's ordeal of the boiling water (the hand *"uncooked"*), the forced confession, Hincmar's *De divortio*, **Pope Nicholas I's defiance and Waldrada's excommunication (866)**, Lothair dying on the road back from Rome still trying to legitimize his son — consumed his reign. **The children, declared illegitimate: Hugh (c. 855–895), Duke of Alsace; GISELA (c. 860/865 – bef. 26 Oct. 907) — married 883 to GODFREY THE VIKING, Duke of Frisia, murdered 885 — the St. Matilda thread; Bertha (c. 863–925) m. Theobald of Arles (Teutberga's own nephew!) then Adalbert II of Tuscany — sons Hugh of Italy and Boso of Tuscany.**

**IVREA — the Anscarids:** founder **Anscar I, a petty Burgundian count**, who with his brother **Fulk the Venerable, Archbishop of Rheims**, tried to crown Guy III of Spoleto King of France (887) — failed, followed him to Italy with 500 Burgundians, and was created **Margrave of Ivrea** — his grandson **BERENGAR II (c. 900 – 966) — KING OF ITALY 950–961 — *"a scion of the Anscarid and UNRUOCHING dynasties,"* named for his maternal grandfather the Emperor Berengar I — the family's own kinsman** — m. Willa of Tuscany; deposed by Otto I, died in German captivity. **The Burgundian branch: Adalbert II's widow m. Otto-Henry, Duke of Burgundy — their son Otto William (982) became the first Count of Burgundy — the Free County** — Reginald I → William I → ... → **Beatrice m. FREDERICK BARBAROSSA** → the Hohenstaufen; **the Castilian branch: Raymond of Burgundy m. Queen Urraca — their son Alfonso VII — kings of Castile and León to the Trastámara.**

**ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 3: BLOIS, THE WELF DEEPER FLOOR, AND MAINE (28 Sept 2026):**

**BLOIS [A — FMG MedLands BLOIS, with charter cites]:** the documented floor is **THIBAUT, Viscount of Tours (d. 944; parentage unknown)** — his son **THIBAUT I "LE TRICHEUR," Count of Blois 944–977**, m. (943/4) **LUITGARDIS OF VERMANDOIS** — daughter of **Heribert II, Count of Vermandois, and Adela Capet**, and **widow of Guillaume I "Longuespee," Count of Normandy** (the one woman married to both a Norman duke and a Blois count) — down through **Eudes I → Eudes II (d. 996+, m. Bertha of Burgundy) → ODO II (983–1037, m. Ermengarde of Auvergne) → BERTHA OF BLOIS (d. 11/13 Apr. 1085) — m. ALAN III OF BRITTANY, the user's direct ancestress** (Chronicon Kemperlegiense: *"Alanus... married Bertham filiam Odonis Comitis Carnotensis"*), then Hugh IV of Maine; her son **CONAN II of Brittany** carries the line to Hawise Fergant. Collateral glory: the line's **Stephen-Henry of Blois (1046–1102) — the First Crusade count whose love-letters to Adela (daughter of William the Conqueror) are the crusade's most human documents**, shamed home from Antioch, dead at Ramla.

**THE WELF DEEPER FLOOR [S/A — FMG; the Annalista Saxo]:** **ROTHARD (CHROTHARD) OF METZ, d. 9 Dec. 771 — witness of Charlemagne's own charters — is the Welf floor**, deeper than Welf I: *"The linking of Rothard with the Welfs allows us to see in Welf, the father of Empress Judith, a descendant of Rothard"* — son, grandson, or nephew, the scholarship leaves open. **WELF I himself bore two names — *"de principibus Bawarorum qui fuit binomius, name et ETICHO et WELFUS dicebatur"*** (the Annalista Saxo) — the Eticho-Welf binomial, d. 824/25, m. **Heilwig, daughter of the Saxon count Isanbart**, abbess of Chelles — Thegan: *"filiam Hwelfi ducis sui, qui erat de nobilissima progenie Bawariorum."* Their sons **Conrad I the Elder — m. ADELAIDE OF TOURS, daughter of HUGH OF TOURS (the Etichonid) & Bava, and *"by some accounts his wife re-married to Robert the Strong"* after his death (864) — the Werner/Mistral alternative, now carried as a documented tradition** — and Rudolf I, whose grandsons became the **KINGS OF BURGUNDY: RUDOLF I (proclaimed at Saint-Maurice, Jan. 888), RUDOLF II (d. 937, united the kingdoms, m. Bertha of Swabia), CONRAD I "le Pacifique" (937–993), RUDOLF III (d. 1032, the kingdom to the Empire).**

**MAINE [S/A — FMG MedLands MAINE]:** **GAUZLIN I & ADELTRUDE** — named in their son's own charter: *"genitor meus Gauzlinus et mater mea Adeltrudis"* (1 Mar. 839) — parents of **RORGON I, FIRST COUNT OF MAINE of the Rorgonid dynasty (d. 16 June 839/40, buried Saint-Maur de Glanfeuil, which he founded 824), Count of Rennes 819, Count of Maine 832.** And the documented liaison: **RORGON'S MISTRESS WAS ROTRUDE OF FRANKS — CHARLEMAGNE'S ELDEST DAUGHTER** (b. 775 – d. 6 June 810) — their son **LOUIS (b. c. 800 – d. 9 Jan. 867), Abbot of Saint-Denis, Arch-Chancellor of Charles the Bald — CHARLEMAGNE'S GRANDSON through the Maine count**, the Annales Bertiniani naming him *"nepos Karoli imperatoris ex filia maiori natu Rohtrude."* **⚠ The flagged link that would crown the mission: a daughter of Rorgon & Rotrude — "perhaps named Adeltrude" — as the wife of GERARD I OF AUVERGNE and mother of RANULF I OF POITOU — the FMG carries it with its chronology caveat: *if* true, the Aquitaine dukes and every descendant of Adelaide of Aquitaine descend from CHARLEMAGNE'S DAUGHTER.** Rorgon's wife Bilichild and the five legitimate children (Rorgon II d. 866, Gauzfrid, Bishop Gauzlin d. 886, Bilichild m. Bernard of Poitou, and the possible Ranulf-mother) complete the house.

**ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 4: THE GRADED CELTIC FLOORS AND THE REGINARID STEM, VERDICTS IN (28 Sept 2026) [S/A — Wikipedia; FMG MedLands BRITTANY; WikiTree citing Cawley/FMG, the Henry Project, and Baldwin]:**
- **WELSH — Gwriad stands, his pedigree falls:** **GWRIAD (fl. ~770s), "probably a Manx chieftain" (the Dictionary of Welsh Biography), is anchored by the *CRUX GURIAT* — an 8th–9th-century cross ON THE ISLE OF MAN inscribed with his name** (Kermode, 1897; Lloyd: *"undoubtedly strengthens the case"*), and by the *Annales Cambriae* for 877 (*"Rotri et filius eius guriat a saxonibus iugulatur"* — Merfyn's son named for him). **But Stewart Baldwin's verdict is adopted: *"the genealogy which purports to make Gwriad a direct male-line descendant of Llywarch Hen is almost certainly a late fabrication"* — the Jesus College MS. 20 pedigree is 14th-century, 500 years late, with chronological flaws and Llywarch's own poetry testifying that his twenty-four sons predeceased him. The Welsh floor is Gwriad; the Coel Hen / Rheged claims are rejected.**
- **BRETON — Nominoë's parents are UNKNOWN:** the FMG MedLands and WikiTree state it plainly — *"Nominoe's parents are UNKNOWN"*; his wife unnamed; three children documented (Erispoë, murdered by his cousin Salomon at the altar 857; Pastheneten; Gurwent). **The "Erispoë I of Poher" father found on modern trees is speculation, not in the FMG — the Breton floor stands at Nominoë (d. 851), annal-documented, the dynasty's founder.**
- **SCOTTISH — Alpín is "supposed":** **Alpín mac Echdach is *"a supposed king of Dál Riata… included in a pedigree chart created in the 10th century to connect the kings of Alba to legendary Dál Riatan and Irish ancestors"* — his alleged father Eochaid "is not mentioned in any contemporary source."** The Scottish floor stands at **Kenneth MacAlpin (d. 858, annal-documented)** — his father beheaded by the Picts in 834, his mother a Pictish princess per the sources — with the Dál Riata king-list (Fergus Mór onward) rejected as the legendary layer it is.
- **REGINARID — the parentage is "not yet confirmed":** **"Reginar I, Comte de Hainaut is often considered to have been a son of Giselbert, Graaf van Maasgau, however HIS PARENTAGE HAS NOT YET BEEN CONFIRMED BY PRIMARY SOURCES"* — and the abduction-marriage of Lothair I's daughter (846, per Goldberg) carries "no conclusive evidence" for her name. The floor stands at **Reginar I Longneck (d. between 25 Aug. 915 and 19 Jan. 916 at Meerssen — the Annales Fuldenses' *Ragenerus Longicollus*) — the man himself documented, his parents probable, not proven.**

**ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 5: THE ANGEVIN, RORGONID, AND UNRUOCHING FLOORS, VERDICTS IN (28 Sept 2026) [S/A — FMG MedLands ANJOU; Wikipedia; the Henry Project]:**
- **ANGEVIN — INGELGER (d. [888], buried Châteauneuf, église Saint-Martin) is charter-documented by his own son: *Foulque I "le Roux" names "Ingelgerio genitore meo" in a charter dated [929/30]*** — the primary citation the FMG leads with. **His parents are the FMG's own dismissal: *"Tertullus is no more convincing than Tortulfus as the name of a historical figure in north-west France in the mid-9th century… it is probable that Tertullus was not a historical person."*** — the 12th-century Gesta Consulum and the Chronicon Turonensis (nepos of Hugh of Burgundy, "chronologically stretched") are the legendary layer; WikiTree: "Parents are most likely fictional." The 19th-century Petronilla-as-Charlemagne's-granddaughter claim was a confusion of Hugh the Abbot with Charlemagne's son Hugh — rejected. *Ingelger's own life is solid: granted half of Anjou by Louis II "le Bègue" [877/79], viscount of Orléans, married Adelais of the lords of Amboise (whose uncles were the archbishop of Tours and the bishop of Angers), a *miles optimus* — and through his descendant Geoffrey Plantagenet, ancestor of Henry II and the present Crown.*
- **RORGONID (Maine) — GAUZLIN I, the oldest known ancestor of the family, documented TWICE: named with Charlemagne in the Spanish March, 2 April 812, and by his son Rorgon's own charter of 1 March 839 (*"My father Gauzlin and my mother Adeltrudis, and my brother Gauzbert"*); he married Adeltrude, daughter of the Count of Bourges; on onomastic grounds "he could be a descendant of the 8th-century Duke of Maine, Charivius." The conjectured father — ROGER, the 8th-century count of Maine — carries the Henry Project's exact grade: *"The conjecture is plausible, but unproven."***
- **UNRUOCHING — the Four Hunrochs, resolved by the Henry Project:** **Hunroch #1 = Hunroch #2, ONE MAN — HUNROCH, COUNT OF TERNOIS, d. 13 November 844×852, buried Saint-Bertin, monk there at the end, father BOTH of EBERHARD OF FRIULI (Sedulius Scottus's *"Hunroci proles"*) AND of ADALARD, ABBOT OF SAINT-BERTIN** — *"a very strong case that Eberhard and Adalard were brothers"* (the testament's geography, the abbot-nephews, Eberhard's sons named Hunroch and Adalard). **The probable EARLIER kinsman — HUNROCH (fl. 802–814): guard of the Saxon hostages, imperial missus, negotiator of the 811 Danish peace, WITNESS TO THE TESTAMENT OF CHARLEMAGNE** — "probably distinct," given the 814–839 gap. **And Hunroch #4 (Huronicus), father of Bérenger of Toulouse, is an OLDER family member, NOT the count of Ternois** — Chaume's decisive objection: Bérenger died before his supposed father. **ALL of Chaume's further conjectures (father Bérenger d. 834, mother a Thurgovie, the Boulogne brother, the Blois sister) are carried at the Henry Project's "evidence poor" grade; the one reasonable conjecture — a daughter m. Suppo III, Margrave of Spoleto — rests on Emperor Berengar I's own 890 charter (*"Unroch consanguineus noster filius quondam Supponis incliti marcionis"*).**

**ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 6: GISELA OF SWABIA AND THE SALIAN STEM, DOCUMENTED (28 Sept 2026) [A/S — Wikipedia; Britannica; FMG MedLands SWABIA/FRANCONIA]:**
- **GISELA OF SWABIA (c. 990 – 15 Feb. 1043) — the empress who married three would-be kings:** daughter of **Herman II, Duke of Swabia (d. 4 May 1003), & Gerberga of Burgundy — daughter of King Conrad the Peaceful of the Welf-King line** — *"both her parents were descendants of Charlemagne."* Three marriages: **(1) ~1002, BRUN I OF BRUNSWICK — the user's Brunonid kinsman, the 1002 royal candidate, murdered by Milo of Ammensleben**; (2) ~1012, Ernest I of Swabia (the Babenberg, d. by an arrow while hunting); (3) before Jan. 1017, **CONRAD II — first Salian Emperor**. **Mother of Henry III.** *The kinship scandal preserved by Thietmar of Merseburg: Archbishop Aribo of Mainz REFUSED to crown her, **"the couple both descending from King Henry the Fowler"*** — crowned thirteen days late by Pilgrim of Cologne. The most active intercessor of the medieval chancery (155 of 191 diplomata), regent of Swabia 1015, regent of the Empire 1037; died of dysentery at Goslar 1043; buried in the Speyer grotto — her tomb opened in 1900: five-foot-eight, the long blond hair still there, and a strand of Conrad's beside it.
- **THE SALIAN LINE — Werner V (d. ~935), "the first documented Salian," Count in the Nahegau, Speyergau & Wormsgau** (deeper: the Widonid conjecture; his father Werner IV "Walaho" m. an unknown sister of King Conrad I — flagged) → **CONRAD THE RED (c. 922 – 10 Aug. 955), Duke of Lorraine 944–953, m. 947 LIUTGARDE OF SAXONY — daughter of Otto I and Edith of Wessex, Edward the Elder's granddaughter** — *killed at the Lechfeld, Widukind's account: "the foremost of all in combat, suffering from battle fatigue caused by an unusually hot sun, loosened the straps of his armor to catch his breath when an arrow pierced his throat"* — → **OTTO OF WORMS (948 – 4 Nov. 1004), Duke of Carinthia twice, "a grandson of Otto I," m. Judith, probably a granddaughter of Duke Arnulf of Bavaria** — sons **Henry of Speyer (m. Adelaide of Metz, of the Matfriding counts of Lorraine), BRUNO = POPE GREGORY V (996–999), Conrad I of Carinthia** — → **CONRAD II (c. 990 – 4 June 1039), elected King of the Romans 1024, crowned Emperor 1027** — raised by Bishop Burchard of Worms after his parents' early deaths — → **the SALIAN CENTURY: HENRY III (m. Agnes of Poitou — the Aquitaine empress), HENRY IV (1050–1106), HENRY V (1086–1125)** — four emperors, a century of rule, extinct 1125, the crown to Lothair III of Brunswick (the Brunonid inheritance) and then the Hohenstaufen. *The kinship braids run through it: the Salians descend from Otto I's daughter — the user's Ottonian kin by marriage; and the Salian emperors' wives were the user's Aquitaine, Swabia, and Saxony kin — the dynasty was woven from the same web the file has been tracing.*

**ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 7: THEOPHANU AND THE HOHENSTAUFEN, DOCUMENTED (28 Sept 2026) [S/A — Wikipedia; Wangerin (JSTOR); Britannica; the FMG SWABIA chapter; the Wibald pedigree]:**
- **THEOPHANU SKLERAINA (c. 955 – 15 June 991), Byzantine princess, Empress, regent of the Empire 983–991** — the user's Ottonian kin by marriage (wife of Otto II, the son of the Emperor Otto's line). **Her marriage certificate of 14 April 972 SURVIVES (Wolfenbüttel) — a masterpiece of Ottonian Renaissance art — naming her *neptis* (niece) of Emperor John I Tzimiskes**; current research (Wangerin) makes her the daughter of **Constantine Skleros (c. 920–989) and Sophia Phokas — the Armenian-Greek military aristocracy, kin to emperors Nikephoros II and Tzimiskes.** *She was not the virgo desiderata* — the Ottonians had wanted Anna Porphyrogenita — but Otto I kept her; **she introduced the fork to Western Europe** (*"a golden double prong"*); regent five years of *"considerable peace,"* issuing diplomas as *imperator augustus*, making peace with Mieszko of Poland; died at Nijmegen, buried at **St. Pantaleon, Cologne — the church of St. Bruno the Great** — with Thietmar's eulogy: *"Though of the weak sex she possessed moderation... protected with male vigilance the royal power for her son."* Mother of Otto III; her daughters ruled Quedlinburg, Gandersheim, and Lorraine.
- **THE HOHENSTAUFEN (Staufer):** origin *"remains unclear"* — Staufer counts appear in Otto III's 987 document as descendants of the **Riesgau counts near Nördlingen, kin to the Bavarian Sieghardinger** [flagged]. **The documented pedigree is the one ABBOT WIBALD OF STAVELOT drew up in 1153 at Emperor Frederick Barbarossa's own request: progenitor COUNT FREDERICK (d. ~1075), Swabian count palatine → his son FREDERICK OF BÜREN (c. 1020–1053), Count in the Riesgau, m. HILDEGARD OF EGISHEIM-DAGSBURG — NIECE OF POPE LEO IX — → FREDERICK I (c. 1050 – 6 Apr. 1105), made Duke of Swabia 1079, builder of Staufen Castle, m. AGNES OF WAIBLINGEN, daughter of Henry IV.** Then the seven emperors — **Conrad III, Frederick Barbarossa (m. Beatrice of Burgundy — the Ivrea line), Henry VI (m. Constance of Sicily), Philip of Swabia, Frederick II, Henry VII, Conrad IV** — the dynasty 1138–1254, ending with **Conradin, sixteen, beheaded at Naples in 1268**, the last male buried at Hohenstaufen itself. *Their marriages braid the whole web: the Salians (Agnes), the Welfs (Judith of Bavaria, daughter of Henry the Black), the Ivrea-Burgundy (Beatrice) — and through Barbarossa, the dynasty the Brunonid inheritance had prepared.*

**ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 8: THE HAUTEVILLES, THE CONRADINES, AND THE HUNFRIDINGS, DOCUMENTED (28 Sept 2026) [A/S — Wikipedia; the FMG's *Hauteville Ancestry* (Moriarty's revisions); FMG FRANCONIA; FMG SWABIA]:**
- **THE HAUTEVILLES — the twelve sons and too little land:** petty lords of **Hauteville-la-Guichard in the Cotentin** — the eponym Hiallt "probably just a legendary ancestor" (*Altavilla* = "high estate"). **TANCRED OF HAUTEVILLE (d. c. 1041)** — *twelve sons by his two wives, Muriella and Fressenda* — and the FMG's revision carries **de Vajay's identification of BOTH WIVES as illegitimate daughters of RICHARD II, Duke of Normandy (d. 1027)** — *if* accepted, the Hautevilles descend from the Norman dukes through the mothers. The sons: William "Iron Arm" (first Count of Apulia 1042), Drogo, Humphrey (*"we will be your faithful sons, but do not stand in our way"* — after capturing Pope Leo IX at Civitate), **ROBERT GUISCARD — m. first Alberada, whom Sanfelice makes a daughter of RENAUD I OF BURGUNDY (the Ivrea line — "both Guiscard and Alberada were descended from Richard II")** — and **ROGER I, Count of Sicily; Roger II the first King (Christmas 1130), patron of al-Idrisi's world map**. **CONSTANCE (1154–1198), the last Hauteville — imprisoned in a convent for thirty years over a birth-prophecy, married Henry VI 1186, captured at Salerno and imprisoned in the Castel dell'Ovo (released only by the Pope's threat to excommunicate Tancred), bore Frederick II before assembled witnesses the day after Henry crowned himself — Dante's *"great Constance"* (Paradiso III) — and through her the Hauteville blood passed to the Hohenstaufen.**
- **THE CONRADINES — the first German royal house:** **"The family is first mentioned in 832, with Count GEBHARD in the lower Lahn region"** — possibly a son of Odo I, Count of Orléans (flagged) — his descendants **Conrad the Elder (d. at Fritzlar 906, fighting the Babenbergs) and CONRAD I OF GERMANY (d. 918), elected King of the Romans 911 — the first non-Carolingian king**, who on his deathbed commended Henry of Saxony (the user's own ancestor's line) to the electors — extinct 1036.
- **THE HUNFRIDINGS — the executed duke and the two kings' wife:** **BURKHARD I (c. 850/855 – 5 or 23 Nov. 911), son of ADALBERT II "THE ILLUSTRIOUS," COUNT OF THURGAU** — Duke of Alamannia 909, the most powerful noble in the land, **executed for high treason before a tribal council** — his sons Burchard II (d. 926, killed in Italy supporting his son-in-law Rudolf, King of Burgundy) m. **Reginlind of Zürich** — and their daughters: **BERTA — m. (1) RUDOLF II, KING OF UPPER BURGUNDY (2) HUGH, KING OF ITALY (d. 947)** — and **HICHA (c. 905–950) — m. WERNER V, the Salian progenitor — "whose son was Conrad, Duke of Lorraine"** — *so the Salian imperial line itself runs through the Hunfriding duke's daughter.* The floor: Adalbert II of Thurgau, father of the executed duke.

- **EGISHEIM-DAGSBURG (Pope Leo IX's house):** **BRUNO = POPE ST. LEO IX (21 June 1002 – 19 Apr. 1054; pope 1049–54; canonized 1082)** — the great reformer, Hildebrand's patron — **youngest son of COUNT HUGH IV OF NORDGAU & HEDWIG OF DAGSBURG — and HUGH WAS A FIRST COUSIN OF CONRAD II, the Salian emperor** — the Egisheim family tied to the imperial web from birth. His niece **Hildegard m. Frederick of Büren — the Hohenstaufen progenitor** (Mission 7). The Nordgau counts descend from **Eberhard IV of Nordgau (d. 972) — the ETIHONID line, the family's own deepest house** — and the pope's pontificate ended in the act that named an age: **the Great Schism of 1054, his legates placing the bull of excommunication on the altar of Hagia Sophia while he lay dying.**
- **THE METZ COUNTS (Conrad II's mother's house):** the DOCUMENTED floor is the sibling group — **GERARD of Metz, ADALBERT of Metz, and ADELAIDE (d. 19 May 1040/46) — m. Henry of Speyer, mother of Emperor Conrad II** — and the controversy is logged honestly: **Hlawitschka's thesis (the Matfrids of the 9th c. + the Gerardides of the 10th c., probable kin to the GERARDIDES OF PARIS — the Beggo family) "meets with the most support," while the Etichonid link recedes** — with one intriguing flag: **Adelaide is carried in some sources as "of Egisheim, d'Alsace" — the ETIHONID Nordgau** — which would make Conrad II's mother an Etichonid. **ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 10: FLANDERS AND THE ARDENNES-VERDUN, DOCUMENTED (28 Sept 2026) [S/A — the Henry Project's definitive Baldwin page; the FMG FLANDERS; Wikipedia House of Ardenne–Verdun; the Henry Project's Ricuin page]:**
- **FLANDERS — the Audacer floor, the legend rejected:** **BALDWIN I "IRON-ARM" (d. 879), first count of Flanders — the *Annales Blandiniensis*: *"Baldwinus filius Audacri obiit."* His father AUDACER (ODACRE) — "it seems likely that the name can be accepted, although Audacer's supposed ancestors must be rejected"** — and the REAL floor is Audacer himself, **a count and *missus* OF CHARLEMAGNE: the *Annales Laurissenses* name *"Grahamannus et Audacrus"* as royal missi at the third Bavarian-Avar battle in 788, and a list of Saxons in Ostphalia, January/February 802, records *"Vulferi filium Sieri habuit Audracus comis."*** — **THE FLANDERS FLOOR IS A DOCUMENTED OFFICIAL OF CHARLEMAGNE'S, fl. 788–802.** The Lidéric-Ingelram "forester" genealogy — *"the alleged descent of Baldwin I from them must be discarded"* (though the two men were real) — rejected. And the kin-braids: **BALDWIN II OF FLANDERS MARRIED ÆLFTHRYTH, DAUGHTER OF ALFRED THE GREAT** (Witger: *"Ex qua genuit filium… Balduinum"*) — the Wessex blood in Flanders; Baldwin's daughter Guinidilda m. Wilfred the Hairy of Barcelona — the Flanders-Catalonia link. *The Flanders counts, through Matilda of Flanders, became the ancestors of the present British Crown.*
- **THE ARDENNES-VERDUN — a Carolingian foundation and a Crusader king:** **the dynasty descends from WIGERIC, COUNT PALATINE OF LOTHARINGIA, m. CUNIGUNDA OF FRANCE — GRANDDAUGHTER OF KING LOUIS THE STAMMERER** — their sons **Frederick I (Duke of Upper Lorraine), Adalbero (Bishop of Metz), GOZLIN (Count of Bidgau — the dynasty's ancestor), and SIEGFRIED — FIRST COUNT OF LUXEMBOURG 963, founder of the Elder House of Luxembourg — the present Grand Ducal dynasty.** Gozlin m. **Oda of Metz — daughter of Count Gerhard I of Metz and NIECE OF HENRY THE FOWLER** — their son **GODFREY "THE CAPTIVE" (d. 1002) — count of Bidgau 959, Verdun 963, Hainaut 974 — brother of ADALBERON, ARCHBISHOP OF REIMS, WHO CROWNED HUGH CAPET** — captured four times, released by Hugh Capet himself in 987 — m. 963 **MATILDA OF SAXONY, daughter of HERMANN BILLUNG, Duke of Saxony, widow of Baldwin III of Flanders** — their sons: the dukes of Lorraine, **Frederick = POPE STEPHEN IX**, and — through Godfrey the Hunchback. ODFREY OF BOUILLON, KING OF JERUSALEM, the Crusader — the last of the dynasty to hold the duchy** — *the Bouillon castle sold to finance the First Crusade, the dynasty's patrimony becoming the Crusader Kingdom of Jerusalem.* *The Verdun-count thread's middle generation — RICUIN (RICHWIN), Count of Verdun before 895–923, Duke (?) — was killed in his sickbed in 923 by Boso, son of Richard of Burgundy, per Flodoard — the Remiremont *Liber Memorialis* recording *"Riquinus dux obiit"* on 15 November.*

**ROYAL-LINES PROGRAM — MISSION 11: AUVERGNE AND THE BELLONID CATALANS, DOCUMENTED (28 Sept 2026):**
- **AUVERGNE — the son-in-law of a king:** **GERARD, COUNT OF AUVERGNE 839–841 — d. 25 June 841 at the Battle of Fontenoy, fighting for Lothair I** — and **JANET NELSON — the premier historian of the period — states: *"Gerard is known to have been son-in-law of PEPIN I, KING OF AQUITAINE, though the name of his wife has not been found."*** — the McKitterick version making his wife a granddaughter of Louis the Pious — i.e., the same daughter of Pepin I. **IF carried, RANULF I OF POITOU — the user's direct ancestor — was the GRANDSON OF A CAROLINGIAN KING.** *The flag kept: Settipani's chronology objection (a grandson of Pepin I born by 838 strains the documented countship of 835–839) — carried at the FMG's grade.* The Auvergne comital line itself runs documented: **Warin I (818–c. 820) → Warin II → Gerard → William I (841–846) → Bernard I (846–868) → Bernard II "Plantapilosa" (864–886) — son of BERNARD OF SEPTIMANIA and DHUODA, whose *Liber Manualis* (843) is the only surviving book by a Carolingian woman, written for her son** — → William I "the Pious" (duke of Aquitaine).
- **THE BELLONID CATALANS — the maker of hereditary Catalonia:** **BELLÓ, COUNT OF CARCASSONNE (d. 812, under Charlemagne)** — the documented floor — his son-in-law **SUNIFRED I OF BARCELONA (fl. 844–848)** — and his son **WILFRED THE HAIRY (d. 11 Aug. 897), Count of Barcelona 878 — *"the last count appointed by the French king and the first to pass his holdings to his sons"* — the man who made the counties hereditary and, in the doing, founded Catalonia.** *Corrections the sources themselves demanded: the *Gesta Comitum*'s claim that Wilfred's wife Guinidilda was a daughter of Baldwin I of Flanders — the Flanders-Catalonia link this file once repeated — is **flagged by Wikipedia as "considered unreliable about this matter,"** the contemporary charters naming a Sunifred as her father; and the legend of Wilfred of Arriaount — the murdered father, the four bloody fingers tracing the Catalan flag — is legend. The line runs forward through Wilfred II Borrell to the **House of Barcelona and the Crown of Aragon.** The Zürich counts (Reginlind's house) remain open — the only queued thread still unwoven.*

Werner V — the Salian progenitor), Burchard III (Duke of Swabia), Bertha (m. Rudolf II, King of Burgundy), Adalric (the holy monk of Einsiedeln)** — **AND SHE IS VENERATED: ST. REGELINDA OF ZÜRICH, FEAST 8 OR 16 AUGUST — the Salian progenitor's mother-in-law is a saint.** The countship passed to **LIUTO (d. after 16 May 960)**, her kinsman. *And the same FMG page carries the 903 charter of the ETIHONID counts — Luitfrid and his brother Hugh confirming their father Otpert's foundation, with "filiis meis Hunfrido (in Norgauwe), Luitfrido (in Sungawa), Hugone (in Eginsheim)" — the Etichonid sons Hunfrid of the Nordgau, Luitfrid of the Sundgau, and Hugh of Egisheim — the family's own deepest house, in the primary record, feeding the Egisheim popes.*
